#3036. Recap | Wizards defense vanishes in 139-132 loss to the Atlanta Hawks

Same opponent, different stadium, same result. After falling to the Atlanta Hawks Wednesday night in Washington, the Wizards traveled to Atlanta and got beat again, 139-132.

With Anthony Davis sidelined with a sprained ankle, the Wizards' defense largely collapsed. The Hawks got free runs at the rim, open threes, offensive rebounds, and largely had their way against Washington.

How bad was it? The Hawks had 105 points in the first three quarters and added another 34 in the fourth. Atlanta had 37 assists and 52 made field goals. Maybe it's because Venus is in retrograde.

Kyshawn George carried the Wizards offense with 18 fourth-quarter points. | NBAE via Getty Images

Trae Young played a solid offensive performance in his first game back in Atlanta since getting traded to Washington. His defense looked more like it did when he was with the Hawks, which is to say inattentive and ineffective. He's a wizard on the offensive end though — a dizzying mix of passing, shooting, crafty drives, and world-class foul-drawing.

The Wizards kept things close to the end with offense powered by Kyshawn George, who played another strong game. George finished 33 points, 4 rebounds, 7 assists. He carried the offense late, pouring in 18 points in a powerful fourth quarter.

Tre Johnson's shooting heated up. He finished with 23 points in 27 minutes off the bench, and he drew at least three, "He's not bashful" comments from Hawks analyst Dominique Wilkins. Wilkins and play-by-play man Bob Rathbun marveled at Johnson's aggressive shot selection.

Washington got as close as three points in the final minutes after trailing by as much as 14 in the second half, but didn't have enough to finish the comeback.

The Wizards fall to 4-5 on the season, and 1-1 in NBA Cup play.

Early defensive play will be a learning experience for rookie AJ Dybantsa. On the play, Atlanta's Onyeka Okongwu got free for a lob dunk. The reason he got free? Dybantsa didn't call out a screener, Deandre Ayton got picked, and Okongwu was wide open.

The first quarter included one of the worst defensive plays I've seen from Young this season, Kingston Flemings made what was going to be a routine cut to clear out the side. Young lost track of him, so it turned into an easy backdoor layup.

I've been frustrated by Bilal Coulibaly's lack of offensive aggression this season. In the first quarter, he attacked in transition and drew a foul. He made both free throws. This is the way.

Early in the game, I noted that it was the most active Alex Sarr has looked all season. His energy level seemed to dwindle later in the game, but he did finish with 13 points, 8 rebounds, 3 assists, 2 steals, and 2 blocks in 30 minutes. That's a solid game.

The Wizards started the game with Ayton in Davis' slot. In the second half, they tried Coulibaly instead of Ayton. It didn't seem to make a ton of difference either way.

Bub Carrington's defense is much improved this season. Example: late in the first quarter, he pressured CJ McCollum into a backcourt violation.

Dybantsa kept doing a good job of getting into the paint and to the rim…and then not finishing. I'm confident the finishing will come.

The Wizards kept setting ball screens for Young that would send him left and into the sideline. I'm not sure why they're doing that other than it's something Young likes. Which, I'm not sure why he'd want that — most teams want to keep the ball out of the middle and are perfectly happy to push it to the sideline. That's what ice coverage tries to do.

Back to Coulibaly for a second — his poor shooting is an issue. The Hawks declined to defend him at the three-point line. They were happy to let him shoot. He made one of three.

George carried the offense in the fourth quarter AND made a terrific defensive play — staying with McCollum through a heady sequence of moves before rising up and blocking the shot. The possession ended with Lu Dort hitting a fading two over Tre Johnson. I thought Coulibaly should have been in the game for defense on that play.

I really liked the simplicity and execution of Washington's out-of-timeout play with 13 seconds left in the game. George inbounded the ball to Sarr and immediately ran a classic "get action" (meaning he followed the ball to get it back from the big). He ended up with a wide open three, which he hit. Good design and execution.

#3034. As AI becomes more fractured, one name stands out: AMD

As Anthropic rushes to takes its shares public, the AI trade is showing cracks, and they are not confined to fringe players.

We recently wrote about CoreWeave, which fell 15.5% on Oct. 14 as yields on its 2032 notes touched 15%, and its default-swap spreads nearly doubled in October. IREN is down more than 20% this year. Last Thursday, the contagion reached chipmakers, which makes sense since much of CRWV and IREN's spending is going there. AMD dropped 8.1% as Meta's financing plans and a supply warning from Lam Research exacerbated data-center fears about the pace of AI spending.

Because when you screen for factors that demonstrably generate outperformance over time, the list narrows quickly. My screen: price above the 200-day moving average; revenue growing at least as fast as the S&P 500 (5%+ per annum); EPS growing at least as fast (7%+); rating upgrades; a PEG below 3; and ROE and ROIC of at least 12%. Of the names reporting in the next two weeks, AMD is effectively the only one that makes the list. Yes, you can (and arguably should, as our staples article described) position your portfolio with slightly less tech exposure than the broad indices provide, but we can't ignore the market's biggest and most transformative sector entirely.

Especially because the growth in this case doesn't just meet my criteria; it blows past them. Consensus for Q3 calls for EPS of $1.94 on revenue of $13.06 billion, versus $1.20 and $9.25 billion a year ago. That's YoY growth of nearly 62% and 41%, respectively. The recent drawdown came from a sector selloff, not anything AMD reported, but it shows that if some names in the space get hit, even the larger players are likely to feel it. On Oct. 6, Lisa Su said AMD would substantially increase supply in 2027 because AI orders keep outrunning capacity.

So how do you balance factors we know work for a fast-growing company against a backdrop of cracks in the AI trade? Options.

Owning the stock outright means absorbing a roughly 9% to 11% implied earnings-related move (day of). Earnings are about a week out, and over the past four quarters the smallest move over a similar window was more than 9%, and the largest move, after the company reported its Q1 26 results, was 38%! Of course, that kind of volatility means options aren't cheap, and that rich premium is also a reason not to simply buy calls.

A call spread risk reversal does both jobs: it reduces risk if the cracks widen, and the short options offset the elevated volatility we would otherwise pay for. The bottom chart shows that, by sacrificing about $1,700 of upside for $5,000 in outperformance during a severe downside move, and given how jittery this trade has been lately, this is a way to stay in it with considerably less risk. $5,000 less, specifically. Of course, selling that out will tie up some cash, but less so than simply buying the stock. And selling both the out and the call reduces long call purchase by 66%

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#3023. FIFA reduce Molina ban after Argentina brawl during 2026 World Cup final

Roma full-back Nahuel Molina has seen his international ban reduced by FIFA after an appeal from the Argentine Federation following the post-match brawl that broke out at the end of the 2026 World Cup final.

Molina had originally been handed a seven-game ban and his teammate, former Roma and Juventus midfielder Leoandro Paredes was given a 10-match ban for his involvement in the World Cup final brawl in the summer. Molina was also handed a fine of $77,000 and his teammate $90,000.

Following an appeal from the AFA, Molina's ban has been reduced from seven matches to four and his fine reduced from $77,000 to $60,000. Paredes's 10-match ban and $90,000 has been upheld.

EAST RUTHERFORD, NEW JERSEY – JULY 19: Nahuel Moiina #26 of Argentina and Rodri #16 of Spain square off after the FIFA World Cup 2026 Final match between Spain and Argentina at New York New Jersey Stadium on July 19, 2026 in East Rutherford, New Jersey. (Photo by Kevin C. Cox/Getty Images)

Molina has already served three games of his suspension: Argentina having beaten Bolivia, Burkina Faso and Benin in a series of friendlies in October since the World Cup final in July.

He was not part of the Argentina national team squad that was announced earlier this week ahead of a pair of international friendlies against Uruguay and Costa Rica coming up in November.

PHILADELPHIA, PENNSYLVANIA – MARCH 22: Lionel Scaloni Head Coach of Argentina at the start of the International Friendly match against El Salvador at Lincoln Financial Field on March 22, 2024 in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania. (Photo by Ira L. Black/Getty Images)

Seven other Serie A players were, including three of Molina's Roma teammates in Leonardo Balerdi, Matias Soule and Santiago Castro, plus Como pair Nico Paz and Maxi Perrone, as well as Parma's Mariano Troilo and Fiorentina's Franco Mastantuono.

#3018. Ripple strikes deal with Robinhood to expand its RLUSD digital dollar

Ripple has struck a deal with Robinhood to expand the reach of its digital dollar, RLUSD, strengthening trading and financing links between their institutional businesses and improving Robinhood's internal money management.

The partnership involves using RLUSD to settle institutional trades, automate Robinhood's treasury operations and expand access to digital asset trading and financing through Ripple Prime and Bitstamp, the global crypto exchange acquired by Robinhood last year.

It also helps meet Robinhood's ambition of becoming a global financial superapp, centralizing its customers' financial lives while expanding the underlying infrastructure that can serve both retail and institutional clients.

"Utility is what moves stablecoins forward. Working with Ripple, Bitstamp and Robinhood will have access to RLUSD for institutional settlement, Ripple Treasury for our operations and Ripple Prime for financing," Robinhood Crypto General Manager Johann Kerbrat said in a statement shared with CNBC. "That is what a connected, global financial system looks like."

Stablecoins have long been a workhorse of the crypto world, giving traders a way to move money among exchanges, buy and sell crypto assets, and park funds between trades without having to convert back into traditional currency. But as the crypto industry evolves beyond trading and markets move toward 24/7 liquidity, stablecoins are becoming essential financial infrastructure because of their ability to enable near-instant settlement and move dollar-denominated value at any time, regardless of traditional banking hours.

As firms look to do more with stablecoins beyond simply listing them on exchanges, stablecoin issuers are betting they'll favor a one-stop-shop approach that bundles the technology and services needed to move money, settle trades and manage cash.

"Stablecoins have evolved from a crypto trading tool into a critical piece of financial infrastructure," Ripple President Monica Long told CNBC. "The clearest evidence of this is where they're now being embedded — from corporate payments to capital markets. Our partnership with Robinhood reflects that evolution."

"This is about much more than trading," she added. "It's about fundamentally changing how money moves and is managed — giving institutions the ability to operate 24/7/365, unlocking liquidity and putting capital to work more efficiently."

Circle Internet Group, a Ripple competitor, is positioning itself for a similar future, where winning platforms, in addition to issuing stablecoins, also provide the infrastructure to put them to work. Earlier this year, for example, it launched the Circle Payments Network, connecting financial institutions and payment providers using its flagship USDC stablecoin and other digital assets for cross-border payments.

The network is Circle's answer to the 8-year-old Ripple Payments business, a service that helps businesses move money across borders, using any of several assets moving through that system, including USDC, Ripple-created XRP and, as of its debut in December 2024, RLUSD.

RLUSD's market cap has grown to more than $2.5 billion. Tether's USDT and Circle's USDC still dominate the total stablecoin market, which now exceeds $300 billion.

RLUSD will also be listed on Robinhood's trading platform, and is currently available to traders on Bitstamp as well as Robinhood Chain, the company's home-grown blockchain for tokenized stocks and other assets.

Robinhood, which reports third-quarter results for the three months ended in September after the market closes, rose about 2.5% in early trading Tuesday, snapping a three-day decline.

Correction: A previous headline misspelled RLUSD.

#3014. Sixers come out flat as Embiid, LeBron struggle in blowout loss to Cavs

Unable to pull a rabbit out of the hat this time, the Sixers were crushed 119-95 by the Cleveland Cavaliers Friday Night. They are 1-1 in NBA Cup action with a -19 point differential.

Joel Embiid had one of his worst games maybe ever, finishing with six points on 0-of-9 from the floor and seven rebounds. LeBron James had his worst outing as a Sixer, putting up eight points, shooting 3-of-12 from the field along with seven rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

Tyrese Maxey struggled for the second consecutive game, shooting 5-of-15 from the floor to put up 17 points and four assists. VJ Edgecombe kept the Sixers in as long as he could, going for 19 points on 8-of-13 shooting along with three rebounds and two assists.

Jaylen Brown was as much help as Edgedcombe had, scoring a team-high 20 points, shooting 9-of-19 from the field with five rebounds and five assists.

The Sixers were without Adem Bona while Cleveland was down Jarrett Allen.

Edgecombe opened the game creating a transition dunk with his speed. From there it was all Cavaliers for the first six minutes of the game. LeBron turned the ball over twice, both of which turned into transition baskets. The Sixers started the game 1-of-7 seven from the field — and committed — four turnovers, with only one of those attempts coming in the paint after Edgecombe's dunk.

LeBron finally backed his way into the post for a layup that ended a 15-2 run to start the game, but on the other end they were having a hard time containing James Harden's pick-and-roll. They gave up four offensive rebounds they were lucky only turned into two points.

After an 0-of-5 start that despite some declarations, did include a three-pointer, Embiid was finally able to draw a foul on Evan Mobley and get to the line. That didn't do the trick as he missed his next two attempts, both from the paint.

A pair of threes from Maxey and Anfernee Simons gave the Sixers the faintest hint of a pulse. Simons was able to draw a foul attempting another three, and Maxey got into the paint and get a jumper to go through contact for an and-1. Mitchell had gotten Cleveland's offense up and running since returning into the game and responded with a couple of baskets. Jabari Walker, the first backup five of the night for the Sixers, grabbed the team's first offensive rebound of the night, earning a pair of free throws that cut the Cavs' lead to 11.

This quarter also started with good stuff from Edgecombe, nailing a three off the catch and a midrange jumper off the dribble. Brown found KCP for another much needed three, but the Cavs made their first five field goals to start the second to keep extending their lead.

Brown also found a groove, as he and Edgecombe brought the Sixers back into the game with an 11-2 run fueled by a fury of midrange pull-ups. Walker, who had a solid shift, capped off the run with a good contest on a Harden three that fell short. He ran quickly out of that contest and Brown rewarded him with a full court pass for the layup.

KCP got called for a reckless closeout on a Harden three that threw cold water on Philly's momentum momentarily as LeBron got stripped for his fourth turnover of the night on the ensuing possession. Embiid looked pretty bottled up on his next post-up, but he was able to swing it out to Maxey who found Edgecombe for a no-dip three to beat the shot clock. KCP got a whistle back by drawing a transition take foul.

Embiid was able to get Harden to foul him on a jumper, but he still wasn't generating shots that looked comfortable. Brown tried to help get him going by feeding him in transition, but Embiid passed it to Maxey who was not expecting the ball, leading to another awkward missed fast break opportunity. The possession was nearly salvaged with an open three for Maxey, but Dean Wade got called for an illegal screen. Harden drilled a patented stepback three to put the Cavs up eight at the half.

The Sixers' offense still looked awkward in their attempts to get Embiid going coming out of the half. LeBron also missed his first two shots of the third, but they put together enough possessions to once again cut the lead to three. Maxey made a layup in transition, drilled a three, and threw a bounce pass down the length of the court to Edgecombe after stripping the ball away from Harden.

LeBron finally drilled a midrange jumper, but every time the Sixers got within a score, they fell right back down in a hole. Brown turned the ball over in traffic and then the team took a shot clock violation. Embiid threw a behind-the-back pass into way too much traffic and LeBron threw another pass right into a Cavaliers' chest.

Embiid's struggles were really felt on both ends as crasher after crasher was able to grab an offensive rebound over him. It probably should have been more, but the eight second-chance points the Cavs had scored on their 12 offensive rebounds hurt quite a bit. He also missed his 18th straight three-point attempt as the offense looked bottled up.

Things continued to spiral towards the end of the quarter despite the Sixers shuffling in the bench lineup. Simons had a particularly brutal stretch where his inbound pass got intercepted and he fouled Peyton Watson on the transition layup. Watson missed the free throws, but got his own miss for another offensive rebound and found Mitchell for a three, extending the Cavs' lead back up to 17.

It was frustrating to see the Cavs having seven missed free throws three minutes into the fourth. They turned the ball over twice in the opening minutes of the quarter. The Cavs gave them plenty of chances to take the game but they didn't take them. Offensively they had an ugly start to the quarter missing a barrage of pull-ups off the dribble.

The best part of every Cup game that becomes a blowout, especially the ones on national television, is the announcers reminding you that it still matters because of the point differential. Despite Stan Van Gundy's enthusiasm, the Sixers couldn't do anything to whittle down Cleveland's lead.

For as much as the announcers need to promote the rules of the Cup, the Sixers didn't need to have LeBron, Maxey and Edgecombe all out there as they continued to fall down more than 20. The tread on the tires, let alone anything happening, is not worth the risk. It's not like they were really helping the point differential anyway. It would still be garbage time but it would also be another chance to get Labaron Philon Jr. extended minutes.

Philon would eventually relieve Maxey with four minutes left, and LeBron's night was done just a few seconds later. Tacko Fall made his Sixers' debut playing the final 3:21 minutes and was credited with one rebound that probably should have gone to Walker and fouled Thomas Bryant on a three-point attempt.

He projects to be a potential key piece to the Colts defensive core if he can continue to improve and develop after such an encouraging debut start last weekend.

That being said, he'll be handling an important job duty, and one that was already the center of an unnecessarily self-inflicted controversy to start the Colts' disappointing 2026 season—as veteran safety Cam Bynum initially handled green dot duties in the Colts Week 1 loss to the Baltimore Ravens.

There was a number of breakdowns and Colts defensive players being out of position, as Bynum was forced to run from down the field and the backend of the Indy secondary to relay the defensive play-calling to his teammates, which didn't provide them as much opportunity to communicate and get set pre-snap. At that time, the Colts were the only team not to use a linebacker for handling green dot duties, and it cost them in Week 1.

Here's hoping Allen's green dot debut goes a lot smoother!

Pierre Gasly ended the Friday practice day at Interlagos in P14. (Source: MEGA)

Pierre Gasly aims for improvement in qualifying at the Brazilian Grand Prix. (Source: MEGA)

The Frenchman replicated the same pace, finishing third in a dramatic, rain-affected Sao Paulo GP alongside teammate Esteban Ocon, securing a double podium for Alpine in 2024.

The second day's action in Brazil resumes with FP3 at 11:30 AM local time, followed by qualifying at 3 PM local time.

#3001. Quenton Nelson undergoing knee procedure one week after blockbuster trade to Seahawks

Quenton Nelson (53) of the Seattle Seahawks warms up before a game against the Chicago Bears at Lumen Field in Seattle on Nov. 2, 2026. Getty Images

"There's a timeline right now that warrants not IR, so that's what we're going to do," Macdonald added.

The eight-time Pro Bowler has played in just one game for the Seahawks since his arrival and will also have to undergo a minor procedure on his knee.

According to NFL Network's Ian Rapoport, Nelson will have surgery to clean out some cartilage in his knee.

Macdonald added that Nelson was dealing with knee problems upon arriving from Indianapolis, with the symptoms worsening in the days since.

"We re-imaged it, and it was aggravated to some degree," Macdonald said. "So we've kind of done the due diligence, and everybody agrees it's best to take care of it now and play the long game."

The Colts sent the New Jersey native to Seattle in exchange for a 2027 third-round pick and a 2028 fifth-round pick during the team's fire sale following their rough start to the 2026 season.

Seattle Seahawks' Quenton Nelson (53) looks to block during an NFL game against the Chicago Bears at Lumen Field in Seattle on Nov. 2, 2026. Getty Images

#2998. AI debt fears ease ahead of Oracle, Alphabet and Amazon updates. Here's what to watch.

Fears over artificial intelligence debt have eased after a bout of volatility. But more tests for AI debt are coming.

Oracle holds its Investor Day on Wednesday. And hyperscalers Alphabet and Amazon.com are scheduled to report quarterly results after the market close on Wednesday and Thursday, respectively.

Credit markets are on edge over a flood of AI debt, though fears have eased up a little lately. Meta Platforms' plans for a $95 billion stock offering, announced on Monday, helped bring down its credit-default swap spreads. Those spreads had risen last week when the company reported negative free cash flow in the third quarter. Other CDS spreads had moved higher too, though they have since backed off.

Investors had expected Meta to bring a large bond deal to market shortly after its earnings announcement, said Richard Cheng, head of Nuveen's investment-grade corporate sector team. The fact that it wasn't announced came as a relief.

"Just the absence of the bond deal itself is a direct catalyst for AI-related spread tightening," he said.

A credit spread is the difference in the yield between the corporate bonds and Treasurys of similar maturities. When spreads tighten, it means investors are becoming more confident about the bond issuer.

The market action over the last few days shows that investors are recognizing that "credit fears may have been overblown," said CNBC contributor Paul Meeks, head of technology research at Freedom Capital Markets.

"I expect that these better feelings, maybe not good but better, may continue as companies announce their quarters over the next few weeks."

Still, spreads on CDS remain wider over the past year due to a surge of debt issuance and free cash flows turning negative across the AI space. Meta's CDS spread, for instance, is still up 177% year to date, while Amazon's spread has gained 129%.

"It's not necessarily fundamentals," said CreditSights analyst Jordan Chalfin. "It's more, I'd say, 80% driven by technicals and sentiment."

Alphabet and Amazon pose the next big hurdle for tech bonds as they update their capex plans and free cash flow forecasts. Microsoft's earnings are also due Wednesday, but the company has yet to issue any bonds to pay for its AI spending.

Both Amazon and Alphabet reported negative free cash flow in the second quarter and analysts expect that trend to continue.

Analysts expect Alphabet to report free cash flow of -$2.3 billion while spending $55.2 billion on capital expenditures in the third quarter, according to FactSet consensus estimates. Amazon is expected to report -$9.9 billion in free cash flow against capex of $58.7 billion.

S&P Global Ratings estimated in an August report that the hyperscalers will have negative free operating cash flow in 2026 and 2027.

More critical will be their outlooks, Cheng says. "The singular focus on the investors' minds is really what the capex guidance trajectory looks like across the hyperscaler space."

Meeks believes elevated capex is a sign of strong AI demand. The hyperscalers have "decided that with all the evidence they have — and they have more than you and me — that this is worthwhile spending, and not just worthwhile; it's required," he said.

CreditSights' Chalfin doesn't expect the hyperscalers to announce major new bond issuance around their earnings reports. Oracle's updates may be more critical for credit markets. The company has issued $117 billion in long-term debt and is clinging to an investment-grade credit rating of BBB-, the lowest level of investment grade above junk, according to S&P.

"It's the most important IG tech credit, just given how it's a bit more stressed relative to the A-rated names," he said. "It's trading more like a high-yield credit, even though it's IG, and so there's just a lot of investor concern on that one."

One wrinkle to the AI debt story: balance sheets don't reveal the whole picture. The companies are using complicated off-balance sheet arrangements for data centers and chip financing, and they aren't fully transparent, S&P Global said in a September report.

"There are commitments they are entering into, for example, into data center leases or purchase commitments on chips that aren't necessarily treated as debt," Mark Habib, digital infrastructure sector lead at S&P Global Ratings, said on CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" Monday.

S&P is trying to provide the market with more visibility on those commitments "to give a fuller picture of what kinds of obligations they're actually entering into," he added.

Oracle aside, hyperscaler credit ratings are all comfortably above A levels — reflecting the fact that they have broadly diversified revenue streams, ample coverage for interest payments, and capacity to add leverage if they choose.

Nuveen's Cheng isn't concerned, pointing out that while there has been some credit repricing, there is no credit crisis.

"They continue to have exceptional credit profiles, strong cash generation liquidity," he said of the largest hyperscalers.

"The market is really not concerned about credit quality at this point," Cheng added. "It's really just the trajectory of the capex needs and the supply of debt to fund that. Both of those are pointing in one direction — and that's up."

Correction: This story has been revised to reflect that Oracle's investment-grade credit rating is BBB-, according to S&P. A previous version mischaracterized the company's credit rating.

#2997. Manchester United challenging Juventus for Serie A star

Marco Carnesecchi has emerged as one of the most highly regarded Italian goalkeepers, attracting interest from several major European clubs. Juventus have followed his progress for years and remain keen to bring him to Turin as they look to find a permanent solution in goal.

The Bianconeri have monitored Carnesecchi since his academy days at Atalanta and continued tracking him during his loan spell at Cremonese. That experience helped him develop into an accomplished goalkeeper, and he has since established himself as Atalanta's first choice.

Juventus view him as a quality option for the future and have continued to follow his situation closely in recent weeks. The position is one they intend to address ahead of next season, with the club keen to secure a reliable long-term solution.

The Turin side have already made several changes to their goalkeeping department. They signed two goalkeepers on loan during the last transfer window and also brought in Neto on a short-term deal as a free agent after Kamil Grabara suffered a season-ending injury.

Despite those moves, Carnesecchi remains firmly on their radar. His performances for Atalanta have kept him among the players Juventus would like to sign, but they may now have to compete with another major club.

According to Tuttomercatoweb, Manchester United are also interested in securing the Italian's signature and could even attempt to sign him during the January transfer window.

(Photo by Francesco Scaccianoce/Getty Images)

The Premier League club reportedly regard Carnesecchi as one of the best goalkeepers in world football and is determined to strengthen their options in that position. Their willingness to consider an early move could complicate Juventus' plans.

United's interest means the Bianconeri may face serious competition if they decide to pursue the Atalanta goalkeeper. A January approach would also give the English side an opportunity to move ahead of their rivals before the summer transfer window.

Juventus have admired Carnesecchi for a long time, but that interest alone may not be enough to secure his services. With Manchester United also keen, the race for his signature could become an important transfer battle in the coming months.

#2987. Doc Rivers explains lead-up to Giannis Antetokounmpo’ Bucks exit: ‘Didn’t want to be the bad guy’

Doc Rivers didn't hold back when discussing Giannis Antetokounmpo.

On an episode of "The Bill Simmons Podcast" this week, Rivers divulged how Antetokounmpo handled his final season with the Bucks before an offseason trade to the Heat, saying the 10-time All-Star wanted to leave without formally requesting a trade.

"Giannis wanted out, but he was a Buck. And he didn't want to be the bad guy," Rivers said. "He wanted [general manager Jon Horst and owner Wes Edens] and those guys to say, 'We're trading you,' instead of him asking for a trade. To me, that was the game that was being played."

Antetokounmpo was traded to Miami during the offseason in a blockbuster deal that sent Tyler Herro, Kel'el Ware, Jaime Jaquez Jr., Kasparas Jakucionis and draft capital to Milwaukee, including multiple first-round picks.

Rivers also explained why he found it "not easy" to coach Antetokounmpo, whom he was with on the Bucks from 2024 until Rivers resigned earlier this year.

"He's not easy. He plays at this crazy downhill pace. He's really a difficult guy," Rivers said candidly to Simmons. "He has this great passing ability, but he doesn't have a super-high IQ at the same time. He really has only one way of playing. Every play, he wants to force it. I wish he'd learned how to execute better."

Giannis Antetokounmpo and the Miami Heat are just 3-6 to begin the 2025-26 season. NBAE via Getty Images

Jason Kidd — who led Antetokounmpo and the Bucks from July 2014 until January 2018 — discussed Rivers' comments on ESPN earlier Friday, and didn't agree.

"Giannis' superpower to get downhill and he makes plays," Kidd said. "Understanding Doc saying that his IQ, I disagree with that. I think Giannis understands how to play the game. His forcefulness getting downhill is what it's all about. And so when he causes a problem, he's one of the top five players in the league"

The Heat have struggled to a 3-6 start despite Antetokounmpo averaging 27.3 points, 12.7 rebounds and 5.6 assists, although the team won three of four prior to losing to the Warriors on Thursday night.

Giannis Antetokounmpo and Doc Rivers were together with the Milwaukee Bucks from Jan. 2024 through April 2026. Getty Images

Head coach Erik Spoelstra is still working to integrate Antetokounmpo alongside Bam Adebayo following an offseason that significantly reshaped Miami's roster, although they are arguably the most disappointing team in the NBA this season.

Through the first nine games, Miami is second in pace but has just the 19th-ranked offense to show for it, and their pace-for-pace's-sake style of play has contributed to a surprisingly inefficient defense so far, where they rank No. 26 in the NBA.

The Heat return on Sunday against the lowly Kings.

#2986. AI debt concerns grow as ING reportedly seeks buyers for $500 million of data center loans

ING is reportedly seeking buyers for a portfolio of 12 data center loans worth around $500 million, joining a broader push by banks to distribute credit risk tied to the artificial intelligence infrastructure build-out.

The auction is said to include a $25 million piece linked to the Stargate project in New Mexico, which involves OpenAI, SoftBank and Oracle, as well as certain other loan assets involving Vantage Data Centers and CyrusOne, Bloomberg reported Friday, citing people familiar with the matter.

ING will hold a bids-wanted-in-competition, or BWIC, auction process, with bids sized at $10 million or more due by Nov. 19, according to Bloomberg. A BWIC is a competitive process for investors to bid on pieces of a loan portfolio.

The sale highlights a growing market for banks to move data center loans to private credit firms, asset managers and insurers as financing for the AI boom expands.

Investment banks typically use a lend-to-distribute model for these types of loans, said Hank Calenti, global markets strategist at SMBC.

"The buyers can range from private credit investors through asset managers to insurers. They would buy these assets for their expected higher returns," he said.

The ING sale comes as fears grow over soaring debt tied to the AI build-out — and potentially disappointing returns among investors.

Those concerns have accelerated following the Democrats' sweeping win in the U.S. midterms, which could see moratoriums or tighter restrictions on data center construction in certain states.

The perceived risk of a construction slowdown sent AI-related stocks into free fall on Thursday, with the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite Index closing the session down 5.5% — its worst day since April 2025 — and into correction territory.

"Whether banks sell more based on AI data centers moratorium fears should be driven by the cost (discount received) versus the benefit of exiting to free up cash for further lending, where the cost includes explaining the exposure to your board," Calenti told CNBC via email.

"This could be business as usual, or financiers looking to exit before the door shuts."

Pruksa Iamthongthong, head of APAC equities at Aberdeen Investments, said the Democrats' gains have increased concerns around greater scrutiny of data center development, at a time when investors were already questioning the financing and returns behind the scale of AI capital spending.

"We think near-term volatility could continue as the market assesses potential delays to U.S. data center construction and constraints around compute capacity."

Earlier this year, Wall Street lenders including JPMorgan Chase and Morgan Stanley were said to be exploring a variety of approaches to reduce their lending exposures to data center construction.

According to a Financial Times report on May 3, these include offloading portions of debt via private sales to non-bank lenders, as well as through so-called significant risk transfers, which transfer part of a bank's credit risk to investors to free up balance sheet capacity.

#2984. 3 musings after Dallas pummels Utah, 112-100

The Dallas Mavericks bounced back with a home win over the Utah Jazz, 112-100. Cooper Flagg led an all-around team performance with 21 points, 10 rebounds, and seven assists to go along with three steals and a block. Lauri Markkanen scored 24 for the Jazz in defeat.

The Mavericks looked spry early, pushing the Jazz and jumping to an 8-3 lead. Flagg's activity in particular stood out; during one sequence, he saved the ball out of bounces on the Jazz side of the court, throwing it to Kyrie Irving, only to sprint the floor and put Irving's missed three through the basket on a tip in. The Jazz rallied back to tie the game at 15 just before the six minute mark, forcing a Dallas timeout. The offensive pace slowed way down the rest of the frame, with a Jazz hot shooting start cooling off some. Markkanen rained from downtown, taking eight threes and making four, but also picked up two fouls in his minutes. Dallas found themselves behind 28-26 after one quarter of play.

The Dallas defense struggled in the second quarter; with the bench unit in action, the Mavericks gave up basket after basket and Utah built an eight point lead. Dribble penetration kills Dallas this season, with no player seemingly able to stay in front of their man for more than a dribble. Santi Aldama got his first minutes in a Dallas uniform and immediately scored on the offensive glass. The Mavericks slowly worked themselves back into the game. PJ Washington kept a scorching offensive start going through the second, hitting his fifth three-pointer of the half and scoring 22 to pace Dallas. The Jazz kept pace, and the game entered halftime tied, 57-57

Quarter three was outstanding for the Mavericks. Cooper Flagg found his rhythm early and never let up, and Morez Johnson Jr. showed his pedigree in a big way, playing stellar defense, rebounding, and helping Dallas take control of the game. It may well have been his best stretch as a professional. The offensive rebounding and second-chance points proved pivotal to Dallas, and they ended up taking an 89-76 lead into the final frame.

While the Jazz did attempt to mount a run in the fourth, at one point going on a 10-2 run, the Dallas Mavericks decided to close the door early. Dallas kept the pressure on, pushing after misses and makes and held off the Utah Jazz in a very satisfying performance.

A couple of days after the Spurs made Dallas look like a youth basketball team on the glass, Dallas thumped the Jazz on the glass at both ends. Led by Morez Johnsons's hilarious 17 rebounds (10 on the offensive end), the Mavericks managed to control both ends of the floor without having a stellar shooting game. The Mavericks shot under 30% from three and under 45% from the floor and still bodied the Jazz badly in this contest.

So now it's question worth asking: was or is Dallas a bad rebounding team or did Dallas simply have to play the Houston Rockets and San Antonio Spurs a total of four times in their first seven games? I suspect the answer is somewhere in the middle, but it's nice to see that they CAN rebound the basketball.

Mr. Washington has taken much heat around these parts and will continue to be part of trade speculation moving forward, but I have to acknowledge just how good he's been. His 26 points, including six three-pointers, helped keep the Mavericks in the hunt during the first half when much of the team didn't have juice. I have been enormously impressed with his shot this season, along with his consistent effort. Washington was the quiet (or maybe not so quiet) X-factor in their run to the Finals, as he did a little bit of everything.

I have no idea what lies in the future for PJ, but watching him find some early success has felt good.

In his last two contests, Kyrie Irving has shot just 16 of 47, which, if you can do math, is not good. And yet, Dallas is a final basket and some meh reffing away from beating the San Antonio Spurs and as we know they just gave the Utah Jazz a beating. Not NEEDING Kyrie to be a Superman is a gift. He'll be just fine.

I lead with that preamble because this was not an easy win. This team has something. What that is, I don't rightly know at the moment, but I am curious to find out.

There are so many positives from this game. So many building points to go off of. The aforementioned Johnson, looking like a man who can control the paint at times. Walter Clayton Jr., a fearless addition to the back court. Zaccharie Risacher and the bundle of frenetic energy. And that's just the bench, and it doesn't consider the guys who couldn't play like Sergio De Larrea.

Not knowing what this team could be feels spectacular, because the arrow is pointed in the right direction. Enjoy it.

#2983. Market sell-off hits hard: Advisors say 'stay the course with an eye on buying'

Top financial advisors aren't urging clients to change their portfolios after Thursday's sharp market sell-off, even as retail investors were heavy sellers of individual stocks.

In a Thursday flash poll of advisors from CNBC's Financial Advisor 100 and Financial Advisor Council, more than three-quarters of respondents said investors' first steps should be to check their allocation against their goals or stay the course. The online poll, conducted from 2:30 p.m. to 6 p.m. ET on Thursday, was sent to 121 firms and 38 responded.

Retail investors sold individual stocks Thursday as technology stocks tumbled, and the Dow gave back its post-midterm election gains. The S&P 500 suffered its worst one-day drop since April 2025 on Thursday. Analysts linked the sell-off to fears of a data center construction slowdown following major Democratic election wins.

The Nasdaq Composite tumbled 5.5%, closing more than 10% below its most recent high and putting it in what Wall Street regards as a correction. The broad-market S&P 500 lost 4.4%, while the Dow fell more than 1,800 points, or 3.4%.

Yet, financial advisors who answered the CNBC poll overwhelmingly responded that they are not recommending changes to their clients' portfolios. The broader message from interviews with several advisors: review whether your portfolio still matches your goals, but don't let a sudden market move make the decision for you.

For investors who continue contributing to a 401(k), a market decline can mean buying investments at lower prices over time, said Andy Pratt, director of investment strategy at Burney Wealth Management in Reston, Virginia.

"If you have a 401(k), money is being allocated there biweekly, monthly, you know, whatever your pay period is, and so you're able to dollar-cost average in," Pratt said. "So when the market does sell off, you're entering at a much better spot."

Pratt said a well-designed portfolio may not need changes during a downturn. John Mullen, managing director, president and CEO of Parsons Capital Management in Providence, Rhode Island, put it this way: "Stay the course with an eye on buying."

Yet, if investors are panicking, that could be a sign to reassess whether their investments still fit their goals and risk tolerance, financial advisors say.

Carolyn McClanahan, a certified financial planner, physician and founder of Life Planning Partners in Jacksonville, Florida, says she tells clients to follow this strategy, which "works beautifully."

McClanahan said investors should stick to an investment policy that reflects their needs and "tune out the rest of the noise."

Tra Sullivan, a certified financial planner, wealth manager, and chief investment officer at Dallas-based Beaird Harris Wealth Management, said historically the U.S. stock market has often risen in the 12 months after midterm elections, but he views that pattern as context rather than a promise.

He added that when markets fall, "there's always a recovery, and a lot of times, in most cases, more quickly than we anticipate", although past rebounds don't guarantee future recoveries.

Nasdaq futures rebounded in pre-market trading on Friday with a bounce in chip stocks following a sell-off that sent the Nasdaq into correction territory the day before.

Still, if market weakness continues, Sullivan said he brings the discussion back to a client's financial plan.

For long-term investors, Dan Moisand, a CFP and financial advisor at Orlando, Florida-based Moisand Fitzgerald Tamayo, said resilience can matter more than trying to time each market swing.

"Being resilient has proven to be more profitable than trying to be nimble," he said.

Correction: An earlier version of this article misstated John Mullen's certifications. He is managing director, president and CEO of Parsons Capital Management.

#2982. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

#2981. Fake job interview scams like the one that hit Binance are getting more sophisticated. Here are the new signs to watch for

Job search scams can cost applicants hundreds or thousands of dollars. But one fake job interview recently impacted over $6 billion in assets.

Binance, the world's largest cryptocurrency exchange, announced on Tuesday it recently experienced a security breach impacting $6.34 billion in digital assets. The hack was enabled in part by a job seeker's nightmare: bogus job interviews.

While the Binance hackers had their sights set on a much bigger target than an individual job seeker, job-related scams are on the rise. The FBI's Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3) tallied employment-related losses amounting to roughly $363 million in 2025, up sharply from $264 million the year prior, and $70 million in 2023. The actual figure is likely higher, since many victims of such crimes do not report them.

Those orchestrating fake job scams have many potential targets today "because the job search is taking so long for so many right now," says Stefanie Fackrell, a former recruiter at Google and Nvidia. While October's jobs report showed promise as the U.S. economy added 165,000 jobs, far more than expected, many applicants have been struggling through a low-hire, low-fire market for months.

Artificial intelligence can also make scams more widespread and harder to detect.

"Cyber scams in general are becoming much more sophisticated just because they're evolving at the same speed as AI and public access to AI," says Garber. "AI is going to make things seem more and more real."

In 2025, victims reported losses of almost $13 million to AI-involved employment scams, according to the IC3.

Binance's announcement included an abbreviated play-by-play of how its hack went down. Fackrell and Garber broke down some of the warning signs in how the events unfolded, including what job seekers can learn from the incident.

What happened: A software engineer, who was not named, was contacted through "a legitimate job-hunting platform" by someone posing as a project director, Binance said in its announcement. That person arranged for the engineer to have an initial interview with a purported HR representative and a second-round interview with the supposed CEO of a fake company.

Experts' red flags: A headshot that's clearly AI-generated or otherwise looks uncanny, as well as sparse profile information or activity, and few connections should give you pause, Fackrell says. On LinkedIn, for example, you can click the three dots on a person's profile to open an "About this member" pop-up, where you can see when they joined LinkedIn and if they've verified their identity, workplace and school.

Though some small startups may arrange for an interview with a C-suite executive, it's generally unusual, particularly for a second-round interview, unless you'd be reporting to that person, she adds.

What happened: In the first round, the fake HR representative had a live video interview with the software engineer, even sharing with him a social media account with personal photos and videos to build trust, Binance said. The tactic was "more advanced and deceptive than previously seen social engineering approaches," Binance added in its announcement.

Experts' red flags: A recruiter might bring up commonalities with a candidate like the same alma mater or ex-employer to "bring that human element into the conversation," Fackrell says. But sharing personal social media in a first-round interview is strange, she adds.

In video interviews in general, some red flags might include the person moving unusually slowly or not responding to one of your questions, or their visual skipping a beat, Garber says.

What happened: The second-round interview with the purported CEO of the fake company was conducted on "a popular social chat app," Binance said, and the software engineer was instructed to download "an 'assignment' containing a malicious code repository onto his work computer."

Experts' red flags: Interviews commonly happen over Microsoft Teams, Zoom or Google Meet, not a social chat app. "I don't know anybody that's FaceTiming or WhatsApping any of their candidates," Fackrell says.

While take-home assignments, particularly for technical roles, may use third-party systems, don't download software if you're not familiar with it, Fackrell advises. These assignments should be shared via email through a legitimate system, not over the phone. Job seekers should take interviews on a personal device, not a work computer.

Job sites like LinkedIn and Indeed, and entities like the Federal Trade Commission and Better Business Bureau, also have more information about common scams.

You should not have to provide sensitive information or payment in order to get a job, according to the FTC. Legitimate recruiters will reach out from their corporate email addresses, not a personal address. Other red flags include pressure to move quickly through the hiring process or seeing multiple typos in communications.

"Do your research before clicking, responding or engaging with anybody," Fackrell says.

Take a minute to think about how you were contacted and what you're being asked to do to proceed, Garber suggests, and cross-check job postings against the company's official careers page.

"If seems too good to be true, it definitely is," says Garber.

#2980. Tsai partner on sports as an asset class and bigger business behind Nets

When Joe Tsai bought a 49% stake in the Brooklyn Nets in 2017, he and longtime investment partner Ollie Weisberg saw value in the NBA's "limited" supply of teams and the growing market for live sports — starting in Brooklyn. Opportunities to buy into the league are rare, let alone to own one of two teams in the country's largest market.

"We wanted to invest in sports because we think it's a phenomenal financial investment," Weisberg told Barron's Patti Domm in a recent interview. "We believe that live sports is extremely unique. Live sports bring people together."

Weisberg graduated from Harvard, where he studied international development economics and Chinese before writing his thesis on Russian and Chinese privatization. He later joined Goldman Sachs where he worked on the firm's original Series A investment in Alibaba and negotiated the term sheet with Tsai. After a decade working for Ken Griffin of investment giant Citadel, he reunited with Tsai to build Blue Pool Capital. And when Tsai bought into the Nets, Weisberg joined him as a minority owner and vice chair of Brooklyn Sports & Entertainment.

Their new partnership was rooted in the changing economics of live sports.

"We believed that we would ride along with the growth of media rights, which primarily, five, 10, 15 years ago were in cable. But as you know, we've had a shift to OTT (over-the-top distribution) on your phone through streaming, and so we believed that would grow. That was a long-term bet on that trend. Then you layer in the concept of buying an asset where there is fixed supply. There were only 30 [NBA] teams.

"We believed that Brooklyn is a massively growing part of New York City," he told Domm. "The team went from Jersey to Brooklyn back in 2012. We were very focused on how we could participate long term in owning control of a team. It's not that often that control deals are on the table. We actually bought the team from Mikhail Prokhorov in two tranches. It allowed us to wade in as a minority shareholder at 49% and then ultimately take control," Weisberg said.

The two believed their experience building businesses could help improve the franchise.

"We felt that we could bring our expertise and history of building businesses to turn the team around, both on the court and off the court businesswise," Weisberg said. While the partnership has yet to produce a title for the Nets, the Liberty won the WNBA championship two years ago and the value of the franchise has more than doubled, per Sportico, going from $3.3 billion in 2019 to an estimated $7.4 billion this year. In addition, the Liberty's valuation is now seen at more than $600 million, dozens of times greater than the estimated $14 million the Tsais paid James Dolan for it in 2019.

That Brooklyn investment was never intended as a short-term play, said Weisberg. But that doesn't mean the Tsais are opposed to bringing in outside investors. In 2024, they sold a minority stake in Brooklyn Sports & Entertainment, the parent company of the Nets, Liberty and Barclays Center, to Julia Koch and her family. Tsai remained chairman of BSE Global and governor of the Nets with Weisberg as the alternate governor.

"We view the Brooklyn Nets as a family asset, and so we capitalize in making it worth more, right? But we're not capitalizing by looking to sell it," he said.

Increasing a team's value also means improving the experience for fans. Weisberg pointed to owners who have invested in the product around the game.

"There have been owners that have taken teams — and we'd like to think we're part of this group — that have made the product better, made the experience better: the food, the wine, the venues, the club space. That drives higher revenue, more money, therefore more value. I would think of this as a circular reference. Obviously, more funds get attracted, more funds get raised, and team values go up. So, the answer to your question is, unabashedly, yes. It is an asset class that has arrived."

The Nets and Barclays Center is investing more than $150 million in renovations over five years, part of it financed with the $688 million the Kochs paid for the minority piece. The latest phase, announced in October, includes a redesigned atrium, a 9,000-square-foot event-level club and a new American Express entrance and lounge.

Technology is another part of that effort.

In September, the Nets announced a multiyear partnership with Alibaba as their official technology and cloud partner. The deal includes 360-degree replay technology and the Nets Figure Lab.

The arena and technology investments are part of a broader approach to sports ownership that extends beyond Brooklyn. Among other sports assets, Blue Pool Capital's portfolio includes a three percent stake in the NFL's Miami Dolphins and SailGP, an international sailing league founded by Larry Ellison, the founder of tech giant Oracle. Last year, Bloomberg estimated that Tsai's sports investments were nearly equal to the value of his stock in Alibaba.

"We're looking at Formula One. We have quite a lot of activity in lacrosse. I get decks (investment proposals) on new teams and leagues every day… Soccer is an area we'll look at. It's about understanding the league, and then understanding which team, like we did with the Brooklyn Nets," Weisberg said.

That approach also applies to the market surrounding a team. Weisberg pointed to Blue Pool's investment in the Miami Dolphins, where the opportunity extended beyond the football franchise itself.

"We're really happy with Miami, and Miami's really interesting because it owns four assets. It owns The Miami Dolphins, the Hard Rock Stadium, the Formula One race, and the Miami Open. We took a view that the economic development of Miami would also propel ticket sales across all those assets.

Of course, the Knicks' success has added another dimension to the competition for basketball fans in New York, but Weisberg said the rivalry is something the organization welcomes.

"I would tell you that this fires us up. We want to compete. It's great for New York. I'm originally a New Yorker. We're all New Yorkers, but it is getting us fired up to compete," Weisberg said.

He also described the relationship between NBA owners as a balance between competition and cooperation.

"One of the things that's beautiful about the NBA is off the court, we're business partners. On the court, we compete. A lot of the media rights are shared by the owners. So, when the Knicks won, I'm happy and thrilled for my partner, Jim Dolan, but it's just going to make us that much more fired up to compete."

Building the Nets' audience is another long-term part of the investment. Weisberg said the organization is looking to attract New Yorkers who do not already have a team while developing a connection with younger generations through its community work. The process, he added, won't be simple or short.

"Strategically, we're looking at bringing over the marginal 40- or 50-year-old that doesn't have a team. And then we're looking through our community work, through our Brooklyn basketball community organization, which includes basketball camps, to long term build a fan base. This is a poignant nuance.

"Building a franchise takes decades. Joe and I are prepared to take the time to do that. When we view ourselves as stewards of the Brooklyn Nets, that's the mentality you have to have. You may know, during COVID, the early days, we paid all our hourly workers before anybody else did. We're a community asset," Weisberg said.

The organization's Brooklyn Basketball programming has reached more than 50,000 young people annually through free clinics and school programming. The program is also expanding into Greenpoint, Bushwick and Gowanus in 2027.

Weisberg used the word "asset" 11 times in his answers to Barron's. He described sports as an asset class, NBA franchises as fixed-supply assets, the Nets as a family asset and the organization as a community asset.

Those ideas aren't mutually exclusive. They see the Nets as a valuable investment, but one whose value also depends on what they build in and around it — a better fan experience, a stronger connection to Brooklyn and a fanbase that can grow over generations.

#2979. Nasdaq drops into correction territory — here’s what typically happens next

The Nasdaq Composite plunged into correction territory on Thursday. History shows more selling can follow in the near term, but a recovery eventually takes hold three months later.

The tech-heavy index slid more than 5% on Thursday, closing more than 11% below its record reached last month. On Wall Street, any decline greater than 10% is a correction. A 20% pullback qualifies as a bear market.

Across 10 previous correction episodes over the last decade, selloffs in 2025, 2022, 2020 and 2018 were followed by further rough selling over the next one month. But the Nasdaq was back to rally mode again after six months for all those occasions except one: 2022.

Overall, the Nasdaq gained an average of 9.41% from the correction bottom over the following three months and 17.03% over six months.

The January 2022 correction was the one that offered a harsher outcome. The Nasdaq lost another 18.3% over the next six months and was down 24.3% a year later.

The catalyst for this correction was Democrats sweeping the House and Senate in Tuesday's midterm elections. The party also made significant gains in Texas, where Gina Hinojosa won the race for governor after campaigning on an immediate moratorium for data center development.

Investors are now weighing whether tighter state-level restrictions could slow data center construction, putting pressure on companies tied to the artificial intelligence trade.

Frank Cappelleri, founder of CappThesis, pointed to past midterm election cycles for guidance and found that points to more volatility in the near term as well.

The chart analyst said the Nasdaq was higher 12 months after the election in all but one of 13 midterm cycles since 1974.

"The caveat is that the path can be a volatile one," he said.

Cappelleri pointed to 2018, when the Nasdaq rallied the day after Democrats won the House. A sharp December sell-off followed, but the index was ultimately about 15% higher a year after the election, he said.

Chip stocks tumbled Thursday, with the iShares Semiconductor ETF (SOXX) falling 12% and for its worst day since the Covid-19 breakout of March 2020.

— CNBC's Fred Imbert contributed to the report

#2978. The Vikings could be ready to make a trade for a quarterback, if they lose Monday night

Kyler Murray or Carson Wentz? Carson Wentz or Kyler Murray?

This week's apparent soft benching of Murray, who has a hamstring injury that possibly wouldn't have kept him from playing if the Vikings had beaten the Lions on Sunday, gives Wentz a chance to beat the 6-1 Bills on Monday night. If he pulls it off, Wentz could keep the job for the remainder of the four-game gjallarhorn gauntlet that ends with trips to Wisconsin to play the Packers and Mexico to play the 49ers.

If Wentz fails, the Vikings may mobilize. Immediately.

The Vikings will have roughly 16 hours after Monday night's game ends to close a deal with another team for a quarterback. On Monday, we made the case for a trade for Colts quarterback Daniel Jones. As the week has unfolded, a variey of indications have emerged that it's a real possibility.

Jones, who surely is dismayed by the Colts' decision to prematurely throw in the towel by trading away guard Quenton Nelson and defensive tackle DeForest Bucker, would likely welcome the chance to return to a team with which he spent a couple of months in 2024 — and to lead the effort to propel the neo-Purple People Eaters to the postseason.

The other possibility would be to try to bring Kirk Cousins home. That would be more difficult. At 4-4, the Raiders are on the fringes of contention. Win or lose on Sunday at San Francisco, why disrupt something that's working? Cousins currently ranks third in passing yards with 2,124 and second in touchdown passes with 19. His passer rating of 101.4 is higher than Josh Allen's.

Even if the Raiders would be inclined to move Cousins and ride with Fernando Mendoza, it would cost far less to get Jones than it would to get Cousins. Especially since the Colts may already be planning to cut Jones before a $4 million roster bonus comes due in March and $6.51 million of his $33.49 million salary becomes fully-guaranteed.

With a new G.M. coming next year, why wouldn't the Colts want a clean slate? Doing so would wipe out $38 million in non-guaranteed cash for 2027. Trading Jones would result in getting value; cutting Jones would get them nothing.

It may all come down to Monday night. If Minnesota wins, chances are nothing happens. If Minnesota loses, Tuesday could become a very interesting day.

#2977. Wall Street's fear gauge may be flashing a buy sign

There's nothing like a good selloff to turn a trader into an investor – so the saying goes. It's an adage that echoes the sentiment of an even older one: buy when there's blood in the streets. The maxims ring true after Thursday's selloff in stocks that sent the Cboe Volatility Index (VIX) to its biggest one-day move since April 2025's "Liberation" week for tariffs, according to data from Bespoke Investment Group. In the 19 instances since 1990 in which the VIX rose at least five points from under 20 – it surged more than 11 points from 16.6 to 28.2 Thursday – the median return for the S&P 500 Index three months later was 4.2%, and 4.8% six months later, according to Bespoke analysis that also showed returns were positive 68% and 79% of the time over each period, respectively. "These types of sharp spikes from placid levels are like a line of thunderstorms coming through in summer," said Paul Hickey, co-founder of Bespoke. "The sky quickly darkens and wind gusts, but the loudest thunder and brightest lightning doesn't last long."

To be sure, the data do show the storms can be disruptive near-term. While the median return one month after such events was 2.1%, the average return was negative 0.4%, thanks to a few big downdrafts following volatility surges in 1990 and more recently, Feb. 24, 2020, when the Covid selloff was just getting started. According to X account OddStats, there were only 12 other days in stock market history prior to Thursday when the S&P 500 lost 4% and the Nasdaq 100 Index lost 6% – including one during Liberation week 2025 and three instances during the 2020 pandemic.Tech volatility was even more pronounced as the Nasdaq 100 posted its worst day since March 2020. The Cboe Nasdaq Volatility Index VXN jumped more than 13 points to 38.3, the highest since April 11, 2025. The selling was so intense in chipmakers that the VanEck Semiconductor ETF (SMH) traded below its net asset value. Yet options traders were still leaning bearish in their trades into the bell Thursday, with 670,000 puts traded out of the total 900,000 options on the ETF. Of the total $1.7 billion options premium exchanged, $1.5 billion of it was in puts, according to SpotGamma.

#2975. Lakers coach reveals next destinations for Bronny James, Cameron Carr

The Lakers recently sent two of their youngest players to their G League affiliate.

Rookie Cameron Carr and third-year guard Bronny James are on assignment with the Coachella Valley Lakers. Lakers coach JJ Redick revealed the messaging he shared with Carr and James on the decision.

Cameron Carr #43 of the Los Angeles Lakers plays defense during the game against the Toronto Raptors on November 1, 2026 at Crypto.Com Arena in Los Angeles, California. NBAE via Getty Images

"You guys need reps, and you need to play," Redick said.

The Lakers' No. 24 overall draft pick began the season with an oblique injury but made his NBA debut last Sunday against the Raptors. Since then, Carr has played a minute in a total of three games.

James, whose $2.3 million contract was fully guaranteed in the offseason, has competed in five games this season and totaled 2.4 minutes.

Los Angeles Lakers guard Bronny James Jr. replaces guard Luka Don¿i¿ during the second half of an NBA basketball game against the Toronto Raptors Sunday, Nov. 1, 2026, in Los Angeles. AP Photo/Scott Strazzante

As bench players, Carr and James evidently haven't received much playing time as the Lakers continue their strong start to the season. Given their limited minutes, the Lakers' decision to send them to Coachella Valley allows the pair to get the reps they haven't received at the NBA level.

That's not to say Carr and James won't contribute to the team as the season goes on.

The guards are set to rejoin the Lakers for their upcoming three-game road trip to Detroit, Atlanta and Milwaukee.
