#2974. Fantasy Football Start 'Em Sit 'Em: Justin Boone's Week 9 lineup advice

Justin Boone provides a weekly look at undervalued fantasy players to consider starting this week and potential busts you might want to leave on your bench.

For the rest of your lineup decisions, consult his Week 9 rankings.

It's almost surprising that Prescott is the QB11 in fantasy points per game this year, considering he went a month without a multi-touchdown game at one point. Fortunately, he's found the end zone at least twice in each of the past two weeks.

Now, Prescott faces a Colts team that's circling the drain before a likely selloff at the trade deadline. In its first game after dealing Quenton Nelson and DeForest Buckner, Indy was dismantled 42-7 by the Jaguars. The Cowboys are 5.5-point favorites on the road with the fourth-highest implied point total on the week.

Though Dak hasn't been the most reliable fantasy option this season, you can start him confidently as a top-five QB versus the Colts.

Malik Willis vs. Lions – With the Dolphins' young receiving corps getting healthy, Willis has been heating up lately as a solid fantasy streamer who offers a high floor and high ceiling. This is setting up to be a potential ceiling game for him, against a Detroit defense giving up the most fantasy points to QBs, including multiple TDs in every outing.

C.J. Stroud at Chargers – Stroud has quietly had a very solid season and, with the Texans' struggles in the run game, you can expect the team to continue relying on his arm. The 25-year-old is tied for the fifth most fantasy points among quarterbacks over the last five weeks. Coming off the bye, Stroud should get a boost from Marvin Harrison Jr. getting more comfortable in the offense and Tank Dell seeing a rise in snaps.

Brown only finished as the RB20 in a great matchup with the Titans last week, but that would have been a top-12 fantasy back if his 20-yard touchdown catch wasn't nullified by a penalty.

The good news is that Brown continues to dominate the Bengals backfield, playing almost 70% of the snaps last week and turning them into 20 touches, 101 scrimmage yards and eight catches.

He also gets another positive matchup with the Falcons defense that's the fourth-easiest matchup for fantasy running backs. Atlanta has allowed seven touchdowns to RBs over its last three games and just got decimated for over 300 rushing yards by the Buccaneers backs in Week 8.

I know it's been frustrating at times having Brown in your lineup; just don't give up now. Bigger days are within reach. If you are planning to sell him, you'll want to wait until after this game to get the most value.

Jonah Coleman at Panthers – Coleman returned from IR and immediately took control of the Broncos backfield, playing 58% of the snaps over the last two weeks and racking up 173 scrimmage yards, six receptions and a touchdown. Now, he gets a Panthers defense that's permitting the second-most fantasy points to his position. The rookie is a top-15 fantasy RB option this week.

Blake Corum at Commanders – Over the last month, the Rams offense has leaned on their backfield more with Davante Adams hitting IR. That resulted in both backs averaging top-15 fantasy stats, with Corum at RB13 and Kyren Williams at RB15. Don't be misled; Williams is still the lead back and Corum hasn't played over 50% of the snaps in any game. However, he's getting plenty of opportunity, which should continue as 5.5-point favorites over the Commanders.

Tyler Allgeier at Seahawks – While I hate the matchup, it's impossible to argue with the setup for Allgeier. Jeremiyah Love is on IR and Bam Knight is in concussion protocol. That leaves Allgeier as the last man standing in Arizona's RB room. Though you can't expect another 191-yard, five-catch, two-touchdown performance (because he's not playing the Cowboys defense), Allgeier can still be started as a volume-based, low-end RB2.

Jefferson isn't a name that makes it to the Start section very often because he's such a talented player that fantasy managers don't need a reminder. However, given the state of the quarterback play in Minnesota, starting Jefferson wasn't as obvious as it had been in the past.

The veteran wideout has been held to receiving-yard totals of 55, 32, 44 and 33 in four of his last five outings and Kyler Murray wasn't finding his rhythm in Kevin O'Connell's scheme.

Murray's new hamstring injury gave the coaching staff a chance to make a change and they jumped on it, naming Carson Wentz the starter for Monday's matchup with the Bills. With Buffalo's defense still being a work in progress, Wentz and company should be able to get the Vikings' passing game back on track.

That's great news for Jefferson, who has averaged 6.4 receptions and 89.1 receiving yards during the seven games where Wentz has played at least 80% of the snaps over the last two seasons. That includes an 8-92-2 stat line from the opener this year.

This move pushed Jefferson back into the top-10 fantasy WRs and in the conversation for a spot in the top-five for as long as Wentz is under center.

George Pickens at Colts – Pickens has been playing through shoulder and hamstring injuries the last couple of weeks and his playing time has taken a slight hit because of it. Jerry Jones was very clear this week that he believes Pickens needs to be getting the ball more, so this could be the rare squeaky wheel game initiated by the owner. As it stands, Pickens is still 14th in receiving yards among wideouts, but that ranking could rise thanks to a matchup with the Colts.

Rashee Rice vs. Jets – For all the concerns about Rice before the season, he's actually performed well in every outing. Rice has at least 83 yards or a touchdown in all four of his healthy appearances, including a 7-53-1 stat line in his first game back last week, despite only playing 56% of the snaps. That number should rise in a favorable spot against a Jets defense that plays a lot of single-high coverage, which Rice excels against.

Rome Odunze vs. Buccaneers – Odunze is 20th in receiving yards, but 53rd in fantasy points scored among wideouts. The discrepancy exists due to a lack of touchdowns, with Odunze not finding the end zone yet this season. Fortunately, the offense has been regaining its form with Caleb Williams back and Odunze has posted two of his best outings this season in recent weeks, with 69 yards against the Patriots and 87 yards versus the Seahawks. Next up is a Bucs defense that's allowed five different WRs to top 85 yards in the last month.

Through the first five weeks, LaPorta was a top-five fantasy tight end against one of the easier schedules at his position. Then came a bye, followed by back-to-back matchups against the two toughest defenses against his position: the Packers and Vikings.

That led conspiracy theorists to speculate that the team was punishing LaPorta for his comments about being frustrated with contract extension talks with the organization. I'm guessing the degree of difficulty being turned up on him had more to do with it.

Thankfully, a softer matchup has arrived with the Dolphins defense giving up the third-most fantasy points to tight ends. The Lions are also tied for the second-highest implied point total on the slate. This all points to a bounce-back week for LaPorta, which is why he's back in my top five.

Kyle Pitts Sr. vs. Bengals – After three straight top-12 TE finishes, Pitts finally came back down to earth with just two catches for six yards in a rainy loss to the Bucs last week. Now, he suits up for a potential shoutout in the dome against a Bengals defense that's the ninth-most generous unit to tight ends.

Mike Gesicki at Falcons – You can also start the other tight end in that game, with Gesicki having a great chance to extend his touchdown streak versus Atlanta. The veteran has scored a touchdown in six of his seven outings and currently sits as the TE6 in fantasy points per game. You could do worse than streaming him in a game that's tied for the highest projected point total at 52.5.

It seems like the distant past now, but Young was the QB4 overall in fantasy points per game through the first six weeks, in part thanks to an easy early-season schedule. Life comes at you fast, though and a slew of injuries on the offensive line have completely changed the outlook for this offense. It doesn't help that their last two opponents have been much tougher, the Buccaneers and Packers, leading to a test that Young ultimately failed.

Coming off his two lowest fantasy outputs of the season, Young now has another difficult challenge ahead with the Broncos defense that got back Patrick Surtain II last week. There will be better days ahead, including the Saints matchup in Week 10, but for now, Young needs to head to your bench.

Geno Smith at Chiefs – Kansas City's defense is allowing the sixth-fewest fantasy points to quarterbacks and tends to be even more stout at home. Meanwhile, the Jets will be without their top receiver Garrett Wilson, who is in concussion protocol. This isn't the spot to play Smith.

Baker Mayfield at Bears – At some point, Mayfield will begin throwing again and this could be the week, since he's facing a high-scoring Bears offense. But forgive me if I'm not interested in starting a quarterback who hasn't topped 13 fantasy points in a game this season and has only attempted 14 passes over the last two weeks.

White came through in Week 8 thanks to a touchdown, but his usage (13 touches, 47% snap share) was disappointing given the matchup with the Eagles and the fact that Jacory Croskey-Merritt is on IR.

The situation isn't getting better this week against a Rams defense that's his toughest opponent yet. Not only are they allowing the seventh-fewest fantasy points to running backs, but it's possible Myles Garrett could be activated for the game.

This all points to a risky setup for White, who will likely need another touchdown to save his fantasy day again.

Zach Charbonnet vs. Cardinals – The Seahawks are 8.5-point favorites, so the possibility of a goal-line carry is always there. However, it's tough to get overly excited about Charbonnet, who managed just 29 yards on nine touches while playing 37% of the snaps in his season debut. Rookie Jadarian Price displayed far more juice during the game and should earn more touches moving forward, while Charbonnet remains in a supporting role.

Omarion Hampton vs. Texans – Coming off the bye, Hampton played a season-low 29.5% of the snaps. The sophomore back has only topped 56 scrimmage yards once this season. Like most of the running backs in this section, he's purely a TD-or-bust RB3.

Jordan Mason vs. Bills – Mason returned to the lineup in Week 7 with a 64% snap share and 15 carries for 68 yards. Unfortunately, just one week later, those numbers dropped to 28% of the snaps and just eight carries for 20 yards. While the matchup with the Bills is a positive one, it's hard to trust Mason until we see more from him and the Vikings offense as a whole.

Wilson finally rediscovered his ceiling with an eight-catch, 111-yard, one-touchdown performance, but it took a very friendly matchup with the Cowboys to get there.

Wilson also accomplished the feat while playing through two broken ribs, something that will continue to be a challenge this week.

It's also worth remembering that Wilson was held to 55 yards or less in three straight games before that springboard encounter with Dallas. The Seahawks defense, which is giving up the fewest fantasy points to wideouts, won't be the same kind of pushover. That makes Wilson a volatile WR3.

Jalen Coker vs. Broncos – The issues on the offensive line and the difficult matchup with the Broncos defense are more than enough reason to avoid Coker this week. He's only managed three catches over his last two outings.

Chris Godwin Jr. at Bears – Godwin hasn't topped 50 receiving yards in a game this season and hasn't reached 40 yards in his last six appearances. Injuries have simply sapped his effectiveness, and given his age, it's likely his best days are behind him.

Xavier Worthy vs. Jets – Worthy has been held to 25 yards or less in five of his seven games this season. He's also only found the end zone once all year, making him a very shaky flex option at best.

Kincaid's best performances have coincided with DJ Moore being in the lineup, which gives you hope that he can get back to being a top-10 fantasy tight end the rest of the way. Unfortunately, this week's matchup is with the Vikings defense, which is the biggest threat to tight ends by far this season.

Minnesota is allowing just 5.5 fantasy points per game, with the second-place Packers coming in at 7.0 fppg. The Vikings also haven't allowed a touchdown to a tight end and we've yet to see them give up more than 65 yards to a player at the position. Even if you look back to last year, Brian Flores' crew was the eighth-worst opponent for tight ends. If Kincaid comes through this week, it will be an outlier based on what we've seen from this defense in 2026.

Chig Okonkwo vs. Rams – After back-to-back 60-plus-yard games, Okonkwo managed just two catches for 32 yards in a tougher matchup with the Eagles last week. The Rams are the seventh most difficult opponent for fantasy tight ends, so I wouldn't get my hopes up here.

Tyler Higbee at Commanders – Higbee has been held to 22 yards, 14 yards and 14 yards over his last three outings, and it's possible Terrance Ferguson could return for this contest.

#2973. Democratic sweep gives markets a reason to rethink path of interest rates

The Democrats' resounding victory in the U.S. midterms has the potential to curb interest rate hikes globally next year, according to market watchers — but the uncertain path of oil prices, U.S. economic growth and fiscal policies will be key.

Central banks around the world are back in monetary tightening mode, with the Federal Reserve, Bank of England, European Central Bank and Bank of Japan all raising rates in recent months.

The shift has in part stemmed from the U.S. and Israel's war with Iran driving up prices in oil and gas markets. The war has also exacerbated a squeeze in global refining capacity which has sent the cost of products like diesel, gasoline and jet fuel soaring.

Traders largely expected big Democratic gains this week after polls pointed in their favor, but there was still uncertainty leading up to the vote about whether the party would capture both chambers of Congress, increasing the ability for lawmakers to challenge President Donald Trump's policymaking.

Crude oil prices tumbled following the clear-cut Democratic victory in both the House and Senate, with international benchmark ICE Brent crude falling 8.7% on Wednesday, closing below $100 a barrel for the first time since September.

That was largely attributed to raised expectations that the result may mean Trump is more inclined to strike a deal to end the war in Iran, helping restore Gulf refinery capacity and improve export conditions. Vice President JD Vance's lengthy statement blaming Republican losses on the Iran war boosted that view.

Dan Coatsworth, head of markets at AJ Bell, said there had been "an admission from the Republican camp that the Iran war cost them a clean sweep in the election, which the market has taken to indicate that a swift resolution to the Middle East conflict could help them save face."

"Trump might relish the opportunity to take all the credit if an end to the war leads to a big drop in energy costs."

If the election result "pushes for a resolution of the conflict in the Middle East, that might bring oil down, and that's been an issue for the Fed and inflation, and part of the reason why rates are higher," said Andrew Slimmon, senior portfolio manager and head of the Applied Equity Advisors at Morgan Stanley.

Historic data shows a tight correlation between oil prices and global rates, Ralph Axel, rates strategist at Bank of America, said in a research note published Thursday. Higher oil prices over the next three to six months would likely mean "significantly higher interest rates" in the U.S., but especially so in the U.K. and Germany, he said.

"If oil prices rise, we think rates will too, especially at the front end where long-end term premium is less of a factor and monetary policy expectations play a larger role," he added.

As the uptick in crude prices on Thursday and choppiness on Friday showed, a resolution to the nearly nine-month-long conflict is far from locked in.

"For now, oil has begun to climb back up again, and interest rate expectations have not radically changed from pre-election levels," AJ Bell's Dan Coatsworth noted.

Money markets on Thursday indicated a moderate change in expectations from a week ago, both in the U.S. and Europe, with traders pricing in a slightly higher chance of monetary easing on a one-year horizon.

The effect on a two-year timeframe was more pronounced in the euro area than the U.S., according to CNBC analysis of LSEG data, with rate pricing down 8.7 basis points on the week for the ECB versus 4.6 basis points for the Fed.

"Republicans don't really have an off-ramp here at this stage," said Laurel Rapp, director for the U.S. and North America Program at Chatham House. "We know the Democrats will seek to take votes in the House and the Senate to constrain the Republicans, to restrain the administration on Iran war powers. But whether that will bind the administration is another matter."

Eurasia Group's Clayton Allen meanwhile told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" that while the war with Iran is unpopular at home, leaving the Middle East region without tangible wins would also be unpopular and that he expected an escalation within weeks.

Andrew Bailey, governor of the Bank of England — which hiked rates by 25 basis points on Thursday — told CNBC that energy price pressures stemmed from a combination of obstructions on the Strait of Hormuz, a shortage in refining capacity, and low European gas storage.

"A peace process in the Middle East will not shift all of those elements," he told CNBC's Steve Sedgwick on Thursday.

"Let's wait and see, because we have seen that fall in energy prices over the last 48 hours, but it is a very volatile situation, as we've learnt over the last seven, eight, nine months."

Ken Egan, head of European Sovereign Credit at KBRA, said the interest rate outlook would be primarily impacted by whether economies have enough underlying momentum for existing inflation pressures to become more persistent.

Europe is "heading into winter after months of elevated energy prices, and the full impact is still feeding through to the wider economy," he said by email.

"The concern for central banks is whether these pressures start to push up wages and prices more broadly, or whether weaker demand limits that pass-through."

A key question for the Federal Reserve will be whether the blue sweep "could lead to more fiscal prudence in the U.S. and thus less inflation," Jane Foley, head of FX strategy at Rabobank, said by email.

"It is very early, however, to be sure of the fiscal outlook, not least because many Democrats were voted in to soften cost of living pressures ... so it is very difficult to come to a firm conclusion at this point as to how the outlook for rates will change next year," she said.

The high level of U.S. fiscal stimulus and whether it is about to change is the "million dollar question" right now, James Sullivan, managing director and head of Asia Pacific equity research at J.P. Morgan, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia."

"The reasons why you're seeing inflation running high is a combination of a range of factors, from the war in Iran, to tariffs, to significant tightness in particular areas of the energy complex," he said.

"But it's also important to recognize the U.S. government has continued to spend at very high levels, into an economic expansion with low unemployment, and that's driving very specific macroeconomic implications. If that changes, you potentially take the pressure off the rate environment in a way that gives the Fed significantly more forward flexibility."

Daniel Clifton, head of policy research at Baird Strategas, said the impact on the data center buildout in the U.S. was another big factor to watch.

"In 2025, we had major fiscal headwinds, and it really didn't matter because we had such a big capex boom in the AI build-out last year. This year was driven a lot by stimulus," he told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe."

"If you have an environment where you have tighter fiscal policy — the Fed is raising rates right now — and you have a slowdown in the AI capex build out, you're probably going to reach a policy mistake at some point in mid-2027," he said.

"That's what happened in 2000 during the Y2K tech boom, the Fed was raising rates, we had higher oil prices, and they just went too far, and eventually it had a recession. Our forecast is not for a recession in the United States. Our forecast is that we'll have a mid-cycle slowdown in 2027."

#2972. Coinbase net income surges 66% in third quarter, benefiting from bitcoin investment gains

Coinbase Global earned third-quarter adjusted net income of $719 million, up 66% from $433 million a year ago, largely due to a recovery in the cryptocurrency market.

Coinbase said it earned $2.51 a share in the latest quarter, although it wasn't immediately clear if that compared with the 6-cent-per-share profit that Wall Street analysts had estimated. Revenue of $1.20 billion trailed the $1.233 billion expected by analysts polled by LSEG.

Net income at the San Francisco-based platform surged thanks to the value of its volatile cryptocurrency investment portfolio, which can swing wildly even when the composition of the underlying holdings are little changed.

The third quarter value of Coinbase's crypto investment portfolio climbed due to a resurgence in the digital asset market. The price of bitcoin — often used as a gauge for all crypto assets — rose 39% in the three-month period ended Sept. 30 and the flagship cryptocurrency recently changed hands at roughly $80,809.

In the third quarter, Coinbase deepened its push into non-crypto products and services, including artificial intelligence integrations for traders, tokenized real-world assets and prediction markets.

In July, VP of product Max Branzburg told CNBC that Coinbase would debut an "everything exchange" spotlighting tokenized stocks and other real-world assets, derivatives trading, prediction markets and early-stage token sales. The trading platform also introduced Coinbase for Agents in early October, supporting users that want to use ChatGPT, Muse or other AI agents to manage their portfolios or research and trade various assets.

Coinbase also expanded its prediction market offerings, making a wider range of event contracts available to users.

In the third quarter, prediction markets revenue grew to more than $200 million on an annual basis, while the number of traders on those markets increased 60% compared with the second quarter.

#2971. AI crush is powering one stock to sky-high valuations

The 2026 midterm elections have profoundly altered the national regulatory environment, most notably with Democrats securing control of both chambers of the U.S. Congress and notching big victories in traditionally business-friendly states like Texas and South Carolina.

Over the past two decades, Southern states like Florida and Texas have attracted massive corporate migration from blue states. With less regulation and lower taxes. Those same benefits also attracted many people seeking lower taxes, lower housing costs, and better economic opportunities. With this political shift, relocated companies may now face a landscape increasingly focused on energy and building constraints, and tighter legislative oversight.

Information technology, semiconductors, and traditional energy sectors experienced immediate headwinds following the election. Companies at the forefront of the AI boom, such as CoreWeave, Tesla, and SpaceX, saw share prices slide as markets priced in tighter Democratic regulatory oversight and potential restrictions on AI deployment. Senator Bernie Sanders, for example, reposted his efforts to advance a data center moratorium proposal he co-authored with New York Congresswoman and socialist Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez.

The market is suddenly realizing that, between shifts in Washington and in states like Texas, the fastest-growing area of the U.S. economy might grind to a halt. Over half of the U.S. hyperscale data center pipeline could now face heightened regulatory friction. To build new facilities, even assuming they get permitted at all, developers may face tax increases, strict "bring your own power" mandates, or new air-permit delays for on-site gas plants. As all-in construction costs rise and timelines extend, immediate demand for advanced chips snowballs down the hardware supply chain.

Traditional oil and gas majors sank, while solar and green energy stocks surged, driven by expectations that a Democratic Congress will resume pushing subsidies for them. Also, the ongoing U.S.-Israel war with Iran has driven crude prices sharply higher throughout the year. With Democrats taking control of Congress, President Trump faces a legislative body that can severely constrain his military options and push back on defense budgets. Tehran almost certainly recognized that a politically weakened Trump administration would have a greater incentive to seek a negotiated deal, something Secretary of State Marco Rubio has now indicated is preferable to any escalating kinetic options. Consequently, crude prices plunged, with Brent dropping below $95 per barrel.

For similar reasons, defense contractors took market hits due to anticipated congressional resistance to the $1.5 trillion Pentagon budget. Although healthcare - as measured by XLV at least came in today, it remains above pre-election levels, along with grocery stocks such as Walmart, Costco, Kroger, and Dollar General, on expectations that a Democratic Congress might boost federal spending in those areas.

This may help explain why Costco was one of the relatively few stocks to close higher on Thursday, and why it also ranked as the 15th most active single-stock option (in notional terms), ahead of names like JP Morgan, Marvell, Oracle, Eli Lilly, and Dell.

At roughly 46x forward earnings, Costco (a perennial on the "Holly Index (MELLOY, CAN WE LINK TO THIS") isn't cheap, but then it is never cheap. The average forward multiple is actually a few turns higher at over 49x. So what were folks trading? Mostly short-dated call options. One possible reason? Costco isn't just trading at a relatively high multiple; as usual, it is also simply a high-priced stock in dollar terms, at over $1,065 as of Thursday's close. A "round lot" (100 shares) would therefore cost over $100,000 to initiate. Like many things packaged at Costco, that might be a little more than some buyers need (or want) to spend.

Alternatively, call options let investors gain exposure to 100 shares for far less than buying the shares. Buying the January 1060 calls, for example, would cost about $53.45, or $5,345 for upside participation on 100 shares of stock above the $1,060 strike price. That may seem more palatable, but a keen observer might notice that prior highs occasionally present some technical resistance, and Costco topped out at about $1,090 back in mid-May. By selling a call at or near that level, such as the January 1090s at 39.65, one could reduce the cost of the trade even further, expecting a pause before new highs. This would reduce the cost of the trade to $13.80, or just under 1.3% of the current stock price.

I won't pretend $1,380 is a small outlay for a one-lot call spread on a much-loved stock trading at 46x forward earnings. Nor is $520 for 8 6-ounce A5 Wagyu filet mignons (a Costco online only deal available now). But relatively, both the steaks at Costco and the options on Costco are very fairly priced. But it does provide participation in 100 shares of Costco between the strikes, for only 30% more than the cost of a single share of the underlying stock.

#2970. Rapid Recap: Raptors 121, Bucks 107

In a really disappointing opening NBA Cup game, the Milwaukee Bucks lost to the injury-ravaged Toronto Raptors 121-107. The only real positive to come out of the night was Brayden Burries' breakout game (as I predicted!): he scored a career-high 21 points on 8/14 shooting, plus 11 rebounds (five offensive) and six assists. For the Raptors, second-year man Alijah Martin went off for a career-high 27 points on 6/7 from deep.

The Bucks got off to a blinding start, leading 15-4 after five minutes largely thanks to Brayden Burries, who was electric as a rebounder to kick this one off; Burries had a maniacal five rebounds in the first four minutes, two of which came on the offensive glass. Unfortunately, Myles Turner picked up two quick fouls, which meant early minutes for Kel'el Ware; and while Ware did some good things, the Raptors seemed perfectly content leaving him open from three, and the Bucks' offence stalled when he kept missing. Before long, it was a one-possession game after Alijah Martin nailed three spot-up triples in short order. Thankfully, Ryan Rollins scored five points in the final two minutes to put the Bucks up 29-24 after one.

Burries opened the second with a baseline hammer, followed by a Rollins and-one to push Milwaukee's lead out to eight. The Bucks knuckled down on defence for the next few minutes, and a Burries mid-range jumper forced the Raptors to call a timeout as the home side led by double digits about halfway through the period. After going scoreless in the first quarter for the third straight game, Tyler Herro finally began to heat up in the second, nailing a step-back jumper against the waning shot clock and making some great interior passes. Burries was fantastic, nailing his first three to reach a career-high 15 points (on 7/10 shooting) with just over two minutes left in the first half! Much to Milwaukee's dismay, though, Toronto closed on a 6-0 run to cut the deficit to single digits, 60-51, at the major break.

The Bucks began the third lethargic, unable to generate good shots as the Raptors opened on an 11-4 run. Milwaukee was getting by on ticky-tack foul calls, while Toronto was running smooth offence. The visitors took their first lead at the six-minute mark after another Martin triple, but Herro and Jaquez responded with threes of their own to take it back; a transition lob to Nate Ament a few plays later capped Milwaukee's 10-2 run and forced the Raptors timeout. Of course, the visitors responded in a flash with a 6-0 spurt as Jenkins called a timeout. Both teams mostly held serve from there on out, with a late AJ Green bomb bringing the Bucks to within two, 89-87, after three quarters.

The Bucks began the fourth like the third: unfocused and lethargic. With Herro taking a break, Rollins assumed point guard duties and did not do a good job, as he threw a reckless whip pass to Turner in the middle of the floor; the Raptors then went down the other end to grab their own rebound and find Martin for his fifth three of the night to add to his career high—really disappointing stuff. Jenkins had to sub Herro back in much earlier than (I assume) he wanted to, as the Bucks went down seven, but thankfully, AJ Green kept them in it with timely threes. Milwaukee made a few runs but could not execute (on either end) when they needed to, losing in ugly fashion to a depleted Raptors outfit.

Before AJ Green's three at the buzzer, the Bucks had scored just 17 points in the fourth. The physical Raptors defence had completely strangled them.

Myles Turner would beat out pillows, butter, and kittens for the softest things on this earth.

#2969. Is Brandon Aiyuk returning to 49ers? Latest on NFL's greatest soap opera

The saga between the San Francisco 49ers and wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk has been playing out for more than a year. The last 24 hours has been something out of a soap opera.

The latest turn saw Aiyuk show up to the 49ers practice facility with his recently hired agent, Drew Rosenhaus, for a meeting with the Niners' front office at Levi's Stadium in Santa Clara, California, on Friday, Nov. 6.

Aiyuk brought Rosenhaus into the fold in an attempt to be reinstated to the NFL for a potential comeback. Both addressed reporters before meeting with the Niners' front office.

"Obviously Brandon is here to meet with the team. Fortunately, he's feeling good physically. He's healthy. He's ready to get back to work," Rosenhaus told reporters. "Don't really have any comment about where he's going to play. We'll keep that between us and the Niners, but obviously we're here with all the right intentions."

Aiyuk hasn't played since Week 7 of the 2024 season, when he was sidelined with an MCL and ACL injury that required surgery. He missed rehab sessions and the entire 2025 season. A year later, he says he's fully healthy and "100%" ready to get back to playing football.

"I'm feeling great, feeling healthy in my recovery process, in my rehab process," Aiyuk said. "(I'm) back to 100% and looking to get reinstated into the National Football League."

When asked if he's hoping to play for the Niners again, Aiyuk responded by saying that his hope is "to get reinstated into the National Football League."

The scheduled meeting between Aiyuk and the Niners happened, but not much information came out of it.

After the meeting, Rosenhaus was even more brief than he was walking into it.

"To be continued," Rosenhaus said. "We don't have any further statements right now. But when we do we'll let you know."

As far as who was a part of the meeting with Aiyuk and Rosenhaus, Aiyuk said "we met with the bosses, man. Big bosses."

At this point it appears the relationship he had with the team, head coach Kyle Shanahan and teammates has seemingly dissolved. After practice, Shanahan seemed less than enthused to discuss Aiyuk after saying he has no role in conversations surrounding the wide receiver.

"No clue. Your guess is as good as mine," Shanahan said about what's going to happen with Aiyuk. He added that he could "care less" about what happens.

"It's been over for our football team for a long time, so I don't hear much except questions about him today. But, it's not too big of a deal for me to answer," Shanahan said.

Multiple players, including Fred Warner, Christian McCaffrey and George Kittle, didn't comment in detail on Aiyuk's situation.

"It's not my place, really," 49ers defensive lineman Nick Bosa said. "I'll dap him up. No hard feelings between me and him."

Shanahan told reporters that he sees the Aiyuk situation as "zero to do with (the) football team".

"Anything that has zero to do with our football team, especially during the season, I will give zero thoughts to," Shanahan said.

Before Aiyuk can return to the 49ers, he may have to pay his dues − literally. According to reports, the wide receiver owes San Francisco nearly $13 million in bonus forfeiture.

Aiyuk was the 25th overall selection in the 2020 NFL Draft out of Arizona State. He's spent his whole career with San Francisco, where he's tallied 294 catches, 4,305 yards and 25 touchdowns in 69 games with the Niners.

Aiyuk was born in Rocklin, California, about 20 miles outside of Sacramento. The 28-year-old was raised in Reno, Nevada, where he starred at Robert McQueen High School. He returned to Rocklin to play football at Sierra College, a junior college, before transferring to Arizona State.

#2968. Top House Oversight Democrat warns Trump family to 'lawyer up'

Rep. Robert Garcia, the California Democrat poised to lead the powerful House Oversight Committee, doubled down on his warning to President Donald Trump and his allies Friday, releasing a video promising sweeping investigations into the administration.

"Oversight Democrats are ready to take on the crooks, the con men and the Trump crime family. Lawyer up," the California Democrat wrote on X.

"The American people have spoken, and they are tired of the unprecedented level of corruption coming out of this White House and under President Trump," Garcia said in the video, which ends with the words "LAWYER UP" splashed across the screen.

The warning comes three days after Democrats' sweeping midterm victories handed them control of the House. When the new Congress convenes in January, Democrats will gain control of the committee's subpoena power, allowing them to compel testimony and obtain documents in investigations that could target Trump, his family and his allies.

Garcia said the committee would investigate what he described as potential corruption involving the Trump family's business dealings, cryptocurrency ventures and Pentagon contracts, as well as alleged abuses by Immigration and Customs Enforcement. He also vowed to scrutinize individuals he said were connected to convicted sex offender Jeffrey Epstein.

"Oversight Democrats are absolutely going to ensure that we investigate every single one of these instances," Garcia said. "Now let's get to work."

Garcia's approach may conflict with what some advisors are telling Democrats — to go after corporate interests rather than Trump and his family.

Garcia, currently the committee's top Democrat, formally launched his bid to become chairman Thursday. He is widely expected to secure the position.

The White House has dismissed Democrats' planned investigations as politically motivated, setting up a potentially contentious showdown between the Trump administration and the incoming Democratic congressional majority.

The White House did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment.

I know it worked last time, but fall behind by double figures almost immediately should not have remained the Sixers' default strategy when facing the Cleveland Cavaliers. #2966. $6.3 billion Binance hack is largest crypto theft on record

Binance said Friday that a security incident impacted about $6.3 billion in client assets, with around $3 billion frozen on-chain, marking the largest crypto hack on record.

The company later added it began recovering assets impacted by the breach after pledging to cover losses using its Secure Asset Fund for Users, which the company created in 2018 as a protection for clients in the event of a hack.

It wasn't clear how the attack took place. The world's largest crypto exchange initially said the hack may involve "a compromised DevOps engineer's laptop, which allowed an attacker to gain access to a service container and submit unauthorized withdrawal transactions." However, Co-CEO Richard Teng later said the attack involved a more complex process rather than the misuse of a single engineer's permissions.

The FBI said Friday it was aware of the incident and was in touch with "relevant public and private partners to understand the scope of the activity, evaluate ​potential impacts, ​and ⁠coordinate appropriate investigative steps."

It wasn't clear either who was responsible for the breach. But TRM Labs, a blockchain intelligence and AI investigations company, pointed to North Korean hackers as the potential culprits. "Several features of this attack match earlier North Korea-linked heists, including the Bybit hack and the KelpDAO hack in April 2026: small Tornado Cash withdrawals funding an operator wallet, staging that began well before the theft, and signatures obtained in advance then executed in one burst," the company said in a post.

Blockchain data platform Chainalysis also said this appears "be the work of North Korean cybercrime group TraderTraitor. This group has repeatedly targeted the cryptocurrency industry, exchanges, DeFi protocols, and investors, to raise funds for the regime. We base our intelligence assessment on on-chain activity observed in the hours following the hack."

Binance temporarily suspended withdrawals while it overhauls its withdrawal security systems and has revoked the affected address authorizations, Teng said. The exchange is also working with law enforcement and blockchain intelligence firms to trace and recover the stolen funds, while deposits remain unaffected.

Binance co-founder Yi He said deposits remained unaffected and continued to operate normally, while the exchange was working on enhancements to its withdrawal service and related address controls. It expects to begin resuming withdrawal services within the next 48 hours.

"We have found no evidence of any wallet private key leak. We will share a detailed technical post-mortem once the investigation is complete and the facts are fully verified," Binance said.

The announcement followed moves by several crypto firms, including Circle Internet Group, Tether and Ripple, to freeze funds tied to the hack.

Exploits are not uncommon in the crypto space. In 2025, Bybit suffered roughly $1.5 billion in losses due to a hack. More recently, Bitget lost more than $350 million worth of digital assets in a cyber theft incident. Over the past several years, bad actors have also drained billions of dollars in crypto from decentralized exchanges.

This week's breach "proves our exact point in proposing regulations for crypto markets earlier this month," Michael Selig, chairman of the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, said Friday in a CNBC "Squawk on the Street" interview. The U.S. derivatives regulator on Oct. 5 proposed a framework that would effectively put crypto exchanges under federal government oversight — after Congress failed to approve legislation to regulate the asset.

"The real issue here is that these are unregulated offshore exchanges, and they don't have the same sorts of safeguards that we have in our financial markets here in the United States. We want to offer a federal option to these exchanges to come and register with the CFTC, and they would be subject to the same sorts of cyber security safeguards that we have in our markets today," he said.

Bitcoin fell more than 4% to $77,187.60 on Friday as the hack weighed on crypto markets.

Circle CEO Jeremy Allaire said Thursday evening in a social media post that the company had frozen more than 800 million in USD Coin associated with the incident. USDT stablecoin issuer Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino also said in on X that his firm had frozen more than 1.5 billion of its stablecoin that may have been related to a potential breach.

Jack McDonald, Ripple's senior vice president of stablecoins, said it was actively freezing affected funds held in RLUSD, its U.S. dollar-backed stablecoin.

#2965. OpenAI, Arm and Qualcomm executives react to post-midterm AI sell-off

As a post-midterm rally Wednesday turned into a steep sell-off Thursday, tech executives sought to look through the notion that potential data center constraints could be catastrophic for their businesses.

In response to a Democratic sweep of Congress as well as a sizeable number of state governorships and legislatures in the midterm election Tuesday night, stocks initially rallied Wednesday, posting their best day in more than 18 months as President Trump accepted the blowout results with little fanfare.

The tide quickly turned Thursday, however, with stocks selling off sharply as investors feared a potential slowdown in the data center build-out after wins by candidates in favor of data center regulation in states like Texas and the possibility that the artificial intelligence trade may be past its peak. Tech led the sell-off, with the "Magnificent Seven" losing over $1 trillion in aggregate market cap.

Stocks recovered some of their losses Friday.

Here's how tech executives reacted to the midterms and sell-off threat:

Haas characterized the postelection fear as "very, very short term thinking" and not likely to be a major threat to Arm's business in the long term, but said the U.S. must keep investing in the data center build-out before the rest of the world catches up.

"If the U.S. hesitates in any way, shape, or form in terms of data center build-out, the rest of the world is ready and willing. There is just huge demand for this capacity," Haas told CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" Thursday.

Friar told CNBC's Sara Eisen Friday that OpenAI will continue investing in data center infrastructure after voters resoundly rejected data centers in their backyards during the midterm election. The company will also focus on community concerns, including electricity. "We will never raise your electricity bill," Friar said."When I think about the midterms, I need to keep thinking about how do we keep investing to meet that customer demand. It starts early with having built relationships with all of the hyperscalers, and it will continue to mean that we raise capital," Friar said.

"If the midterm means more focus on data centers and communities, we're going to keep doing the work to get into those communities and to build trust with them."

Amon said the anxiety about the future of the data center build-out doesn't pose a significant threat to the chipmaker's business, as data center infrastructure is just one segment. Qualcomm slid 9.49% in Thursday's sell-off before recovering some losses Friday.

"The whole market is down, but I think the Qualcomm story is a little bit different," Amon told CNBC's Jon Fortt Thursday. Unlike traditional PC and data center chipmakers, Qualcomm has historically focused on low-power consumption computing. "The story of Qualcomm is what we have done outside that on the data center specific question."

Levie told "Squawk on the Street" Thursday that productivity gains meant AI "is clearly going in one direction," but said a tendency for industry messaging to coalesce around "doomerism" can exacerbate the public perception problems AI companies face.

"I think it's a little bit easier to rotate it toward maybe the more risk-oriented messages, and that's something that our industry needs to work on clearly. But ultimately, obviously, the consumer needs to see the direct benefit, and that's something that will just take time," said Box's Levie.

Risher said the dour market sentiment wasn't concerning for Lyft's business, as consumer sentiment and bookings remained strong. "I wouldn't get too distracted about the short term stuff of what's going on in the market. If I look at the consumer, for example, we just had the biggest Halloween we've ever had," Risher told CNBC's "Squawk Box" Friday.

#2964. Lions have new rallying cry after Jack Campbell’s terrifying injury

The Lions have a new mantra as they prepare to play without injured linebacker Jack Campbell: "Play like 46."

The message has become a centerpiece throughout the organization following the worrisome neck injury Campbell suffered during Sunday's 19-15 victory over the Vikings.

Campbell, 26, was carted off the field after attempting to make a tackle on standout Vikings tight end T.J. Hockenson during the second half.

Following the collision, Campbell remained motionless on the turf before being transported to a hospital.

Jack Campbell is tended to by trainers after suffering a neck injury during the Lions' Week 8 win over the Packers on Nov. 1, 2026, at Ford Field in Detroit. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Defensive coordinator Kelvin Sheppard said Thursday the team adopted its new motto, "Play like 46," following an emotional meeting Wednesday.

"So, the theme has been let's prepare and play like 46," Sheppard told reporters. "Every day I wake up, 'What would 46 want me to do today? Would he want us to sulk and feel sorry?' I can guarantee y'all know Jack. Absolutely not.

"He'll be pissed off if he found out guys are crying and guys are like, about him," he added. That's just his makeup and that's what makes him the person that he is. So, that's what we're doing. And everything we do, every meeting I told the unit, every walkthrough, every play, you all know how Jack prepares. You all know how he plays. The way you honor him is to wipe those tears from your eyes and make sure we prepare and play like him. So, that's the theme moving forward to this week."

The Lions received encouraging news Wednesday when Campbell released a statement regarding his recovery.

"I'm incredibly encouraged by the progress I've made in such a short period of time," he said, via the Lions. "I have full movement in my lower body, and I'm continuing to make progress with movement in my upper body. I know there's still work ahead, but I remain very optimistic. I'm taking things one day at a time, staying positive, and focusing on getting better."

Jack Campbell is carted off the field after suffering a neck injury during the Lions' Week 8 win over the Packers. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

Campbell remains hospitalized, with no timetable announced for his return to football activities.

The 26-year-old was the 18th overall pick in the 2023 NFL Draft and has been a starter since his rookie campaign.

Campbell was a Pro Bowler and a first-team All-Pro for the first time in 2025 for the first time in his career after recording five sacks, three forced fumbles and 176 tackles.

#2963. Raptors secure first NBA Cup win vs Bucks

The Toronto Raptors secured their first NBA Cup victory of the season, defeating the Milwaukee Bucks 121-107 at Fiserv Forum.

Raptors guard Alijah Martin was the standout performer, recording 27 points off the bench and providing the spark Toronto needed to secure the win.

The Raptors were without Scottie Barnes and Immanuel Quickley, who were both sidelined with injuries. In their absence, Jamal Shead and Collin Murray-Boyles were called upon to step up, joining Ja'Kobe Walter, RJ Barrett and Jakob Poeltl in the starting line-up.

The Bucks got off to a strong start, with Jaime Jaquez Jr. opening the scoring with a lay-up and a three-pointer. Milwaukee also found success on the offensive glass, building an early 7-0 run before Murray-Boyles ended it with a dunk off a nice pass from Shead.

Toronto struggled to find its rhythm offensively, continuing a trend seen throughout much of the season. Despite creating quality scoring opportunities, the Raptors had difficulty converting their looks, while Milwaukee continued to capitalize on second-chance opportunities.

The Bucks extended their advantage with a 10-0 run following a Raptors timeout midway through the opening quarter. However, Toronto responded late in the frame, with Murray-Boyles, Martin and Jamison Battle helping spark a 17-5 run to cut the deficit to just two points.

Martin was particularly impressive, knocking down two three-pointers and converting both of his free throws. He finished the opening quarter with a career-high 14 points, showcasing his scoring ability from beyond the arc.

Despite Milwaukee's strong start, Toronto battled back to close the gap, trailing 29-24 heading into the second quarter.

Milwaukee carried its momentum into the second quarter, with Brayden Burries and Ryan Rollins quickly finding the basket to extend the Bucks' lead to eight.

The two teams continued to trade baskets midway through the frame, with Shead and Murray-Boyles connecting for Toronto. Shead was particularly effective as a playmaker, recording seven assists in the quarter.

The Bucks began to pull away once again, led by a strong performance from Burries, who contributed a tough lay-up and a three-pointer.

He had 15 points and seven rebounds as Milwaukee extended its advantage to 12. A subsequent 7-0 run, capped by a basket from Kyle Kuzma, pushed the lead to 14 and prompted Raptors head coach Darko Rajaković to call a timeout.

Toronto responded with a late push, highlighted by a three-pointer from Martin and a tough and-one finish from Barrett. Shead continued to create opportunities for his teammates, recording his 10th assist of the half. Despite the late surge, Milwaukee maintained its advantage, taking a 60-51 lead into half-time.

Toronto came out of the half with a renewed sense of urgency, quickly cutting into Milwaukee's lead. Shead recorded his 11th assist of the night, finding Poeltl, who kept the ball in play before finishing with a smooth finger roll.

A subsequent steal led to a Walter dunk, bringing the Raptors within five. Shead continued to find Poeltl, setting him up for two more baskets as Toronto pulled within one.

Martin then gave Toronto its first lead of the night with a three-pointer midway through the quarter. However, Milwaukee responded with back-to-back three-pointers from Tyler Herro and Jaquez Jr., followed by an alley-oop finish to extend its advantage. The Bucks put together a 10-2 run after briefly surrendering the lead.

Shead continued to lead Toronto's offence, recording his 13th assist with a pass over the defence to Collin Murray-Boyles. The rookie then found Martin for another basket to tie the game. Shead also recorded a double-double as the Raptors began to build a five-point advantage.

Murray-Boyles added a strong and-one finish, while Milwaukee continued to battle to stay within reach. Toronto's third-quarter response proved crucial, as the Raptors carried a narrow two-point lead into the final frame.

Toronto opened the final quarter with a jumper from Jaden Bradley, followed by another three-pointer from Martin to extend the Raptors' lead to seven, their largest of the night at that point.

Martin continued to deliver from beyond the arc, knocking down another big three to bring his total to 27 points.

The two teams continued to trade baskets, with Milwaukee answering nearly every Toronto scoring run. The Bucks refused to go away quietly, keeping the game competitive as both sides looked to gain control down the stretch.

However, Toronto began to pull away as its defence tightened and the offence found its rhythm. The Raptors built a 12-point advantage before Poeltl recorded a double-double late in the game, helping put the result beyond Milwaukee's reach. Barrett also contributed with key baskets as Toronto extended its lead to 15. The Raptors continued to push late due to the fact that point differentials mean a lot in this tournament.

Milwaukee could not find an answer in the closing minutes, allowing the Raptors to see out the victory.

It was far from the prettiest of wins, but that has been the Raptors' approach throughout much of the season: finding ways to get the job done, regardless of how they get there.

Slow starts offensively continue to be a concern, while Toronto also struggled at the free-throw line, an area that will need improvement moving forward.

Martin was one of the biggest positives, taking advantage of his opportunities and finishing with 27 points. He has also shown strong chemistry alongside Jamal Shead, who had an outstanding night with a double-double, recording 16 assists and 10 points. Shead continues to demonstrate the potential to become Toronto's starting point guard of the future.

Barrett also put together another impressive performance, finishing with 27 points. Poeltl was equally important, recording a double-double with 19 points and 11 rebounds. It has been a difficult season for Poeltl, but hopefully this performance gives him something to build on moving forward.

Murray-Boyles also looked more comfortable playing at forward rather than centre, which could be something worth exploring as the season progresses.

Toronto will look to carry this momentum into its next outing when it returns home to host the Memphis Grizzlies on Monday, November 9. Tip-off is scheduled for 7:30 p.m.

#2961. Eagles locker room rallying around rocket-armed QB Joe Milton

Eagles locker room rallying around rocket-armed QB Joe Milton  originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

If you happened to be in South Philadelphia on Wednesday afternoon and have supersonic hearing, you might have already known the Eagles have a new starting quarterback.

"He zips it. He zips it," receiver Darius Cooper said shaking his head. "You can hear it flying in the air."

The rest of the world found out the Eagles made the decision to bench Tanner McKee and go with Milton from ESPN less than 2 hours after head coach Nick Sirianni failed mention a thing during his Wednesday press conference. And neither Milton nor McKee were available to reporters after Wednesday's practice.

But despite some janky attempts at obfuscation from a couple of starting offensive linemen on Wednesday, the team knows who is starting against the Giants on Sunday afternoon. It's Milton.

Dallas Goedert said he and his teammates found out the news in a meeting minutes before the public heard.

"We were kind of hoping that it would stay under wraps," Goedert said. "It would be a competitive advantage and they wouldn't find out until Saturday. But it didn't take long for people to know."

Milton, 26, was as sixth-round pick out of Tennessee back in 2024 and has played in games with the Patriots and Cowboys, but this will be his first start. The Eagles just signed him off the Cowboys' practice squad on Oct. 16. It took him a week to get bumped up to the backup job and three weeks to become the starter.

After Jalen Hurts was lost for the season with blood clots in his lungs, the Eagles gave McKee three starts and the offense has been struggling. So now they're turning to Milton and hoping he provides a spark. We'll see if Milton can do that on Sunday, but he did seem to inject some juice into the locker room to kick off this week.

"He's a real good athlete. That stands out," Goedert said. "He's got a cannon of an arm. Today it seemed like he was really accurate, got through his reads good, made check downs when he needed. But it'll be exciting for him to go out there. I know he's excited to get on the field and play. Going to do whatever I can to help him succeed."

Milton is 6-foot-5, 236 pounds and he is elusive in the pocket. That elusiveness will help an offense that has been giving up too many pressures, especially against the blitz, all season.

But the thing everyone always brings up about Milton is his rocket of a right arm. That was no different after Wednesday's practice.

"He can spin it," tight end Eli Stowers said. "You feel it when he throws it to you and it hits your hands pretty quick. When he lets it go down the field, it's pretty good."

"It's kind of perfect for a pass catcher," Goedert said. "The balls that are in the air for a long time when you can think, those ones are the scariest ones. The ones that get on you and you just have to catch it, those are the easy ones. It seems like he's been putting the ball in a good spot and I'm excited to see what he does Sunday."

Goedert on Wednesday was surprised to find out that Sunday's game will be Milton's first-career NFL start. Goedert said he thought he read that Milton had already started six or seven games; that's not the case. Milton has been in the NFL for two seasons and he's played in five games and has thrown 53 passes — but he'll make his first start in a few days.

Despite that inexperience, Milton apparently doesn't lack for confidence. Goedert didn't even seem to question the thought that Milton had started games before.

"He's got a lot of swag to him. He's confident," veteran receiver Hollywood Brown said. "He's excited for this opportunity as well. That was his whole reason to come here, to get a chance like this. He's excited for it.

"It's a testament to the journey he's been on. He's probably been that confident his whole life. So for him, it seems natural. For all of us, we're rallying around it, trying to get a W."

Even though Milton has shown confidence, the Eagles are going to try to help him as much as possible on Sunday. Cooper said he wants to help give Milton tips on where their motions are coming from, Goedert said he wants to be a reliable outlet, Brown said he wants to be able to create after the catch. The Eagles are likely without DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks again, so any little thing will help.

Milton didn't sign with the Eagles until Oct. 16, but he immediately fit in. Stowers said that Milton has been "a light in the locker room" since his arrival and praised the quarterback for making an attempt to get to know all of his new teammates on a personal level. That goes a long way, especially now that Milton will lead them into a game on Sunday.

"I'm excited for his opportunity," Cooper said.

#2960. OpenAI will keep investing in AI data centers despite election results, CFO tells CNBC

OpenAI CFO Sarah Friar on Friday said the company will continue to invest in artificial intelligence infrastructure, even after voters strongly opposed data centers during Tuesday's midterm elections.

"When I think about the midterms, I need to keep thinking about how do we keep investing to meet that customer demand," Friar told CNBC's Sara Eisen in an interview at the Citi TMT Leadership Summit in Pebble Beach, California. "It starts early with having built relationships with all of the hyperscalers, and it will continue to mean that we raise capital."

Out of 41 local ballot measures across 10 states, voters backed restrictions or rejected pro-development measures in all but one, according to an analysis by Ballotpedia and additional results gathered by CNBC on Wednesday. Opposition to the facilities mounted swiftly over the summer as Americans grew worried about their potential to harm the environment, raise utility bills and contribute to job loss.

Investors have turned skeptical on the AI trade of late due to a combination of higher borrowing costs, concerns about soaring debt and public backlash toward the industry. The Nasdaq on Thursday suffered its steepest drop in 19 months, led by a plunge in shares of the chipmakers and neocloud companies most closely tied to the AI buildout. The stocks rebounded some on Friday.

OpenAI depends on data centers to train its AI models and serve customers, and Friar said she doesn't think the election results dramatically changed anything for the company. On Thursday, OpenAI published a policy blueprint and a series of commitments that it pledged to apply to company-owned and -operated data centers in the future.

"If the midterm means more focus on data centers and communities, we're going to keep doing the work to get into those communities and to build trust with them," Friar said.

OpenAI confidentially filed its prospectus with the Securities and Exchange Commission in June, and Friar said Friday that the company would continue to look into a 2027 debut. Anthropic, OpenAI's chief rival, is trying to go public first, with an aim to hit the Nasdaq in mid-November.

Anthropic's revenue in the first nine months of the year jumped roughly 1,200% to $33.2 billion, according to its prospectus. The company said it has entered into purchase commitments and lease agreements with an aggregate committed spend of about $895.4 billion, "substantially all of which is related to compute and infrastructure."

OpenAI is in talks to raise funding at a $1.25 billion pre-money valuation, Friar said Friday. She said "a good CFO always raises money when she can" and that the round is ongoing.

The company has closed an initial $11 billion portion of that funding round, CNBC confirmed on Friday, and it could raise up to an additional $9 billion in the coming weeks. Bloomberg was first to report the $11 billion portion.

"The demand for AI is real," Friar said. "I don't think anyone's arguing with that, or if they are, I think it's a losing argument."

#2959. 13 young Eagles worth watching in what's left of a lost season

13 young Eagles worth watching in what's left of a lost season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles aren't even at the halfway point yet.

There are still nine games to go in this miserable 2026 season. And now that the Eagles are sitting at 2-6 with a point differential of -65 it probably feels a little daunting to have to watch this team play nine more times.

Maybe some Eagles fans really will do something else on Sundays — not to mention Thanksgiving and Christmas Eve — but let's be honest: Most of you are still going to watch when the Eagles play.

So other than rooting for a good draft pick, how do you find enjoyment out of a lost season like this? The answer is pretty easy. You get to monitor the development of the young players on the team. And, hopefully, you'll be able to see their progress in the second half of this season.

With that in mind, here are 13 young Eagles who are worth watching the rest of the way:

QB Joe Milton: Maybe the Eagles really do just ride it out with backup quarterback Tanner McKee in place of Jalen Hurts. But there's some definite intrigue with Milton. The Eagles signed him off the Cowboys' practice squad on Oct. 16 and it didn't take long for Milton to supplant Cole Payton as the new backup. The 26-year-old Milton (6-5, 236) has played in just five career games but the former sixth-round pick has plenty of arm talent. His highlight reel from his 2023 season at Tennessee is littered with highlight plays. Milton has a big arm and can elude pressure. He just wasn't very consistent. But in this lost season, if McKee continues to struggle, it would be fun to see Milton get a chance.

QB Cole Payton: The Eagles on Sunday night made an interesting decision. They kept Cole Payton active over offensive lineman Jake Majors but Payton didn't play. My first thought was that Payton would have a role on special teams but he didn't. So perhaps there was an offensive role in mind for Payton that the Eagles didn't get to in their 27-10 loss — or maybe he was just an emergency RB option. Payton is a heck of an athlete and while he's developing as a QB behind the scenes, maybe there's a chance to get him on the field in some other roles.

RB Jaydon Blue: In the loss to the Commanders, Blue played just 8 snaps on offense and 7 snaps on special teams, despite Saquon Barkley being banged up and others missing. Blue 22, was a fifth-round pick out of Texas last year but played in just five games with the Cowboys as a rookie. Blue has an intriguing skillset. He ran a 4.38 at the Combine last year and also showed an ability as a receiving threat at Texas. He's a different kind of back and it would be fun to see him get some burn.

WR Makai Lemon: The first-round pick from USC got off to a slow start in his rookie season but has been getting better and better once the Eagles scrapped the portion of the playbook that had him going backwards. Over the last couple of weeks without DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks, Lemon has played more and has had his two biggest games of the season. Overall, Lemon has 31 catches on 45 targets for 251 yards and a touchdown. Among rookies, Lemon is sixth in receiving yards behind Carnell Tate, Denzel Boston, KC Concepcion, Germie Bernard and Antonio Williams. But Lemon has been coming on stronger recently.

WR Darius Cooper: The UDFA from Tarleton State is still just in his second NFL season. He made the roster again in 2026 and has 11 catches for 175 yards and 4 touchdowns this year. But he has caught just 45.8% of his targets, which is a very low number. Cooper hasn't been a star but he has made some big-time catches and should continue to get some run the rest of the way.

TE Eli Stowers: The second-round pick from Vanderbilt missed the first six games of the season on Injured Reserve with a quad injury. But Stowers made his NFL debut in Week 7 and then made his first NFL catches in Week 8. A couple of those catches came in garbage time but it was still good to see him finish with 3 for 53. When the Eagles drafted Stowers, we knew he was a developmental tight end. The best way to develop is to get him more playing time this season. Stowers is far from a finished product but he has the type of athleticism that's worth developing.

OL Willie Lampkin: The Eagles have had Landon Dickerson back in the starting lineup at left guard the last couple of weeks, but it'll be interesting to see if Dickerson remains with the team through the trade deadline. If not, maybe we see a bit more of Lampkin. The 5-foot-11 offensive lineman started a couple of games at left guard in between Cam Jurgens and Dickerson. Even if Lampkin can't get back on the field as a starter, the Eagles could find some unique ways to use him. He lined up as a fullback on the Saquon Barkley direct-snap touchdown on Sunday night.

OG Micah Morris: The sixth-round pick from Georgia has played just one snap all season so he probably won't get many chances to play this season. But if more injuries hit, Morris could get some chances. He's a really athletic rookie who is worth developing.

C Drew Kendall: The Eagles drafted Kendall out of Boston College in the fifth round last year and have been high on him for a while. We saw that this year when they got him into the starting lineup from Week 2. Kendall has now started five games at center this season and could be the Eagles' long-term fix at the position. The Eagles traded away his predecessor Cam Jurgens earlier this season, so if Kendall plays well the rest of the way, he could keep that job for a while.

OT Markel Bell: The third-round pick from Miami has had some ups and downs this season. Nick Sirianni seemed impressed by Bell's performance against the Cowboys, but Bell didn't have his best game against the Commanders. Lane Johnson started the first three games of the season before he abruptly retired and Bell has now started the last five games. According to PFF, Bell has given up 17 pressures and 5 sacks this season. He has had some good moments in the run game too. Johnson seemed really impressed by Bell in training camp; we'll see if Bell ends up being his long-term replacement.

DT TY Robinson: Robinson played 104 defensive snaps as a rookie fourth-round pick last year. Through eight games this season, Robinson has played just 52 snaps and is firmly behind Jalen Carter, Jordan Davis, Moro Ojomo and Byron Young on the depth chart. Robinson has played just 9% of the snaps, while Young has been on the field for 27%. Robinson had a solid training camp and preseason so it might make sense to start having him eat into Young's snaps.

LB Jihaad Campbell: This is a tricky one because Campbell began the season as a full-time starter next to Zack Baun and then the Eagles started up a rotation between Campbell and Jeremiah Trotter Jr. While it looks like Trotter might simply be the best option right now, Campbell was a first-round pick and has a ton of athletic ability. Campbell hasn't lived up to expectations but he's in Year 2 as a first-round pick. The only way for him to get better is to continue to play.

S Drew Mukuba: Mukuba is in a similar position as Campbell but there's no one like Trotter pushing him. The second-year, second-round pick from Texas has had a mostly disappointing season as a full-time starter in 2026. But he did have a good game against the Commanders with a forced fumble and an interception that was negated by a penalty. Through eight games, Mukuba has played 556 of 558 snaps. We might learn a lot about him over the final nine games of the season.

#2958. ‘Seattle is done’ – Seahawks fans think Sam Darnold may be heading back to the Vikings

The Seattle Seahawks' season is sitting in a precarious position heading into this weekend's game against the Arizona Cardinals, with a long list of injuries leaving a lot to be concerned about.

Having won the Super Bowl earlier this year, it seems as if Mike Macdonald's team is already at a make-or-break point in their season following Monday's heavy defeat to the Chicago Bears. Among a host of big names set to miss the game on Sunday, quarterback Sam Darnold has been ruled out with a quad injury.

The injury to the struggling QB appeared to come out of nowhere earlier in the week. Drew Lock has been named as the starter despite his own elbow issue. However, there is a bigger-picture scenario going on with some Seahawks fans, who feel as if Darnold's injury coincidentally ties in with the Minnesota Vikings' reported interest in a new QB.

Seattle signed Darnold following his impressive spell in Minnesota. The decision to allow Darnold to walk is one that many Vikings fans look back on with regret, considering he went on to lift the Super Bowl. Following Adam Schefter's comments on the Vikings' search for a QB, a lot of Seahawks fans feel as if Darnold's absence may be bigger than just an injury.

'The new injury report is a disaster. They will be lucky to win ten games. If Sam is healthy enough… it benefits both teams. Seattle is done this year,' said one fan on X.

'I actually thought the same thing this morning when I heard Schefter's comments. Minnesota would probably need to send two 1's and/or a player. Jordan Addison might be a good option who would immediately bolster the Seahawks receiving corps. Lock is a competent QB,' replied another.

What Seattle would be able to bring in for Darnold is another question altogether. He is, after all, a Super Bowl-winning quarterback, but his form this season has been nothing short of disastrous, having thrown nine interceptions across his last six games.

'Makes perfect sense to me. If we took on dead cap and say Taylor? Ojomo? Henry? We would still have more cap space next year than projected now?' one fan speculated.

'If the GM's team thinks so, I'll be for it. As long as the comp is fair,' another fan approved.

Seattle's offense has stalled this season and looks incredibly stale. Beyond Jaxon Smith-Njigba's brilliance, there has been very little to praise about what offensive coordinator Brian Fleury has been able to put together on that side of the ball in the Pacific Northwest.

'I jokingly said this on X to a Vikings fan. Real life would be insane,' added another Seahawks fan.

'Tons of buzz around the Vikings working hard to land a QB right now. Some of the, shall we call it "unusualness" around how the Seahawks announced the Darnold injury. One could connect some pretty intriguing dots – if they were so inclined,' said another.

#2957. Gael Monfils appointed captain of France's Davis Cup team following retirement

Gael Monfils has been appointed France's Davis Cup captain a month after calling time on his playing career.

The flamboyant Frenchman, known for entertaining both on and off court, quietly retired in October - appearing on the International Tennis Integrity Agency's inactive list without an explanation - amid the breakdown of his marriage with Ukrainian player Elina Svitolina.

Having initially planned to bid farewell to the sport with one last appearance at the Paris Masters, he was instead honoured with a ceremony in the French capital earlier this week, joined by fellow fan favourites Jo-Wilfried Tsonga, Gilles Simon, Richard Gasquet and Benoit Paire.

He now transitions into a courtside role after succeeding Paul-Henri Mathieu as captain for France's upcoming Davis Cup campaign, with qualifying in February.

"It is a great source of pride to become captain of the French national team," Monfils said. "This blue jersey represents so much to me. I want to build a real collective project and create the dynamic that will allow us to win titles together."

The French Tennis Federation (FFT) thanked Mathieu for his commitment in the role for the past three years, having guided France to the quarter-finals in 2025.

The 40-year-old represented France in eight editions of the Davis Cup during his 22-year professional career, winning 12 of his 15 singles matches.

Gael Monfils retired from tennis last month and was given a rousing send-off in Paris (Reuters)

He delivered one of his best performances in the 2014 final, beating Roger Federer in straight sets, although France would ultimately go on to lose the tie to Switzerland.

The former World No 6 was not in the ‌team when France won their 10th Davis ​Cup title in 2017 but is now keen to ⁠add to their trophy cabinet as captain, ⁠a non-playing role.

The FFT says Monfils will be assisted by the likes of compatriots Simon and Gasquet in the role.

"We are very happy to welcome Gaël as head coach of the French team," FFT president Gilles Moretton added.

"We are convinced that his infectious energy and enthusiasm will be valuable assets in advancing the ambitions of French tennis."
