#2907. CNBC's experts on whether the AI trade is ending and what investors should do next

Amid a powerful rout in tech stocks, investors told CNBC on Thursday that something may be fundamentally breaking in the AI trade following the big success of Democrats in Tuesday's midterm elections and the threat they try to slow the data center buildout.

Democrats are more aggressively anti-data center than Republicans, and Wall Street is quickly redoing its math what any restrictions they implement could mean for the related companies' future growth.

"Right now, investors are questioning that $1.2 trillion of capex that we forecast for next year," Peter Boockvar, chief investment officer at One Point BFG Wealth Partners, told CNBC. "Well, maybe that's not going to happen."

Boockvar believes this could be the start of the end of the AI trade as we know it.

Whether it's completely over or not, investors need to be reassessing how they're planning to play the AI trade going forward, the analysts said.

The Nasdaq Composite dropped 5% on Thursday and fell into correction territory, off by more than 11% from its record high set last month. The iShares Semiconductor ETF dropped 11% on Thursday, its biggest decline since during the Covid breakout in March 2020. The Global X Artificial Intelligence & Technology ETF (AIQ) is down 6% on the day and down 15% over the past month.

"There has been a huge change in the outlook for AI infrastructure buildout," Eric Johnston, head of equities at Cantor Fitzgerald, told CNBC. "The entire economy and the consumer and earnings growth are all levered to that buildout."

The selling Thursday spilled over to non-AI stocks with the Dow Jones Industrial average down more than 1,600 points.

Josh Brown pointed to the 4.8% yield for the 10-year Treasury yield, saying a compelling risk-free rate is causing investors to reconsider taking the risk in these volatile AI stocks.

"You don't need to be at 80% U.S. stocks and have 60% of that exposure be tech," Brown said.

Many investors believe the AI trade isn't over completely, investors just need to get more selective.

"The AI buildout just got harder," said Rob Sechan, Newedge Capital Group Founder & Managing Partner:

"Nobody voted to make compute less scarce," Sechan said, citing Microsoft's Azure cloud business growing 48% year over year in its most recent third quarter. That level of growth suggests its capacity constrained, not demand constrained, he said.

"When new capacity gets harder to build, existing capacity becomes more valuable," Sechan said.

Richard Clode, portfolio manager at Janus Henderson, noted a few bright spots in tech on Thursday despite the highly correlated downward movements in the big tech indices.

"Motorola Solutions had some good results," he said. "We're seeing a little bit of rotation into some of the … internet names...Sony reported some good results, and again they were up strongly overnight."

"So there are some brief pockets of respite from this, but obviously pretty painful across the board today," he said.

Some experts believe the selling was overdone and investors could start nibbling.

This "indiscriminate selling" is a "buying opportunity," said Larry Adam, Raymond James chief investment officer. If investors "take a longer term perspective, which is what we do, I think the valuations at today's levels will tend to prove to be a good buying opportunity," he said, urging investors to look at the fundamentals.

Victoria Greene, chief investment officer of G Squared Private Wealth, said she saw some strong supports around the S&P 500's 200-day moving average and was on the lookout for a market bounce following Thursday's rout.

"Typically, the average return is plus about 180 basis points the next day," she said. "The question we have to ask ourselves: Is this a pullback in an uptrend, which is what I believe? Or is this the end-all, be-all [and] the wheels are coming off and everybody just needs to buy T-bills? I don't think we're there yet."

#2906. Roob's Eagles Observations: Life without Jalen Hurts just isn't any fun

Roob's Eagles Observations: Life without Jalen Hurts just isn't any fun originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Mulling life without Jalen Hurts. Thoughts on Tanner McKee getting benched after just three games. And the absurdity of something that the Eagles have done for the first time since 1938.

We've never had to do 10 Random Eagles Observations during a six-game losing streak before, but here we are.

MISSING JALEN HURTS: I miss Jalen Hurts. And I hope people out there who never became Hurts supporters for whatever reason even after all the wins and and all the big plays and five straight playoff berths, two Super Bowl appearances, a championship and a parade two years ago miss him, too. Since 2021, when Hurts plays the Eagles win 70 percent of the time and when he doesn't play they win 30 percent of the time. Love him or hate him that 70 percent figure is pretty impressive. Patrick Mahomes is the only NFL quarterback to win more games than Hurts and win a Super Bowl since 2021. No, Hurts is not perfect. He's human and he makes mistakes and sometimes he even loses games – but just 25 of them since 2021. But, dang, he makes things fun and exciting and gives this team a heck of a good chance to win football games when he's on the field. He's special, and right now there's not a lot special about the Philadelphia Eagles. I don't know exactly what this season would have looked like if Hurts hadn't been sidelined. There still would have been issues with the defense and the running game and pass protection. But one thing I do know is that Hurts raises the level of everyone around him and when he's not playing the Eagles miss more than just his quarterback play. They miss his leadership, his accountability, his presence. There are a lot of unknowns with Hurts' medical condition. I just hope he's able to make a complete healthy recovery and first, that he can live a healthy life with his family but second, that he's able to return to play quarterback for the Eagles in 2027. Because it's just not nearly as much fun without him.

AT LEAST THERE'S ONE GUY PLAYING WELL: Makai Lemon is on pace for 65 catches, and the Eagles' record for rookie wide receivers is 67 by Jordan Matthews in 2014. Tight end Keith Jackson caught 81 passes as a rookie, but Lemon has 31 receptions and needs 37 in Eagles' the last nine games to break Matthews' record. That's just over four per game, and based on the growth we've seen from him over the last few weeks there's no doubt in my mind he'll get that record and finish somewhere in the 70s. Which is truly impressive considering he missed much of training camp with a hamstring and got off to a slow start once the season began, with just 10 catches for 65 yards in his first four games. He has 21-for-186 the last four games and is really looking comfortable out there, catching the ball smoothly, breaking tackles, picking up yards after the catch and blocking in the run game. At some point, DeVonta Smith will be back from his own hamstring injury, and Dontayvion Wicks will be back as well. But the Eagles can't let Lemon's snap count drop. He plays mainly in the slot – 66 percent of his snaps are inside so far this year – and Smith is outside 88 percent of the time and Wicks is outside 72 percent of the time so Lemon shouldn't be affected. But the kid has to play. Not a lot to be excited about with this miserable offense, but Lemon is the real deal, and he's only going to keep getting better.

A QUICK HOOK FOR TANNER #1: I didn't think Nick Sirianni would pull the plug on Tanner McKee this quickly, especially with the bye week coming up. That's normally when you make these sort of changes. Especially with a new guy who's still learning the offense. But at this point, why not? You're 2-6, you've lost six straight, you're certainly not going anywhere with McKee at QB, what do you have to lose? McKee has ranged from poor to mediocre in his three starts and just never got into any sort of rhythm as a passer. It's a tough evaluation because the o-line has been so bad, the running game has never really gotten going and he's been without DeVonta Smith and to a large degree Saquon Barkley. And Joe Milton will be facing the same handicaps as McKee did, but the hope is that he can rise up above all those handicaps with his athleticism and provide some juice to the NFL's worst offense. McKee has just been lifeless. Out of 38 quarterbacks who've thrown at least 50 passes this year, he's 33rd in passer rating, 37th in yards per attempt 37th in yards per completion and 33rd in success rate. His four completions of at least 20 yards the last three weeks are 24th-most in the league. One more than Daniel Jones. Maybe it would have gotten better, but I haven't seen any reason to believe that or expect that. McKee has just looked overmatched. It seems awfully early for Milton, but he can't be any worse. Right?

A QUICK HOOK FOR TANNER #2: McKee lost more games this year than Jalen Hurts lost in 2022 and as many as Jalen lost in 2024.

A QUICK HOOK FOR TANNER #3: If McKee doesn't start another game, he'll be the first Eagles quarterback to start at least three games in a season and lose them all since Ken O'Brien went 0-4 in 1993. The only other QB to start three or more games (not including 1987 strike replacement games) and lose them all was John Reaves, who was 0-7 in 1972. During the strike, replacement QB Scott Tinsley went 0-3.

HOW CAN NONE OF THEM HAVE A SACK? It's hard to believe Jalyx Hunt, Moro Ojomo, Jonathan Greenard and Jordan Davis have a combined half of a sack through nearly half the season. Over the last two years, those four combined for 35 sacks. Now they have a half. Like not even an integer. I don't understand how an entire defensive line can go into a slump at the same time. Jalen Carter has four sacks, but it feels like he's making a few impressive plays a game but without the consistent production the Eagles need from him. Carter still gets a lot of double teams and Ojomo is also getting doubled a lot, and when anybody up front gets doubled it should be an opportunity for someone else to take advantage of a good matchup, but other than with Nolan Smith, who has four sacks of his own, that just hasn't happened. Ten sacks in eight games is pitiful. The Eagles once sacked Troy AIkman 11 times in one game. In 1989 they had 36 sacks after eight games. Now they have 10. Their 2nd-fewest ever after nine games, one more than the 1993 team had. I expected the offense to have issues this year. I did not expect the defense, especially the defensive line, to underachieve like this.

THIS REALLY OUGHT TO BE IMPOSSIBLE: Maybe the most damning stat regarding the 2026 Eagles is that they've been outgained by an average of over 100 yards per game. How is that even possible? Through Week 8, the Eagles have allowed 2,938 yards and netted 2,122 yards for a difference of 816 yards – or 102 yards per game. That's what happens when you're ranked 32nd in the NFL in yards per game and 27th in yards allowed. This is the first time since 1971 – that was 55 years ago – the Eagles have been outgained by at least 100 yards per game through eight games. Also happened in 1937, 1938 and 1957. The 1930s were not good years for the Eagles. Neither is 2026. The Eagles have been outgained by at least 71 yards in each game during the six-game losing streak. They've been outgained by an average of 148 yards during these last six games, the most yards they've been outgained by in any six-game span since 1976. This is only the second time in franchise history they've been outgained by 70 or more yards in six games in a row. It also happened during the 2023 late-season collapse. Fun season.

WHAT WAS THE QB PLAN? I feel like the Eagles have mis-managed the quarterback position since camp began, starting with the wonky competition between Andy Dalton and Tanner McKee during training camp, when neither one got enough reps to be ready for the season. And then going with Dalton as No. 2 but demoting him after just one pass? And alienating him so much that he opted to sign with the Falcons instead of returning here after Jalen Hurts was sidelined for the season? Then a quick hook for McKee to go with a QB who was drafted in the sixth round, just got here 3 ½ weeks ago and has never started an NFL game? Jalen wasn't playing great when his season ended, McKee regressed, Dalton threw the worst pass of the season and Cole Payton isn't ready. Maybe Milton will play well Sunday, but I don't know how you start training camp with four quarterbacks on the roster and go into a Week 10 home game against a division rival with someone else as your starter.

CAN THEY MAKE IT FOUR IN A ROW? With Joe Milton making his first NFL start Sunday, it's interesting to note that the last three Eagles quarterbacks to make their first NFL start all won. That's Carson Wentz in the 2016 opener against the Browns, Jalen Hurts against the Saints in Week 14 of the 2020 season after replacing Wentz and Tanner McKee in the meaningless year-end game against the Giants in 2024. The last Eagles QBs to lose their first career start are Kevin Kolb in 2009 against the Saints and Nick Foles in 2012 in Washington. Before that, Donovan McNabb won his first start in 1999 after replacing Doug Pederson, at the Vet vs. Washington, and A.J. Feeley won his first start late in 2002 against the Cards at the Vet. So Eagles QBs making their first career start are 5-2 in their last seven games. Before that? Eagles QBs making their first start were 3-9-1.

THEY REALLY NEED A HEALTHY SAQUON: With Dameon Pearce averaging 2.6 yards per carry, Tank Bigsby at 3.3, Will Shipley 2.9 and Elijah Mitchell 3.2, the Eagles could have four running backs average less than 3.4 yards per carry in the same season for the first time since 1991, when James Joseph averaged 3.3, Heath Sherman 2.6, Thomas Sanders 2.3 and Robert Drummond 2.3. Just another thing to look forward to over these last nine weeks.

HOW MANY GAMES ARE THEY GOING TO WIN? As the Eagles head into the second half of a lost season, it's interesting to look back at the biggest drops in wins in franchise history from one season to the next. The Eagles won 11 games last year and I'm not sure how many more they'll win, but it won't be a lot. But what's crazy is that the Eagles haven't had a dropoff of more than four wins in more than 20 years. They won 13 games in the 2004 Super Bowl season, then went 6-10 in 2005. Not including the 1982 strike-shortened season, the last time before that they won four fewer games from one season to the next was 1971 (6-7-1) to 1972 (2-11-1). The seven-game dropoff from 2004 through 2005 matches the largest in franchise history. They also won seven fewer games from 1961 to 1962 (10-4, 3-10-1). They'll need to find a way to win three more games to avoid tying the largest single-season dropoff in wins in the franchise's 94-year history, and I'm not sure they can.

FIRST TIME SINCE 1938? And this is just astounding: The Eagles have been out-rushed by 350 yards and out-passed by 466 yards so far this year. This is the first time in 88 years they've been outgained by at least 350 yards both on the ground and in the air eight games into a season. It also happened in 1937 (649 rushing, 360 passing) and 1938 (517 rushing, 538 passing). You didn't want to be an Eagles fan in the 1930s.

#2905. Trump says U.S. carried out ‘minor strikes’ on Iran as missiles hit Qeshm Island

President Donald Trump told reporters Friday that the U.S. carried out "minor strikes" on Iran, amid reports that two missiles struck Qeshm Island near the Strait of Hormuz.

The missiles struck the island on Friday, Iranian news agency Tasnim reported, according to Reuters. Tasnim said there was no official information on damage, Reuters added.

The island lies off Iran's southern coast, near the Strait of Hormuz, the vital artery for oil and gas that has become a flashpoint in the war.

Speaking to reporters Friday evening after landing in West Palm Beach, Florida, Trump didn't comment specifically on Qeshm Island, saying: "I don't want to talk about it."

But he added: "We do minor strikes, but they knock out. They built a little bit of an anti-aircraft device, and we knocked it out in about 15 minutes, maybe five." He did not explicitly confirm that he was referring to the reported strikes on Qeshm.

The strikes are the first since Tuesday's midterm elections, after analysts and regional officials had debated whether the vote would lead Trump toward further escalation or renewed diplomacy.

The U.S. struck the same island Monday, in what a U.S. official told MS Now was a "defensive measure."

Trump also told reporters Friday that Iran wants "to make a deal very badly. They have 300% inflation. Their navy's gone. Their airport, everything's gone, and they want to make a deal."

The International Monetary Fund forecast that Iran's consumer prices would rise by an average of 78.6% in 2026, in a report published Oct. 13.

When asked if he wanted to make a deal "really soon," Trump said: "Well, we have nobody getting hurt, nobody getting killed.

"You know, this is a war that we may have been there for seven months, but we probably fought for three or four weeks, really fought. And again, you look at World War One; that's a war. World War Two; that's a war."

Iran has said it wants more than "paper commitments" from the U.S. before entering a new phase of diplomatic talks.

"We must see practical measures by the Americans," Ali Bagheri Kani, deputy secretary of Iran's Supreme National Security Council, told state broadcaster IRIB on Friday, according to Press TV.

#2904. San Antonio vs. Sacramento, Final Score: Spurs’ talent outweighs sloppy moments for 7th straight win, 124-109

The San Antonio Spurs got their first taste of Emirates Cup play on their successful trip to the home of the struggling Sacramento Kings. Despite the Kings being overmatched in talent, they gave a solid effort looking for their second win that made the Spurs sweat at times.

Things started getting ugly as the visitors kept plugging away on the glass and raised their lead to 16 points in the non-Victor Wembanyama minutes thanks in part to their 3-point shooting, ball security and Keldon Johnson's hustle. They went on to have their second-highest scoring first half of the season (76), but had trouble getting back into their flow in the third quarter.

Their large cushion was soon sliced nearly in half until Wemby led the charge that got them back on track. There were some defensive lapses in the following period, but the Spurs quickly atoned for them with a 3-pointer or a move inside.

They were playing like they thought it would be easy early, allowing open 3-point attempts and transition baskets, but the flow of the game started changing when the Spurs started consistently getting to the line. The trickle effect was forcing the Kings to slow down offensively and take more bad shots, which put the Spurs in the transition.

Wembanyama was merciless, scoring 30 points on 55.6 shooting at short and long range, while adding 14 rebounds and two blocks. He took his final rest with eight minutes left as the Spurs were up 22 points.

Darius Acuff Jr. has been the league's highest-scoring rookie, yet he can be a bit of a chucker, so the Spurs defended him mostly without help and in drop coverage on screen rolls. The strategy was more successful in the first half, holding him to 37.5 percent shooting, yet he started getting loose after.

For a while, the Spurs were making mincemeat of the Kings, even having Acuff and Leaky Black frustrated with each other within five minutes of the game. It started with Devin Vassell's early marksmanship helping steady the offense, and then Victor Wembanyama's inside pressure piled on, leaving them without answers. The Kings would also go to a zone when Wemby sat, but the ball movement made them pay for it.

The Kings do a bad job of helping the helper, which resulted in tons of clean outside looks and the Spurs setting a new season high in 3-point percentage. Wemby and Vassell combined for nine 3-point makes.

The Spurs had a rough start to the third quarter, making 25 percent of their attempts through eight minutes. The Kings made four 3-pointers in that span, yet the flow started slowly turning back to the Spurs because the Kings could not defend without fouling, again. To boot, the Spurs went on to shot the most free throws of the season (42).

In the first half, Stephon Castle was too strong on the move to be denied. He wasn't as assertive later but finished an emphatic lob supplied by Wemby. He finished with 19 points on 53.8 percent shooting with seven assists against one turnover.

Crisis averted: Domantas Sabonis committed a flagrant one by sticking his foot under Wembanyama's landing space on a 3-point attempt, causing his ankle to roll. Wemby made two-of-three at the line and subsequently looked fine in the next half.

De'Aaron Fox didn't have the biggest night on the stat sheet, but made good reads that helped take and maintain the lead.

Aside from the trophy, each player on the winning side of the in-season tournament gets $604,985.

#2903. Eagles notebook: What will the future hold for Drew Mukuba?

Eagles notebook: What will the future hold for Drew Mukuba? originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Vic Fangio doesn't just dish out compliments so what he said about Drew Mukuba this week really stood out.

What has Fangio made of Mukuba's season so far?

"Overall good," Fangio said. "Again, some plays that he'd like to have back, but I think he's a good young player that's going to be a good player in this league for a long time."

For Fangio, that's high praise. Mukuba is a second-year safety who has struggled at times this season. But Fangio's saying he's going to be a good player in the league for a long time didn't go unnoticed.

"It just means that he has a lot of confidence and belief in me," Mukuba said. "I've got to just feed off of that and keep building."

The entire Eagles defense has struggled this season and Mukuba has been a part of that. But the 2025 second-round pick had perhaps his best game of the season in Week 8 against the Commanders when he forced a fumble and had an interception that was negated because of a penalty.

Through nine weeks, Mukuba has one interception and one forced fumble. Getting takeaways is a big part of his game and if Mukuba is going to live up to his potential, he needs to get more.

"I feel like that's why I play safety, is to get the ball back," he said. "I feel like that's why they brought me here is to make plays on the ball. That's my job and I need to do a better job doing that."

When asked about which part of his game needs to improve most, Mukuba thought about giving a general answer and saying everything, but then he got honest: His tackling.

Mukuba this season, according to PFF, has missed 11 tackles. Just four players in the NFL have missed more. Mukuba said his issues are more about bad angles than they are about wrapping up. He said practice reps are the only thing that will help him improve there.

And Mukuba has also gotten beat in coverage this season. While defensive back coverage stats are generally pretty unreliable, especially for teams who use a lot of zone, Mukuba's coverage numbers are not good. He has given up 15 catches on 17 targets for 2 TDs and 1 INT this season. His opponent passer rating is 133.5.

But Mukuba is still a young player in the NFL. And because he is a second-round pick last year, the Eagles are obviously hoping he can become a long-time starter at the safety position.

"I feel like I'm getting better every game," Mukuba said. "I feel like I'm playing my best ball with more left in me."

Eagles quarterback Jalen Hurts was back in the team facility this week and was able to sit in on meetings, ESPN's Tim McManus first reported on Wednesday.

"Jalen's great. Obviously a great leader," Tanner McKee said. "It was fun seeing him again. Talking to somebody on the phone, it's not the same as seeing him in person. Definitely lifted the mood. It was really great seeing and talking to him in person."

Hurts, 28, is going to miss the rest of the season with blood clots in his lungs. But just having him around as a leader will mean something to his teammates. On Wednesday, head coach Nick Sirianni was asked how much he has talked to Hurts.

"Of course, I've seen him, and I have no new update on anything," Sirianni said. "But of course, I've talked to him and am in constant communication with him and know he's resting and recovering. I miss him like crazy. But nothing new on that front.

"I know he's resting and recovering, and we're all still praying for him and hoping for a speedy recovery."

There are a lot of numbers to illustrate just how bad the Eagles have been this season, but they're even very bad in the metrics that Sirianni always talks about. Since he was hired in 2021, Sirianni has always stressed two things: The turnover battle and the explosive play battle.

• Through eight games, the Eagles have just four total takeaways and have turned the ball over eight times. That's a turnover differential of -4. Just four teams have a worse turnover differential this season. This is their worst turnover differential through eight games in the Nick Sirianni Era.

2022: +152025: +42021: +12024: -22023: -32024: -4

• The other thing Sirianni tracks are explosive plays. The Eagles under Nick Sirianni have always defined explosive plays as 10+ yard runs and 16+ yard passes. They have won that battle just once this season and through eight games they're -24 in explosive plays.

The Eagles had held the Commanders to under 100 rushing yards on Sunday until the very end of the game when Rachaad White busted a 33-yarder late in the fourth quarter. The Commanders finished with 135 rushing yards.

And that means the Eagles have now given up at least 100 rushing yards in all eight games to begin this season. That's their longest such streak to begin a season since 1968.

"Inconsistent. Inconsistent," defensive coordinator Vic Fangio said about his run defense after eight games. "Thought it was OK to borderline good [on Sunday]. Then at the end of the game, we gave up the 33 yarder."

Overall, the Eagles are now 28th in run defense this season, allowing 131.9 yards per game. That's obviously not good enough. The Eagles have also struggling rushing the passer but many think these two issues are connected. Sometimes you have to earn the right to rush the passer and that starts by stopping the run.

Jordan Davis is the lead run-stuffer in this defense and after signing a $78 million extension this offseason, Davis isn't living up to expectations.

"He's been not quite as good as he had been in the past, these last couple weeks," Fangio said. "Think he's got a little problem with his hand there, but he can be better, but we love Jordan and he'll get there."

#2902. Diving deeper into Cavs blowout win over 76ers: Cleveland plays their most complete game of season

CLEVELAND — The Cleveland Cavaliers avenged their opening-night collapse by thoroughly outplaying the Philadelphia 76ers for 48 minutes, leading to a 119-95 victory.

The Cavs need Mitchell to look to get teammates involved, but that can't come at the expense of his aggressiveness. The start to this game was the prime example of the balance you want to see from Mitchell.

Mitchell got the scoring going with two layups attacking the basket and then a running jumper in transition in the first minute and a half of the game. That opened up avenues for him to find his teammates, which he did during the rest of the first quarter, resulting in three assists.

That opening set the tempo for what was a complete game from Mitchell. He was the undisputed best player on the court. It's no surprise the Cavs won the minutes he played by 28.

"He feels the moment, he knows we needed this win," head coach Kenny Atkinson said. "He made some great passes too. He was very aggressive tonight."

22 points, 6 rebounds, 4 assists, 1 steal, 1 block

Mobley filled in admirably for Jarrett Allen at center. He controlled the paint on both ends and showed his range as a shooter, going 3-for-4 from deep. Comfortably hitting threes like he has been this season opens up Cleveland's entire offense and allows him to easily fit at the four and the five. This was the ideal version of Mobley.

"He's really playing great basketball," Atkinson said.

15 points, 10 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 steal

The Cavs needed rebounding and a scoring punch from the bench considering who was out of the lineup. Hezonja provided both. He took advantage of mismatches inside, going 4-for-4 on shots inside the arc.

Hezonja is slowly becoming an integral part of this team. His presence has become invaluable over the first nine games.

8 points on 3-of-12 shooting, 7 rebounds, 6 turnovers

You could make a convincing argument that the Cavs would be at the top of the conference if James chose them in free agency instead. Cleveland has dropped games due to a failure to close out contests and poor rebounding. James would help immensely in both aspects. That said, those benefits didn't exactly come through in this dreadful performance.

James wasn't able to establish any kind of rhythm. He couldn't create any separation whenever he attacked off the dribble, and he wasn't able to get out into the open court. On top of that, he had only two more points than he had giveaways. That all added up to arguably his worst professional game ever played in Cleveland.

Embiid struggled mightily. He had a difficult time getting around Mobley and generating clean looks. This resulted in going 0-for-9 from the field. On the other end, he couldn't move laterally quickly enough to hang with Mobley.

This wasn't a standout offensive performance, but his energy and effort on the glass made him a difference maker. That matters for a player who is being asked to fill very different roles depending on who he's sharing the court with.

Meleek Thomas plays with a confidence that can't be taught. Sequences like this show that.

LeBron got the mismatch against Thomas, except the rookie didn't realize that it was a mismatch. Thomas held his ground, and then forced a steal that led to a basket on the other end.

"He's talented, man," Atkinson said of Thomas.

"Nothing can get as worse as December 2, 2010, from a reception standpoint. I've seen the end of the depths of hell when it comes to reception here, all the way to the heavens above. So anything in between is easy money."

– LeBron James on his return to Cleveland

Cavs fans gave James a respectful, but apathetic welcome home.

The Cavs took 43% of their shots at the rim (92nd percentile). This allowed them to outscore the Sixers 50-30 in the paint.

Cleveland's offense is at its best when it's getting inside. They were able to do that with ease on Friday.

Consistency has been an issue for the Cavs this season. They showed glimpses of how good they can be, but had yet to show it for 48 minutes against a quality opponent. Even in their impressive win over the Minnesota Timberwolves, they struggled for prolonged stretches in the first half. This game, however, was different, due to the contributions of their three stars.

Mitchell, James Harden, and Mobley all fulfilled their roles perfectly. Mitchell was dynamic on ball as a scorer and passer. That energy carried the offense. Harden battled defensively and kept the offense on schedule. His steady hand helped Cleveland maintain their grasp of the game. Mobley controlled the paint on both ends, taking advantage of his slower frontcourt opponents.

There's a downstream effect to the stars playing well. The rest of the rotation was able to more easily slot into their roles, even though they were shorthanded. Players like Meleek Thomas, Tyrese Proctor, and Mario Hezonja have much simpler and more defined roles when they're able to play off the stars and aren't forced to pick up the slack. That makes it easier for them to excel, as they did.

This all added up to a convincing and thorough beatdown of the team with the most wins in the league while also being the antithesis of the opening-night collapse.

The Cavs have shown incredible highs and low lows through nine games. At this point, we don't know who this team will be on a night-to-night basis, and they haven't quite discovered their identity. Yet, the talent is there. If they can put the pieces together, they have the potential to be dangerous.

#2899. ‘There's no magic potion:' Vic Fangio pinpoints the issue with Jonathan Greenard

'There's no magic potion:' Vic Fangio pinpoints the issue with Jonathan Greenard originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jonathan Greenard, the Eagles' $98 million free agent edge rusher, doesn't have much to show for his first two months as an Eagle.

Two tackles, one quarterback hit and one forced fumble (that the Eagles didn't recover) to show in 118 snaps over parts of five games.

"What I see is a guy that has had very, very little practice," Fangio said Tuesday. "I mean, when I say very little, minuscule.

"He missed all of training camp, missed the first two weeks of the season, no practice, started practicing, was on a pitch count, played 19 plays, was going to increase the next week, got hurt early in that game, didn't finish that game, didn't play the next game, and he's been limited in practice."

Greenard hasn't had a full week of practice yet. The Eagles, 2-6 with a six-game losing streak, open the practice week on Wednesday for a home game Sunday against the Giants.

Greenard has had exactly six days of full practice so far this year – the Friday before the Bears game, all three days leading up to the Rams, the Friday leading up to the Panthers and the Friday before the game Sunday night in Washington.

"You know, contrary to some people's popular belief, practice is important," Fangio said. "And he has had next to none."

Greenard missed all of training camp and the first two games with a pectoral injury, played 18 snaps against the Bears and 19 against the Rams, then missed the Jaguars game with an ankle injury. He's played 24, 29 and 28 snaps the last three weeks with modest results.

Prior to the Cowboys game, Greenard was limited on Thursday and missed practice Friday and Saturday. Last week, he was limited at practice on Wednesday and Thursday and full on Friday.

When he has played, Greenard hasn't been close to the player the Eagles envisioned. This is a guy who had 24 ½ sacks in 2023 and 2024 and made a Pro Bowl in 2024. His $24.5 million average annual salary is 12th-highest among all NFL edge rushers.

"Practice," Fangio said. "You know, there's no pill, there's no magic potion. You can watch all the tape you want, you can sit in meetings all you want, you can do walkthroughs all you want. The guy's got to practice. Practice is important for everybody."

The Eagles could sure use some pass pressure.

Nolan Smith has four sacks but he's the Eagles' only edge rusher with any sacks. Overall, the Eagles have just 10 sacks, 4th-fewest in the league and the fewest in franchise history through eight games.

You always take Pro Football Focus grades with a grain of salt, but out of 118 edge rushers who've played at least 100 snaps his 57.1 overall grade ranks 93rdand his 65.1 pressure grade ranks 77th.

According to Stathead analytics, Greenard has no quarterback hurries, and he's played the 6th-most snaps among all edge rushers who have no sacks or hurries.

"Yeah, he's just not into a groove," Fangio said. "He's not comfortable in our system totally and what we want him to do. And it's not because he doesn't know or doesn't want to. It's just he hasn't executed it. Walk-throughs are an assignment check. It's not execution.

"I don't know that I've had a guy that's had so little practice."

#2898. Is Luka Dončić already an MVP lock? Lakers star continues torrid start with 53-point outburst

LOS ANGELES – Walker Kessler had as good a look as anybody inside Crypto.com Arena at the biggest shot of the night.

The Los Angeles Lakers big man grabbed a Sidy Cissoko airball with six seconds left in the first half, handed the ball to Luka Dončić, set a screen for him and let the Luka Magic happen from there. Dončić launched a 62-footer from the top of the opposite key. The crowd held their breath with what felt more like anticipation than anxiety as the ball floated high in the air before coming back down and banking into the net for yet another unreal buzzer-beater.

"He's incredible to watch," Walker said after the Lakers' 124-118 win over the Portland Trail Blazers on Friday. "He's incredible to play alongside. He's my MVP. I think it's pretty clear right now."

Trail Blazers head coach Micah Nori had the utmost praise for Dončić when he spoke with reporters at Crypto.com Arena on Friday afternoon, saying the Slovenian superstar "can do anything in the world."

Dončić proved his opponent's point in the closing seconds of the first half.

What made the shot even more notable, perhaps, was that the Lakers had collectively struggled shooting the ball up to that point, starting 0-for-15 from 3-point range and falling behind by as many as 16. Dončić's shot at the end of the half was just LA's fourth make from deep in 23 attempts; it also sent the Lakers into the locker room having cut the Portland lead to just seven points.

"I think it changes the whole energy, in the team and in the gym. I thought it was an important shot to make," Dončić said before cracking a smile. "So that's why I decided to make it."

But it's not just Dončić who has an unshakeable confidence when it comes to that kind of shot, which he works on meticulously after practices and shootarounds. His teammates are starting to expect them to go in.

"At this point, it's pretty normal for him," Jake LaRavia said. "It's high difficulty for a lot of players, but it's pretty easy for him. I feel like he's just been playing at such a high level. Incredible offensive talent."

And true to what Dončić expected, a switch flipped for the rest of the night.

The Lakers dominated the third quarter, outscoring the Trail Blazers 34-15 in the frame behind 20 points from Dončić, who has now dropped at least 40 in four consecutive games, joining Kobe Bryant, Elgin Baylor and George Mikan as the only players in franchise history to do so. Dončić finished the night with a season-high 53 points, five rebounds and five assists. That brings his average up to an unthinkable 40.1 points per game, something that hasn't been accomplished since Wilt Chamberlain.

"I'm having a lot of fun," Dončić said. "Obviously playing basketball, it's a lot of fun for me. It's more when you play like this and when you win games. It's been like this my whole life. Basketball was my dream. Playing in the NBA was my dream."

The 27-year-old has completely kicked in the door and is dominating the early MVP conversation amid this historic hot streak.

Dončić currently leads Lorenzo Reyes' NBA MVP rankings, and a performance like the one on Friday only furthers the hold Dončić has on the top spot. Just listen to the multiple "M-V-P" chants that echoed through the building at various points in the night.

Not that MVP discussions have much meaning at all 10 games into an 82-game season. Head coach JJ Redick himself said pregame he doesn't get into those talks until they're at least 50 games in. But the level Dončić has been playing at makes it hard not to at least acknowledge it.

Redick, who has previously called Dončić a supernova, was asked if he's running out of superlatives to describe his best player. His response?

"Sure. I'm gonna just sit around and we'll gather one day, come up with more superlatives so I have more words ready," he said sarcastically. "For now, I don't have any words."

The Lakers look like the real deal early on. Their chemistry is off the charts; that much was on full display with their 17 total assists on Friday, or in Kessler's 16-point, 12-rebound night, or Austin Reaves' 25 points.

But Dončić has been the engine behind it all. He's playing like the best player in the NBA, and the team is that much better because of it.

#2897. The AI ‘credit card’ is getting costlier as CoreWeave bond yield tops 16%

Debt markets are sending a message to AI data center builders: we're hiking the interest rate on your credit card and may put some new curbs on your credit limit.

That analogy comes courtesy of Jefferies global head of equity strategy, Christopher Wood, who put it in a note on Friday: "The most plausible way the AI capex arms race will end is that the credit card will be taken away."

That isn't happening yet, but credit markets have been spooked by the new political landscape — seeing more hurdles to data centers under Democratic governors in key states, along with a Democratic-controlled Congress.

The poster child for debt stress is neocloud Coreweave. The yield on its 2032 bond with a 9.625% coupon has climbed above 16%. Coreweave's five-year credit default swaps spiked above 1,289 basis points on Thursday, widening above their October peak, according to Deutsche Bank. The CDS eased 8% Friday to around 1,155.

"We've had plenty of warnings from the credit space," said Jim Reid, Deutsche Bank's global head of macro and thematic research, in a note on Friday. Debt and equities are now moving more in sync, he added, "so we'll have to see if credit goes any further."

Investors are demanding more compensation to insure against default among even companies with the strongest balance sheets and credit ratings, including Nvidia, Broadcom, and Amazon.com.

CDS on Nvidia debt is up 76% in the last month. Broadcom's CDS is up 49%, while Amazon's has gained 44%. Their CDS remain low on an absolute basis; Nvidia's spread stood at roughly 139 basis points, compared with about 298 bps for Oracle and roughly 1,100 for CoreWeave. A large increase from a low starting point does not imply the same credit risk.

Still, rising CDS are a sign that markets want more protection against default among even the largest and healthiest debt issuers. And rising CDS imply higher financing costs down the line.

The market is now grappling with the prospect that at least some data center spending will be delayed or scrapped under incoming Democratic governors in Ohio, Texas, and Georgia, the three states with much of the planned capacity.

Moratoriums could impact 45% of planned capacity in the U.S. with a large amount of that in Texas, according to Goldman Sachs. Texas governor-elect Gina Hinojosa has said she's not opposed to data centers but wants a moratorium until the state legislature passes protections for communities.

None of these means AI debt financing is collapsing. Goldman tallies five AI bond deals totaling $67 billion since October 29, pushing total hyperscaler debt issuance this year to $296 billion. Goldman forecasts $440 billion of hyperscaler debt issuance next year, plus an additional $300 billion of project-level issuance across investment-grade and high-yield.

The Trump administration is vowing to support data centers. National Economic Council Director Kevin Hassett told CNBC on Friday that he expects data center growth to continue as forecast, despite Democrats' big election wins.

The big question now is how quickly AI spending will generate enough cash for hyperscalers to keep investors comfortable with future capex plans. For the neoclouds, any sign of consolidation or retrenchment in capex could result in fewer or shorter contracts for data center compute or delays in prepayments.

Goldman analysts said hyperscalers collectively reported negative free cash flow in the third quarter as capital spending exceeded operating cash flow.

"Delayed monetization and growth in purchase commitments—which we view as additional and sizable uses of cash, separate from Capex—are the key risks to more debt issuance, subject to market conditions," according to the bank.

Peak AI debt issuance may now be closer at hand. Bank of America analysts estimates that AI-related credit issuance will rise 31% from 2026 to 2027, hitting $685 billion, including public and private debt. They then see it falling 23% to $531 billion in 2028.

As for the risk? "Downside to these forecasts exists from a potential slowdown in AI infrastructure projects on the back of the Blue Wave, which will reduce capex needs and thus debt market funding," BofA said in a note.

-CNBC's Nick Wells and Michael Bloom contributed reporting

#2895. 10 things you need to know about new Eagles quarterback Joe Milton

10 things you need to know about new Eagles quarterback Joe Milton originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Joe Milton on Sunday will become the Eagles' third starting quarterback this year and the first former Cowboy to start at quarterback for the Eagles in more than 30 years.

What fruit is Milton famous for throwing the length of a football field? What long-time Eagles safety went to the same high school as Milton? Who's the only other quarterback drafted by the Patriots to play for the Eagles?

You'll find answers to these questions and several other fascinating nuggets in our 10 Things you Need to Know about Joe Milton.

Guaranteed it's more interesting than 10 Things you Need to Know about Andy Dalton.

1. Milton's cousin is Anquan Bolden, the long-time Arizona Cardinals wide receiver, who caught 1,076 passes for 13,779 yards in his 14-year career. Both went to Pahokee, Fla., High School, also the alma mater of long-time Eagles safety Andre Waters. Another cousin of Milton's is Kevin Bouie, the Eagles' 7th-round pick out of Mississippi State. Bouie, who also attended Pahokee High School, never got into a regular-season game for the Eagles but played for the Chargers in 1996 and Cards in 1997.

2. Milton spent the first two years of his college career at Michigan, backing up Shea Patterson. During the 2020 COVID season, Milton started three games but gradually lost playing time to Cade McNamara and then transferred to Tennessee before the 2021 season. Milton was hurt most of 2021 and backed up Hendon Hooker for most of 2022, although he did win two late-season starts after Hooker suffered a torn ACL in a game against South Carolina. Milton went 8-4 in 2023 with 20 touchdown passes and five interceptions, passing for 2,769 yards and rushing for 292 with six more TDs.

3. Milton was originally the Patriots' 6th-round pick in 2024. He played in one game in New England, going 22-for-29 for 241 yards and a 48-yard touchdown pass to Kayshon Boutte in a 23-16 win over the Bills on the final day of the 2024 season after Drake Maye played one series. Milton also ran for a touchdown. Patriots head coach Jerod Mayo was fired soon after the game ended. Milton's passer rating of 111.4 was highest by a Patriots rookie since Bailey Zappe had a 118.4 in a game against the Browns in 2022

4. Although Milton has thrown about a quarter as many career passes as Tanner McKee – 202 to 53 – the strong-armed Milton has two more completions of at least 35 yards than McKee. Milton threw a 48-yard TD to Boutte in 2024 and a 35-yarder to Jalen Tolbert for the Cowboys in a 44-23 loss to the Broncos last October. McKee's longest career completion is the 34-yarder to Eli Stowers on the final play of the loss to the Commanders Sunday. In his career, Milton has averaged 8.0 yards per attempt and 11.5 yards per completion, compared to McKee's 6.1 yards per attempt and 9.8 yards per completion. Among 79 active quarterbacks who've thrown at least 50 passes, Milton's 8.0 yards per attempt is 3rd-highest behind Brock Purdy and Maye and his 11.5 yards per completion ranks 22nd.

5. Milton will become the Eagles' first quarterback to start a game after joining the team after opening day in 33 years. In 1993, the Eagles signed Ken O'Brien on Oct. 4, and he started four games, going 0-4, the last four starts of his career. O'Brien, who had made two Pro Bowls in nine seasons with the Jets, made his first start as an Eagle against the Cowboys on Oct. 31, 27 days after he signed. Milton will top that, starting against the Giants on Nov. 8, only 23 days after the Eagles acquired him on Oct. 16.

6. Milton will become the first quarterback from the University of Tennessee to throw a pass for the Eagles since Pat Ryan played in four games at the end of 1991. Ryan threw no touchdowns and four interceptions as an Eagle and had a passer rating of 10.3, worst by an Eagles quarterback who threw at least 25 passes since 1943. The Eagles' only other Tennessee quarterback was Johnny Butler, who threw 13 passes in 1943 and lost his only start, 13-7, to the Brooklyn Dodgers at Ebbets Field in 1943. Previous quarterbacks who've played for both the Eagles and Cowboys include Cunningham, Rodney Peete and Mark Sanchez. Only Peete played for the Eagles after the Cowboys.

7. Milton will be the second quarterback (and third player) drafted by the Patriots to throw a regular-season pass for the Eagles. Matt Cavanaugh, the Patriots' 2nd-round pick out of Pitt in 1978, spent 1986 through 1989 with the Eagles backing up Ron Jaworski and Randall Cunningham. He went 1-1 in two starts for the Eagles, both in 1986. Cavanaugh spent 13 years in the NFL – four with the Patriots, three with the 49ers, four with the Eagles and two with the Giants. He was 8-11 in 19 career starts with 28 touchdowns and 30 interceptions. After retiring from the NFL, Cavanaugh coached with the Cards under Buddy Ryan, the 49ers under George Seifert, the Bears under Dave Wannstedt, the Ravens under Brian Billick (where he was Cunningham's final NFL offensive coordinator), the Jets under Rex Ryan, the Bears again under Marc Trestman, Washington under Jay Gruden, the Jets again under Robert Saleh and he's currently a senior offensive with the Commanders under Dan Quinn. The only other player drafted by the Patriots to throw a pass for the Eagles was safety Joe Scarpati, the Patriots' 13th-round pick in 1964. He was 1-for-2 with a three-yard completion to Sam Baker in a 1968 win over the Lions at Tiger Stadium.

8. Following Jalen Hurts, Andy Dalton and McKee, Milton will become the fourth Eagles quarterback to throw a pass this year. Last time four quarterbacks threw a pass for the Eagles in the same season was 1991 when five did – Randall Cunningham, Jim McMahon, Brad Goebel, Jeff Kemp and Pat Ryan.

9. How strong is Milton's arm? You can find the video online, but while he was at Tennessee he threw an orange an estimated 100 yards. Milton, during a practice at the Vols' 80-yard practice facility, hurled the orange from his own end zone across the field and into the facility's back wall. He is said to have thrown a football 100 yards as well and a baseball 95 miles-per-hour.

10. Tradition in Pahokee is for kids to chase and catch rabbits that scatter when local farmers burn the sugarcane fields in the fall. According to local tradition, the faster you are and the more rabbits you catch, the better football career you'll have. Milton even has a tattoo of a rabbit on his arm to honor the tradition. Another Pahokee native, Janoris Jenkins, a Pro Bowl cornerback with the Giants, actually went by Jackrabbit Jenkins during his 11-year NFL career to commemorate his hometown tradition.

#2893. Luka Doncic scores 53 as Lakers avenge loss to Trail Blazers

The absurd 62-foot three-pointer Luka Doncic hoisted and made late in the second quarter Friday night seemed to ignite him, pushing him to even higher heights on the way to scoring 53 points.

Doncic especially went to work in the third quarter, scoring 20 points in that span in delivering the Lakers to a 124-118 win over the Portland Trail Blazers Friday night at Crypto.com Arena.

The Lakers had been down by 16 points in the second quarter, but after Doncic's shot, they trailed by only seven points and saw the momentum swing in the process.

"Oh yeah, definitely. I think it changes the whole energy in the team and in the gym," Doncic said. "I thought it was an important shot to make. So that's why I decided to make it."

It was the second time Doncic has scored 50 points or more this season, his first time coming when he scored 52 points against the Utah Jazz on Oct. 25. It was the fourth time Doncic has scored 50-plus points as a Laker, passing LeBron James and tying George Mikan for fourth in franchise regular-season history.

It was Doncic's 11th 50-plus point game of his career, which moved him pass Kareem Abdul-Jabbar and tied him with Allen Iverson for the 10th most in NBA history.

Lakers forward Jake LaRavia and Trail Blazers guard Scoot Henderson dive for a loose ball during the first half Friday night at Crypto.com Arena. (Gina Ferazzi / Los Angeles Times)

Doncic played 38 minutes and nine seconds. He shot 17 for 33 from the field and five for 15 from three-point range.

At one point, while Doncic was talking to the media, he blurted out, "I'm tired."

A few minutes later, he was asked how he has been holding up during his offensive onslaught and how far he can keep pushing this.

"Feeling great," Doncic said. " But what do you mean how far?"

"Oh yeah, I feel like that," Doncic said. "But as long as we win, I'm good."

Doncic is now averaging 40.1 points per game this season.

He was asked if he knew the last player to average 40 points per game in a single season.

No, it was not Kobe Bryant, Doncic was told.

It was Wilt Chamberlain, who averaged 50.36 points per game during the 1961-62 season and then 44.83 points per game during the 62-63 season.

"Man, it's obviously a long season," Doncic said. "We're just 10 games in. We got 72 left. But as long as we win, I'm good. Got them up today, not gonna lie. Hopefully it continues, but you never know."

Doncic added five rebounds and five assists to help the Lakers win their first NBA Cup game after losing the first one to the Golden State Warriors last week.

When the Lakers saw their 16-point lead sliced to four in the fourth quarter, naturally it was Doncic who saved them.

His seven-foot floater with 27.7 seconds remaining gave the Lakers a six-point lead that stood.

Now everyone knows why Doncic is shooting a career-best from three-point range and why he practices those long-range threes all the time.

After Walker Kessler got a defensive rebound with 4.3 seconds left in the second quarter, he passed the ball to Doncic, who had nowhere to go with the Portland defense around him. So, Doncic just tossed up the three-pointer from the three-point line on the Portland side of the court that banked in as time expired. He looked at the crowd as he walked off the court for halftime.

"When he shoots them, it's not a surprise if it goes in," said Austin Reaves, who had 25 points. "So, it's not a high-percentage shot by any means, but he seems to make them a little, you know, easier than the majority of people."

Redick explained why both Bronny James and Cameron Carr were assigned to the Coachella Valley Lakers.

"[The] messaging was very simple," Redick said. "It's just you guys need reps and you guys need to play."

Redick said both James and Carr will be with the Lakers on the upcoming trip to Detroit, Atlanta, Milwaukee and Phoenix next week.

#2892. Eagles vs. Giants predictions for Week 9 of the 2026 NFL season

Eagles vs. Giants predictions for Week 9 of the 2026 NFL season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles (2-6) host the first-place New York Giants (4-2-1) in an NFC battle at the Linc on Sunday afternoon.

I'm not sure how good the Giants are, but I do know how bad the Eagles are. Really bad. Seems like John Harbaugh has done a nice job transforming the Giants' culture from losing to winning almost overnight. The Giants are 4-2-1, just the second time in the last decade they've had a winning record eight games into a season. This is the first time the Eagles and Giants will meet with the Giants at least two games over .500 and the Eagles at least two games under .500 since 2016, when the Giants were 10-4 going into a late-season game against the Eagles at the Linc and the Eagles were 5-9. The Eagles won that one 24-19 behind Carson Wentz, who was teammates with current Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy in Minnesota earlier this year. Anyway, hopefully Joe Milton injects some life into the offense, but I just don't think the Eagles are good enough to beat anybody right now. Seven in a row? Really? Really.

Have I lost my mind? Maybe. But I have a gut feeling about this game that the Eagles are going to snap their losing streak. This is not a prediction that they're about to turn around their season or that Joe Milton is some sort of great answer. Just that inserting Milton into the offense will provide a bit of a spark and the Eagles will finally be able to scrape out a low-scoring game against another QB who wasn't with his team when the year began. The Giants have been playing tough, but I'm not a real believer. We know the Giants are going to blitz the heck out of the Eagles, but at least Milton will be able to get away from some of that pressure and give the Eagles a chance to create. The Giants might not have Cam Skattebo, which would really hurt their offense and make them more one-dimensional. If that's the case, then the Eagles might actually be able to finally get some pressure on the QB. If the Eagles win this game, the defense will probably have to force a couple turnovers and I think they can finally do that. Is this the wisest prediction of the year? No way. I don't really want to pick the Eagles but I'm going with my gut. A win before the bye week — a win that should not change any decisions ahead of the trade deadline.

Considering what we've seen from the Eagles over the last six weeks, it's shocking to see they're only 3.5 point underdogs at home to the first-place Giants. The switch from Tanner McKee to Joe Milton didn't have much impact on the betting line, but it should at least increase the watchability factor of Sunday's game. Milton has the physical tools to be an NFL starter but has yet to show he can put it all together. It was surprising to see him cut by the Cowboys after an impressive preseason. He even went unclaimed on waivers, letting you know the entire league didn't even see him as a backup. After settling in on the practice squad in Dallas, he's now starting for the Eagles less than a month after being signed to the 53-man roster by Howie Roseman.

The timing of the switch – with the bye week looming – is quite curious. They could have waited and a game and given Milton a two-week runway into his first NFL start. Perhaps this Tuesday's trade deadline has something to do with it. Either way, the Eagles should get a spark but it won't be enough to beat John Harbaugh's bunch. The Giants play tough and have stars in the trenches. JJ McCarthy has a new lease on his NFL life and is making the most of it. He sends the Eagles into their week off on a seven-game skid.

Joe Milton takes over as the Eagles' starting quarterback, and I think the element of the unknown could actually work in Philadelphia's favor. There is very little film on Milton, and even the Eagles themselves may not know exactly what to expect from a quarterback making his first career start. Sometimes, that's a good thing. Don't overthink it — just go out and play. Saquon Barkley should also be a little healthier after getting another week of rest for his hamstring and back, which could allow Milton to lean heavily on the Eagles' running game. That's why I expect this game to be close. However, it's difficult to make a strong case for the Eagles winning another game until they show they can consistently move the football and generate some offense through the air. On the other side, I've been impressed with what New York has been able to accomplish despite having a relatively average roster. Credit the Giants' coaching staff for getting the most out of this group. Matt Nagy has been a strong offensive addition, and quarterback JJ McCarthy appears to be giving the Giants a spark as they look to make some noise in the NFC East.

As much uncertainty that there is surrounding Joe Milton's first career start, expect some fireworks on Sunday against the Giants. Milton is as talented a thrower of the football as I've seen up close, as the ball just pops out his hand with a type of velocity matched by few on Earth. That's the honest truth. However, there is much more to succeeding at the NFL level than just having a howitzer for an arm, and that's where it starts this week for Milton and the Eagles' offensive coaching staff. Minimizing the playbook and focusing on what he does best (RPOs, bootleg, designed QB runs) will remain paramount if the Eagles want to leave Week 9 with a win. The offense as a whole hasn't looked good at all whoever has been under center, which leaves me with little confidence that the metaphorical lightbulb will click on this week, but you should be very excited about the skillset that Milton possesses. There will be plays that make a highlight reel, and plays that make you scratch your head, but why not let him go rip it this week. Let's just hope the offensive line gives him a few seconds to go through his progressions, as Giants DC Dennard Wilson won't be shy in heating him up.

Defensively, muddy up looks as much as you can against JJ McCarthy. He thrives in rhythm and in the intermediate areas. Jonathan Greenard, Jalyx Hunt, Nolan Smith, the focus is on the guys off the edge to create some push this week. The Giants leave their tackles on an island a bunch, and if you can create some push 1-on-1 there should be opportunities to force a few turnovers. Overall, I just don't trust this team right now to play complementary football for a full 60 minutes. They have failed to show any type of adjustments in any of the three phases of the game week after week, and their opponent in the Giants have a true identity as a football team who are playing some good ball right now.

#2889. Victor Wembanyama scores 30 as Spurs beat Kings for 7th straight win

SACRAMENTO, CA ― The San Antonio Spurs continue to roll, earning their seventh consecutive victory in taking down the Sacramento Kings, 124-109, in an NBA Cup group stage game on Friday, Nov. 6.

The Spurs were led by center Victor Wembanyama with 30 points on 10-of-18 shooting. He added 14 rebounds and two blocks. Four other players scored in double figures as Stephon Castle, Devin Vassell and Dylan Harper each had 19. Harrison Barnes scored 13 off the bench.

The Spurs dominated from the opening tip all the way to the end of the game. Wembanyama seems firmly in the early MVP race.

"I do not have a goal for Victor to get MVP," Spurs coach Mitch Johnson said before the game. "I think that there's a lot of things that we're trying to get better and improve on and execute that's gonna be a byproduct of the conversation that could produce."

"He has shown in a short amount of time, in his career, and season by season, however you want, compartmentalized time. His improvement is rapid," he added. "When he gets ahold of certain things, whether that's how he's being guarded, actions we want to run, teammates he's playing with, when he gets ahold of certain things, he can become very dominant. And if he continues to put his energy into the right stuff as he has, and to continue to have that level of retaining what works for him and works for us, then whatever the goals are, I would not bet against."

Spurs guard De'Aaron Fox was welcomed with a mix of boos and cheers as he was announced in the starting lineup. Fox was drafted by Sacramento with the No. 5 pick in the 2017 NBA Draft and spent eight seasons with the Kings before being traded to San Antonio. Fox got the first points on the board on Friday. He finished with nine.

Sacramento only led in the game for a brief moment early in the first quarter. Kings center Domantas Sabonis opened the game with a thunderous slam over Wembanyama. He finished with a double-double, 17 points and 12 rebounds, in the loss.

Kings dynamic scoring rookie Darius Acuff Jr. followed Sabonis' dunk with a 3-point field goal to put Sacramento ahead 5-2 with 10:45 left in the first period. Acuff picked up two early fouls, which sent him to the bench with 6:19 left after playing six minutes in the opening quarter. Acuff finished with 22 points and eight assists. He shot 9-of-20 from the field, including 3-of-9 on 3-point attempts.

The real disparity came with player fouls. The Kings committed 26 fouls as the Spurs shot 42 free throws.

"I mean, early on, it was good, but, I mean, you know, when you get to the free throw line as much as they were, that's tough," Kings head coach Doug Christie said. "Because not only are they a good defensive team, they're even better when their defense is set, and that's what the free throw allows you to do. When we were able to get in open court, we were able to find some things and create the type of 3-point shots and different things, collapse in the defense."

The Kings have back-to-back losses after getting their first win of the season against the Dallas Mavericks in a 115-100 finish on Nov. 2. Sacramento welcomes the Miami Heat on Sunday, Nov. 9 to the Golden 1 Center.

The Spurs won the first game of their four-game road trip. They immediately board a plane to face the Portland Trail Blazers on Saturday, Nov. 7.

#2886. Why Joe Mazzulla hugged Neemias Queta instead of yelling at him in win over Hornets

CHARLOTTE — Early in the third quarter of the Celtics' 119-80 win over the Charlotte Hornets, Joe Mazzulla called a timeout and walked over to Neemias Queta.

Queta had just gotten tangled up with multiple Hornets players while trying to secure a rebound. And, as is often the case, Mazzulla grew frustrated with his big man's longstanding tendency to foul.

But this time, instead of reaming him out, Mazzulla chose to embrace him.

"I'm just trying to channel my anger into love," Mazzulla said afterward.

"Instead of just yelling at him, I gave him a hug."

It's no secret that Mazzulla is hard on the Portuguese big man, who began his journey with the Celtics on a two-way contract in 2023 and blossomed into the Celtics' productive and reliable starting center last season.

Sometimes, Mazzulla's yelling gets picked up by the TV broadcast. More often than not, his anger is visible from press row.

And, none of that tough love is something that the Celtics head coach denies.

"I'm hard on Neemi," Mazzulla said on Friday night. "I'm hard on our bigs. Those guys, the way they need to play for us, there's a ton of pressure on them. We talk about it all the time — the fives have the toughest job on this team because they got to do a ton on both ends of the floor."

Mazzulla has frequently discussed how difficult the center position is in the team's system, particularly as of late. That's also applied to Mitchell Robinson, who has struggled in his acclimation to the Celtics as he takes on more responsibility in a new system.

Still, Mazzulla wants to make sure that amid his tough coaches style, there's an understanding that all of it is rooted in love. He visits players in the offseason in order to better understand what makes them tick; this past summer, he joined Queta in Portugal, Mitchell Robinson in Nashville, and Luka Garza in Iowa.

In difficult moments, there's a relationship that runs deeper than basketball.

"I appreciate that they allow me to be hard on them, but I also want them to know I appreciate them," he said. "Just trying to do that for them."

Queta is not the only player to get a hug from Joe Mazzulla as of late; Payton Pritchard got an in-game hug from Mazzulla in a win over the Chicago Bulls, after a couple of high-impact plays.

"Joe challenged me," Pritchard said after that win. "I feel like the first four games my defensive activity and my rebounding have been poor… He's challenging me to step up in that area. I think the hug was for me doing that."

On Friday, Pritchard reflected on how Mazzulla has successfully tapped into both sides of the emotional spectrum as a coach.

"Joe, he's a competitor, and he has a lot of emotion, so it brings the best out of us, and it's also cool when you are doing well; he shows the emotion of a loving brother type situation," Pritchard said. "And then we'll have moments where we get into it, and we have disagreements, and it's all right. It's just part of it — we go through love and hate."

Pritchard said his bond with Mazzulla has strengthened over the years; he's been playing for him since he took over as head coach in 2022, and it's come with plenty of variance. Pritchard has gone through stretches in which he's fallen out of the rotation, won Sixth Man of the Year, and was a part of the 2024 championship team.

"It's a journey," he said. "We've definitely gone through a lot of different situations, a lot of ups and downs, and you just grow your relationship through that, and it becomes stronger and stronger."

Mazuzlla echoed that sentiment last week, after his hug.

"I like pushing him, I like coaching him, and I love him like a brother," Mazzulla said of Pritchard.

For Mazzulla, turning anger into love has been the result of an intentional effort, especially when it comes to his relationship with Queta.

"A lot of meditation, a lot of journaling, a lot of just not being so mad at him all the time," Mazzulla said. "But I love him."

Still, Mazzulla's ability to leverage anger hasn't gone away, the Celtics' veterans say.

"It just depends on the day," said Jayson Tatum with a smile. "Some good days, some bad days."

"They're both needed," Pritchard said. "You can't have love without the anger."

#2882. Nick Sirianni admits that he misled reporters about quarterback situation

How do you know when a football coach is lying? If he’s good at it, you don’t.

Because they all do. Particularly in so-called “must-lie” situations.

There’s an art to it. A way to conceal a lie with another lie. On Friday, Eagles coach Nick Sirianni opted not to lie about the fact that he lied on Monday when he told reporters that quarterback Tanner McKee would not be benched.

“Tanner’s our guy,” Sirianni said. “We’re not making any changes there.”

On Wednesday, the Eagles made a change there, elevating Joe Milton over McKee. The move was announced after Sirianni had his midweek media availability, which prevented him from being asked questions about the change in position from “Tanner’s our guy” to “Tanner’s not our guy.”

On Friday, Sirianni finally faced the music regarding his comments from Monday. In lieu of coming up with an explanation premised on the idea that what he said was true when he said it and circumstances changed, Sirianni surprisingly admitted that he wasn’t telling the truth, the whole truth, and nothing but the truth to reporters and, in turn, the fans.

“I was going to hold onto that as long as I possibly could, every second, every minute,” Sirianni said. “Again, I’m not real shy with you guys. I’m going to tell you what I want to tell you guys. I respect the job that y’all have to do, but I’m going to tell you what I want to tell you guys because I’m always going to protect the team first and foremost.

“Every second that goes by in a situation like that, that you can hold onto information as long as you possibly can, is a little less time for the opponent to get ready for the game. I know there’s a lot of interest in everything that you all want to hear, the fans want to hear. My job is to win football games, do everything we can do to help win football games, protect the guys here on this football team, and handle everything that I can inside.”

In an industry where the truth is only told when it’s coincidental to strategic interests, it’s jarring that Sirianni told the truth that he was lying. It would have been easy to say, “We took a more careful look at the situation after Monday.” Or, even more simply, “We changed our mind. Shit happens.”

Instead, Sirianni opted to say, essentially, “Bullshit happens.” Instead of bothering to concoct a cover story regarding his lie, he said, essentially, “Yeah. I lied. I’ll do it whenever I feel like I have to.”

That’s the most important thing to remember. Coaches lie whenever they feel like they have to. Despite that fact, reporters and fans routinely take their words at face value, swallowing the bullshit while saying, “Thank you, sir, may I have another?”

So thank you, Nick, for making my job a little easier. In my first week of law school, one of the professors said, “The most important thing I can do for you is to give you a good crap filter.”

Because crap is everywhere. Usually, the people spewing it don’t admit it. When they do, it provides a tangible reminder as to whatever they say in the future.

For Sirianni, that’s the most problematic aspect of his decision to tell the truth about lying. There’s now an “Exhibit A” to which anyone and everyone can point as to anything and everything he says when assessing whether he’s being straight with reporters and, in turn, with fans.

#2881. Hirving Lozano going 'step-by-step' with LA Galaxy, sets 2030 World Cup goal

That's what Hirving Lozano says. Body language analysts may conclude otherwise, but even with the suspensions, the disagreements and ultimately him being frozen out at San Diego FC, Lozano says he still felt that wave of emotion when he did take the field.

"I've always had joy playing soccer. It's what I like to do most, what I'm most passionate about. Playing soccer is something marvelous for me. So, every time I'm on the field, every time I touch a ball, I'm happy," he told USA TODAY Sports this week. "Right now, I'm playing, touching the ball, getting on the field, that's the best for me, the best thing possible."

Lozano is enjoying two returns: The first, being on the field with the LA Galaxy and scoring in a 4-1 win over Austin FC. A win over Real Salt Lake on Nov. 7, and the Galaxy would lock up a playoff place, with Lozano now fit and ready to help the team in the postseason after a hamstring issue kept him out for all of October.

The other return, weeks earlier, saw him playing professional soccer again after that hold out with SDFC.

Signed by the 2025 MLS expansion club as its franchise player, meant to be the perfect star to connect with supporters of a team that wants to be relevant with fans both in Southern California and northern Baja California, Lozano put together a strong campaign in helping SDFC top the Western Conference and secure a place in the Concacaf Champions Cup in its first season of play.

Lozano was suspended in October 2025, and didn't recover his starting spot in the playoffs - though he played in the club's final four postseason games as they fell to the Vancouver Whitecaps in the conference finals. Prior to the season, SDFC general manager Tyler Heaps stated flatly in January 2026 that Lozano would "not be part of the sporting plans moving forward." Rather than move elsewhere, Lozano spent the first half of the year training on his own before an Aug. 7 move ot the LA Galaxy.

"I'm very happy. The reception at the Galaxy, to be honest, has been marvelous, giving me the chance to play again. I'm putting in a lot of effort to try to get to my best level," he said this week. "I know it's been a bit complicated just not being able to play the whole season, not having a preseason, but I'm going step by step trying to get better every day and I'm really very happy to be here."

The second return is being part of the push to get the Galaxy into the playoffs and contending for another MLS Cup.

Lozano's mini-renaissance, getting back on the field for the Galaxy and earning a recall to the Mexico national team for the first time since fall of the year before, was cut short by a hamstring issue.

The Pachuca product credited his family and the Galaxy's medical team for getting him back on the field quickly, a return he hopes will help push his new club into the postseason and give it a chance at adding to its trophy case.

A professional since 2014, and a veteran of both the 2018 and 2022 World Cup, "Chucky" knows he may have to convince fans - and coaches - that he still has enough in the tank to help teams to silverware, especially after his time on the shelf to start 2026.

That's why the call-up from new Mexico manager Rafa Marquez for the September-October friendlies was something he felt he needed to make the most of. The manager knows Lozano well.

Marquez and Lozano were teammates when the attacker scored the most important goal of his El Tri career at the 2018 World Cup, pushing Mexico past Germany in its opening match of that tournament.

"We have a really good relationship. The call he gave me, giving me the chance to return to the national team was fantastic. I'm really thankful to him, as I always have told him," Lozano said. "We've got to work really hard to get back there. Hopefully I can be back there soon."

Lozano's absence from Mexico's November squad can be put down to a lack of rhythm after the injury that kept him out of action in October. But Marquez isn't going to do him any favors just because of what they experienced in the past, giving Lozano extra motivation as he pushes for a place as a veteran on Mexico's 2030 World Cup roster.

"A player always wants to get to a World Cup, which is an incredible thing," he said. "Thankfully, I've been able to experience two.

"I think getting to another World Cup would be fantastic. So, that's my goal. That's what I want, but it's always step-by-step working really hard to be able to be there."

Now 31, Lozano knows he needs to be playing well and playing consistently to be back in the picture for the 2027 Gold Cup, much less the World Cup when he will be 35.

The question on the outside is where he'll be doing that playing. Rumored deals to clubs in his native Mexico didn't materialize or were reportedly shot down when he was looking for a lifeline out of San Diego, and the Galaxy move is only a temporary one - for now.

He is on loan with LA through December 2026, though there is a "permanent trade option" that could keep him in LA. The Galaxy have a designated player slot he could fill, having sold Gabriel Pec to Cruzeiro in the summer.

A kumbaya moment where he and the SDFC brass grasp hands ahead of the 2027 campaign seems unlikely, but stranger reunions have happened in soccer. The Galaxy may make the decision to lock him down before that, with Lozano having contributed two goals and an assist in nine matches heading into Decision Day.

"At the moment, I'm really concentrated on the Galaxy," Lozano said. "We're fighting to get into the playoffs and try to have the chance to get to the top. I think this is the most important thing. In the future, when the season ends, we'll see. I think the top thing is just the right now."

More Mexico moments? The 2030 World Cup? He knows the type of work it will take to reach those long-term goals. The right now, he says, is where he's feeling the joy, once again taking the field and having a ball at his feet.
