#2878. If you stayed up late enough, you saw why the Eagles are so high on Eli Stowers

If you stayed up late enough, you saw why the Eagles are so high on Eli Stowers originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Eli Stowers said two weeks ago that whenever he did finally get the opportunity to make plays, he was confident he'd be ready.

Then he went out Sunday night and showed he was.

It was largely garbage time and the game was all but over, but Stowers – the Eagles' rookie 2nd-round pick – caught three passes for 53 yards in the second half of the Eagles' sixth straight loss. They were the first three receptions of his career in the second game of his career.

Stowers caught a seven-yarder from Tanner McKee for a first down in the third quarter and then caught passes on the final two snaps of the game, a 12-yarder that gave the Eagles a 4th-and-7 on the Washington 42-yard-line with two seconds left and then a 34-yarder down to the 8-yard-line as time expired.

"It's always good to get the first one out of the way and to be able to feel that in a game," Stowers said at his locker after practice Wednesday. "Anytime you go out there and you get a first catch, that's a blessing to be able to even say that, 'I got my first catch in the NFL game,' so I think it did help the confidence.

"My body's been feeling better and been able to move a little better so it felt good."

After a middling training camp, Stowers spent the first six weeks of the season on Injured Reserve, and it was fair to wonder what exactly the Eagles had in this converted quarterback from Vanderbilt. He made his NFL debut in the loss to the Cowboys and had one target but no catches.

But anyone who watched till the end of the game Sunday night got to see the soft hands, athleticism and speed the Eagles like enough about Stowers to make him the 54th player and second tight end taken in this year's draft.

He actually almost scored on that final snap of the game before finally getting tackled at the 8-yard-line after the longest pass play of McKee's career.

"I caught the ball and it was a lot of space," he said. "And so anytime you catch the ball, you've got to go try to score. That's the mindset that any ball carrier should have. So that was my mindset. I was hoping I could drag them in there. They ended up getting me down, but it's all right."

What's really important is that Stowers is healthy after a lost summer. The Eagles didn't rush him back, and it paid off.

"I had hurt my hamstring and then I hurt my quad and then it was a span of like seven weeks where I just had to get my body back healthy from camp," he said. "I don't think it was really a matter of me being behind physically. It was just I had to get back healthy, get on the field and perform well."

Stowers became the Eagles' first 2nd-round pick to not play the first six weeks of a season since guard Bobbie Williams in 2001. Williams wound up playing 12 years in the NFL and starting 136 games, mainly for the Bengals.

"Sometimes injuries like that, you can't do anything about it," he said. "Take care of your body the best way you can. Get good sleep, eat right, drink, do all the stuff. But sometimes you just can't do anything about it. So if you sit there and you stay on it, kind of sulk about it, nothing's going to get any better. So I just attacked it every single day of getting it back right and be prepared for when I get back."

It's no coincidence that the Eagles put Stowers' locker next to Zach Ertz, whose 828 career catches are 5th-most in NFL history by a tight end.

Ertz is currently on the practice squad and out of elevations, but that doesn't mean he can't be a sounding board every day for the 23-year-old Stowers.

"He's been awesome," Stowers said. "Everybody in that room has been awesome. We got all vets in there and I really appreciate how much they've kind of poured into me.

"One of the first things Zach told me when he got here, he was like, 'Yo, if you have anything, any questions, anything,' let me know. Don't be scared to ask. Sometimes he'll even, when I'm sitting here being quiet, he'll encourage me to ask him a question. Like, 'Have you got something?'

"So, I appreciate that, man. He's helping me get better."

#2875. Brandon Aiyuk plans to meet with 49ers at team's business offices, not team facility

49ers tight end George Kittle was right, sort of. Receiver Brandon Aiyuk won’t “get in” the team facility on Friday, because he’s not trying to.

Per agent Drew Rosenhaus, Friday’s meeting will happen at the team’s business offices and not at the team’s practice facility.

That makes the session not a formal reporting-for-duty by Aiyuk, in the hopes of forcing the team’s hand. As explained earlier today, the NFL still hasn’t reinstated Aiyuk from the reserve/left squad list — even though Rosenhaus filed the request on October 27.

Instead, it’s a meeting presumably aimed at negotiating a resolution to the situation.

The 49ers pushed to reporters on Thursday night the idea that Aiyuk’s return to the NFL is tied to the $13 million the team believes he owes in previously paid signing bonus. That shouldn’t be part of the analysis as to whether he’s reinstated by the league, whether the 49ers put him on the roster again, and/or whether the 49ers cut him.

But they’re making it an issue, because they can tie his ability to play to his willingness to wire the disputed funds to the team.

So that’s what’s happening. The brakes already have been applied to what should have been an administrative and automatic process. The league and the 49ers are working together to help the team get money back from Aiyuk before Aiyuk is permitted to get back into the league.

Then there’s the fact that an active arrest warrant is pending for Aiyuk, arising from a speeding incident. The business meeting could potentially be interrupted by an attempt to enforce a specific item of police business.

#2874. Eagles making the switch to QB Joe Milton for Week 9 vs. Giants

Eagles making the switch to QB Joe Milton for Week 9 vs. Giants originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles are pushing one of the last buttons they have.

After Nick Sirianni on Monday afternoon claimed he was sticking with Tanner McKee as the their starting quarterback, the Eagles (2-6) are now reportedly making a switch to Joe Milton for Sunday's game against the Giants. This will be his first-career start.

ESPN first reported the news on Wednesday afternoon.

While Sirianni didn't indicate a change during his press conference less than two hours earlier on Wednesday, the head coach was asked about his new starting quarterback. He praised Milton, the recent addition from Dallas.

"I think he's worked really hard," Sirianni said. "Him and (Quarterbacks coach) Parks (Frazier) have spent a ton of time together, getting him caught up to speed. I know the developmental periods, those always help to be able to run our plays. But he works extremely hard. He is always in the building, doing the extra things. I like his progression."

The Eagles signed Milton to their 53-man roster off the Cowboys' practice squad back on Oct. 16. It took him just about a week to supplant rookie Cole Payton as the backup quarterback and he is now the starter just three weeks after joining the team.

Starting quarterback Jalen Hurts has not played since Oct. 11 against the Jaguars in Jacksonville. He is out for the rest of the season with blood clots in his lungs.

McKee, 26, has started the last three games and has struggled. McKee this season has completed 63.2% of his passes for 114 yards with 2 touchdowns and 2 interceptions. But he really struggled with pocket awareness in the loss to the Commanders on Sunday in Washington.

"Tanner's our guy," Sirianni said on Monday. "We're not making any changes there. Again, how do we find solutions? That could be within scheme, that could be within how our schedule is, practice plays. Everything's on the table for us in that aspect. But right now, this is where we are and we're trying to find solutions there. We're looking at everything as far as what we can do differently as coaches."

The hope is likely that Milton will be able to create more than McKee has been able to over the last few games.

Milton, 26, was drafted out of Tennessee by the Patriots in the sixth round of the 2024 draft. Milton (6-5, 236) has a big arm and a ton of talent but was seen as a developmental prospect when he got drafted.

Over his first two NFL seasons, Milton has played in five games without a start. He has completed 37 of 53 passes (69.8%) for 424 yards with 2 touchdowns and 2 interceptions.

The Eagles have mishandled their backup QB spot since training camp. They rotated Andy Dalton and McKee throughout the summer and gave Dalton the job. But after one throw and one interception against the Bears, they demoted Dalton and then eventually released him. So when Hurts was ruled out for the Panthers game, the Eagles turned to McKee. Now they're turning to a new QB who arrived less than three weeks ago.

#2872. ‘Proud of me?’ Valparaiso’s wild Purdue upset sparks touching TV moment

Valparaiso basketball's double overtime upset of No. 24 Purdue was cool enough. But it held added significance for Big Ten Network analyst Bruce Weber, despite Weber's nearly two decades as a Boilermakers assistant coach.

Weber, the former Illinois head coach, was on the call for BTN on Friday night as the Beacons shocked Purdue with a Justus McNair 3-pointer at the buzzer in the second overtime for the 83-80 win.

Coaching the Beacons was Roger Powell, who played two seasons under Weber at Illinois, winning the Big Ten title both years. Powell is in his fourth season as Valpo coach, and the Beacons are coming off their first winning season since 2019-20.

In the postgame interview, Weber and Powell shared a neat moment.

"To do it with my coach calling the game was really special. Having you at shootaround today meant a lot to me, and proud of me?" Powell asked Weber.

"I am proud of you. It was special. You weren't ready to be a head coach before, but you are tonight," Weber responded.

"I'm learning. I'm growing. But these guys are special. We've got a special bunch. A special group of guys that are tough. They never give up. That's a part of our program, our culture. We talk about grit and we showed a lot of grit today."

New-look Purdue is now 0-2 to start the season for the first time since 1976 and looks far off its usual level under longtime coach Matt Painter. Purdue opened the season with a 78-72 loss to Gonzaga in Las Vegas, and its loss to Valparaiso marks that program's first win over a Big Ten opponent since beating Penn State in 2004.

#2871. Player Grades: Lakers vs. Blazers

For so long in the LeBron James-Anthony Davis era, the Lakers were searching for 3-and-D wings to put around their stars.

One of the best parts of this current Lakers team is the dearth of wings. Not only does it allow them to have multiple of them on the floor throughout the game, but it also affords them options depending on who is playing well.

On Friday, for example, Matisse Thybulle, who has been a consistent contributor this season, struggled. The Lakers were able to turn to Jake LaRavia, then Ziaire Williams and even debuted Adou Thiero as well. It's such a luxury that this team hasn't had for years, allowing them to pick and choose the best groupings each night.

So, let's dive into the win. As always, grades are based on expectations for each player. A "B" grade represents the average performance for that player.

24 minutes, 2 points, 2 rebounds, 2 fouls, 3 steals, 2 turnovers, 0-1 FG, -8

Something about playing the Blazers messes with LaRavia's brain or something, because his two worst games of the year have come against them. Fortunately in this outing, he was able to mix in plenty of good moments to still be a positive contributor.

37 minutes, 16 points, 12 rebounds, 2 assists, 1 steal, 5 fouls, 1 turnover, 7-10 FG, 2-3 FT, +8

Another quietly impressive game from Kessler, who held his own against Donovan Clingan throughout the night. His funniest basket of the night came toward the end when the Blazers forgot about him in front of the rim on an inbound.

38 minutes, 25 points, 3 rebounds, 2 assists, 4 steals, 4 fouls, 2 turnovers, 8-22 FG, 1-8 3PT, 8-8 FT, -6

Austin was at the center of the Lakers' poor start, going 4-15 from the field in the first three quarters. But when he was needed, Reaves responded in the fourth, scoring 15 points on 4-7 shooting, including a huge steal and dunk late.

38 minutes, 53 points, 5 rebounds, 5 assists, 1 steal, 3 fouls, 3 turnovers, 17-33 FG, 5-15 3PT, 14-18 FT, +16

You know what kind of run Luka is on right now? The type where, from the moment he let go of that shot from the opposite free throw line, every Lakers fan immediately thought, "That looks good."

20 minutes, 2 points, 2 steals, 2 fouls, 1 turnover, 1-3 FG, 0-2 3PT, +19

Grimes still looks a bit of a mess on offense. It screams of overthinking and not finding a rhythm. Even as he's struggled, though, he's still been a positive on the defensive end.

But they could really use him finding a groove on offense.

20 minutes, 7 points, 3 rebounds, 1 assist, 1 steal, 1 block, 2-6 FG, 1-3 3PT, 2-2 FT, +19

We got a look at the Mamu-Kessler front court in the first half tonight, something the team probably hasn't been able to do as much as they wished. Mamu has had to be used as the backup five, not leaving many minutes for a jumbo-sized frontcourt.

19 minutes, 8 points, 3 rebounds, 1 assist, 2 steals, 1 foul, 1 turnover, 3-5 FG, 2-4 3PT, +8

Having a player like Ziaire available to come off the bench is such a luxury. His tip of the pass late in the fourth that Austin was then able to steal was a crucial play to help seal the win.

10 minutes, 1 assist, 5 fouls, 0-4 FG, 0-1 3PT, -10

I feel like Sexton has been afforded an appropriate amount of time for not getting a preseason. This is just really, really bad now. If you wanted to find a place for minutes for Cameron Carr, Sexton can't be far away from being taken out of the rotation.

While he only played six minutes, Thiero was fantastic. The Lakers were using him as a screener and lob threat and, unironically, it's a great use of him. He got a lob and found Ziaire for a dunk of his own in the first half.

His energy and activity level is always so high that he just makes an impact.

I'll give Redick credit for what I mentioned above in his use of Thiero, who replaced Jarred Vanderbilt in the rotation. The team, realistically, can't survive having two non-shooting wings on the floor and investing in Thiero is the right call.

I'll knock him for that second challenge, though. But overall, it was a solid night from him.

#2870. Patrick Mahomes is closing in on the record for most TD passes in a QB's first 10 years

Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes is three touchdown passes away from the NFL record for the most ever in a player's first 10 seasons.

Mahomes, who is in his 10th NFL season, has 325 career touchdown passes, counting the playoffs. Only Peyton Manning, who had 327 touchdown passes in his first 10 years in the league, has had more.

So if Mahomes throws for two touchdowns on Sunday against the Jets, he'll tie Manning's record. With three touchdowns, Mahomes will have the record all to himself.

As a rookie, Mahomes played in only one game and didn't throw any touchdowns. In the eight and a half seasons since then, Mahomes has 279 touchdown passes in the regular season, and another 46 in the playoffs.

Once Mahomes breaks the record, he's likely to own it for a long time. The only active quarterback who's closing in is Josh Allen, who is currently in his eighth NFL season and has 260 career touchdown passes. Allen has a chance of throwing for 68 touchdown passes in the next year and a half and moving ahead of Manning, but he's highly unlikely to finish next season ahead of whatever Mahomes' total is by the end of this season. This is a record Mahomes will own for a long time.

#2869. Kyle Shanahan on Brandon Aiyuk's meeting with 49ers: I couldn't care less

49ers team officials will meet with wide receiver Brandon Aiyuk and his agent, Drew Rosenhaus, at the team’s business offices in an hour. Coach Kyle Shanahan said it has nothing to do with him.

“No clue. Your guess is as good as mine,” Shanahan told reporters Friday when asked what he expected out of the meeting. “I [couldn’t] care less.”

Aiyuk last played in a game on Oct. 20, 2024, when he tore the ACL and MCL in his right knee. The relationship between the team and the player fell apart after that, and the 49ers voided the guaranteed money due to his unwillingness to rehab at their facility.

The 49ers placed him on the reserve/left squad list on Dec. 13, 2025. Rosenhaus filed a reinstatement request on Oct. 27, but the NFL still hasn’t reinstated him.

The only certainty is Aiyuk will never play for the 49ers again.

“It’s been over for our football team for a long time,” Shanahan said. “So, I don’t hear much except questions about him today, but not too big of a deal for me to answer.

“I look at this as zero to do with our football team. Anything that has zero to do with our football team, especially during the season, I will give zero thoughts to.”

#2866. What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique

What makes this 76ers team stacked with superstars so unique originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

They're a team of superstars that don't play superstar basketball.

That's the beauty of this 76ers team. They play a brand of unselfish team-first basketball you rarely see from a team loaded with stars.

LeBron James is one of the two or three greatest players in NBA history, a four-time MVP, a four-time NBA champion, a 1st-ballot Hall of Famer.

Joel Embiid is a seven-time all-star, a former MVP, an Olympic gold medalist and a likely Hall of Famer.

Jaylen Brown is a five-time all-star, an NBA champion, a career 20 points-per-game scorer.

Tyrese Maxey is a two-time all-star, one of only two NBA guards averaging 27.0 points per game over the last four years.

Four certified megastars on one team. And somehow they've figured out how to leave their egos at the front door and play smart, tough, disciplined team basketball, the style of ball you just never see on these superstar-laden teams.

On offense, they patiently work the ball around, always looking for the open man or a positive matchup. You've got this bunch of big-name, high-priced All-Stars who are just as happy to pass the ball as shoot it. That never happens.

The 76ers are sixth in the NBA in assists, and they're one of only four teams with twice as many assists as turnovers. Smart, unselfish, patient, disciplined.

When an all-timer like LeBron averages 7.3 assists per game that speaks volumes. LeBron is fifth in the NBA in assists per game among forwards, which is wild. You can see the joy on his face when one of his passes leads to a basket. When one of the greatest players in history has that mentality, it rubs off on everyone around him.

Embiid hasn't really got going yet on offense and still takes too many 3's, but he's also embraced the team-first mentality, and his 4.8 assists per game are more than one full assist above his career average and 4th-most in the NBA among centers or forward-centers. And still one of the most dominating defensive players in the league.

Brown is averaging 27.0 points, which you expect, but he's also averaging 4.3 assists per game, significantly above his career average of 3.8 coming into the season. He's fit in so well so fast you forget its his first season here.

And what can you even say about Maxey? Just a beast. He's averaging nearly 30 points per game but never wastes a possession, always makes the right decision with the ball in his hands and is still finding time to average 4.3 assists per game.

With James, Embiid, Brown and Maxey, the 76ers are the only NBA team with four guys averaging at least 16 points and 4 assists per game. Only the Pacers, Magic and Raptors have three.

But where we're really seeing this group playing out of their minds is on the defensive end, where this bunch of all-stars, including two newcomers, has been astounding.

The 76ers have the 2nd-best defensive efficiency rating in the league, behind only the Pistons, who they play Wednesday night, and they're allowing the 2nd-fewest points in the league, also behind Detroit. Last year, they allowed 123 points per game through eight games. This year they're at 111.

They're holding teams to 43 percent shooting from the field, 3rd-best in the league and their lowest since 2011-12. Their 9.3 steals per game are 6th-most.

All five starters are among the top 25 players in the league in defensive rating – James is fourth, Embiid 11th, Edgecombe 15th, Maxey 22nd and Brown 23rd. How is that even possible?

It's rare to see superstar-caliber guys play as hard on the defensive end as the offensive end. Everybody on this team embraces it. They hustle, they work, they communicate and most importantly they commit to it.

The biggest concern when the 76ers built this dream team was that it would be a team of individuals out for themselves, but the reality has been the opposite.

In a very short period of time, they've learned how to play together. How to play for each other.

And we haven't even mentioned V.J. Edgecomb, who at 21 years old has figured out how to assimilate into this loaded lineup, never force anything, wait for his opportunities and make huge shots when he gets his turn. Edgecombe is at 12.7 points and 3.0 assists per game, and his numbers are understandably down a bit from last year, but if that bothers him it sure doesn't show.

And the role players – Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons, Kentavious Caldwell-Pope, Jabari Walker – have each adopted the same ego-free style of basketball, supporting the starters however they need to.

Nick Nurse deserves a ton of credit for getting this bunch to play this brand of hoops. It's not easy to get any NBA player to put team goals ahead of individual goals. But he's got Hall of Famers and all-stars doing it and having a blast along the way. They all seem to love playing basketball the right way and they seem to love playing together.

The 76ers are 7-1 and looking like a real threat to win the East, and they're doing it by playing the game the right way.

By committing to each other and not themselves.

#2859. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2858. Myles Garrett: Rams' opponents will have to pick their poison, me or Aaron Donald

Rams defensive end Myles Garret and defensive tackle Aaron Donald will finally be on the field together Sunday in Washington, and Garrett says that's going to be bad news for opposing offenses for the rest of the season.

Donald did not play in Week 1 because he was still ramping up for the season after returning from his two-year retirement, and Garret hasn't played since Week 1 because of knee surgery. Garrett said the two of them together, two multiple-time Defensive Player of the Year award winners side by side, will present a unique challenge for opposing offenses.

"I think offenses really have to pick their poison. Who's going to be freed up this play? I think either of us are pretty good at winning one-on-one, so we'll have some fun," Garrett said. "Going into this season I never thought I'd have the chance, and as you all know he kept us in suspense for quite a while, so it was very exciting, and I'm looking forward to getting on the field and playing some real snaps with him."

Garrett said his poor performance in Week 1 against the 49ers showed him he needed knee surgery, something he would have preferred to avoid.

"I was frustrated after the game. I knew that wasn't me, and I was being hampered by something," Garrett said. "I didn't want to miss any time, but I had to be realistic about where I was physically, and I had to make the best choice for me and the team."

But now Garrett is back, and he said he and Donald playing together can give the Rams an "elite defense" for the rest of the season.

#2855. Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch

Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers and Cavaliers have already played one game that looked like two.

Cleveland led by 29 points during the Sixers' home opener on Oct. 22 and carried a 69-45 advantage into halftime. Philadelphia responded by winning the second half, 69-37, and completing a 114-106 comeback.

It was the Sixers' largest comeback since the NBA began maintaining detailed play-by-play data in 1997-98. Only nine other teams during that span had overcome a deficit of at least 29 points.

Fifteen days later, the teams will meet again Friday night at Rocket Arena. This time, the matchup counts toward the NBA Cup standings.

The 8-1 Sixers have won five consecutive games and opened group play with a 98-93 victory over the Knicks. The 4-4 Cavs have dropped two straight and will make their Cup debut.

Here are five numbers that help frame the rematch.

Philadelphia's comeback required more than a hot shooting stretch.

The Sixers committed 15 turnovers in the first half, allowing Cleveland to build a 22-6 advantage in points off turnovers. Their pressure then changed the game after halftime.

The Cavs committed 21 turnovers by the end of the night. What had been a 16-point Cleveland advantage in points off turnovers finished even at 22 apiece.

"They upped their pressure and physicality," Cavs head coach Kenny Atkinson said after the game. "It was pretty clear. We did not handle it well."

The Sixers scored 64 of their 69 second-half points through their starting lineup. Tyrese Maxey finished with 28 points, and Philadelphia turned a game that appeared finished into a franchise-record comeback.

The first half of the opening meeting now looks like an exception.

The Sixers have committed 12.9 turnovers per 100 possessions this season, the NBA's second-lowest rate. They are also third in turnover differential, averaging 3.4 fewer giveaways than their opponents.

That is notable for a team that entered the season intending to play faster. Philadelphia has pushed the ball without routinely surrendering possessions.

LeBron James' presence as the primary facilitator has helped. So has Maxey's willingness to move the ball when defenses trap him. Joel Embiid has averaged 5.2 assists, third among qualified centers, and the Sixers have increasingly trusted VJ Edgecombe to make decisions late in games.

Cleveland's defense has not forced many mistakes. The Cavs enter Friday near the bottom of the league in opponent turnover rate.

The first meeting swung when the Sixers stopped giving away possessions and forced Cleveland into mistakes of its own.

The Sixers have allowed 105.1 points per 100 possessions, the NBA's second-best defensive rating. Cleveland enters with the league's ninth-ranked offense.

The Cavs are the only top-10 offense the Sixers have faced through their first nine games.

Philadelphia has held opponents to a 50 percent effective field-goal percentage and 31.8 percent shooting from three-point range. Its defensive shot profile has been encouraging, too. Only 46 percent of opposing three-point attempts have been classified as wide open, the fifth-lowest share in the league, and opponents have taken just 22 percent of their threes from the corners.

There is reason for caution. Based on the quality of shots Philadelphia has allowed, only Washington's opponents have underperformed their expected effective field-goal percentage by a wider margin. The Sixers have defended well, but opponents are unlikely to remain this cold indefinitely.

Cleveland, with Donovan Mitchell, James Harden and Evan Mobley, can test whether those numbers hold against high-level individual shot creation.

The Cavs have attempted exactly 42 three-pointers per game and made 37.2 percent of them.

Cleveland went 7 for 29 from beyond the arc in Philadelphia. Mitchell, Harden and De'Andre Hunter combined to shoot 3 for 17. The Cavs scored 60 points in the paint and still reached 106, but their 24.1 percent performance from three prevented them from stopping the Sixers' comeback.

Friday's game could look considerably different if Cleveland generates its normal volume.

The Sixers have controlled the three-point line well so far, but Mitchell is coming off a 27-point performance against Oklahoma City and scored 32 in the first meeting with Philadelphia. Harden added 16 points that night, although his six turnovers became costly as the Sixers increased their pressure.

Philadelphia has been outrebounded by 5.1 per game. The Cavs rank sixth with 47.5 rebounds per contest.

The Sixers have already shown they can survive a major deficit on the glass. They beat Milwaukee by 11 despite getting outrebounded by 22. That is not a formula they will want to test regularly against Cleveland's size.

Mobley had 18 points and eight rebounds in the first meeting. Jarrett Allen added 14 points and seven boards, helping Cleveland win the rebounding battle, 46-44.

Whether Allen plays Friday remains uncertain.

Cleveland listed Allen as questionable because of a right knee injury after he left Wednesday's loss to Oklahoma City in the first quarter. Allen collided knee-to-knee with Thunder forward Chet Holmgren and did not return.

The Cavs also listed Peyton Watson as questionable with right hamstring soreness and Tyrese Proctor as questionable with an ankle injury. Proctor left Wednesday's game during the fourth quarter. Jaylon Tyson is out with a right ankle sprain.

Allen's availability could significantly alter the matchup on the glass. If he sits, Thomas Bryant would likely handle more of the center minutes behind Mobley. If he plays, Cleveland will have the same starting frontcourt that outrebounded the Sixers during the first meeting.

Atkinson has emphasized the possession battle during Cleveland's two-game slide.

"You've got to get more shots than the other team," he said this week. "Rebounding is a big part of it."

That also carries added importance in the NBA Cup, where point differential is the second tiebreaker after head-to-head results.

Philadelphia enters Friday at 1-0 with a plus-five differential in East Group B. Miami is also 1-0 at plus-20, while Cleveland is playing its first group game.

The Sixers already know they can erase 29 points against the Cavs. A cleaner start would be far more useful than proving it twice.

#2850. How Joe Milton could unlock Saquon Barkley's running game for Eagles

The Philadelphia Eagles are turning to Joe Milton III to revive their struggling offense, but the quarterback change could also significantly boost Saquon Barkley's running game. Milton will replace Tanner McKee for Sunday's Week 9 matchup against the New York Giants, bringing mobility and a different physical skill set to an offense that has struggled to sustain drives. Philadelphia enters the game at 2-6, averaging just 17.1 points and 265.3 total yards per contest.

Barkley has rushed for 274 yards and two touchdowns on 56 carries this season, averaging 4.9 yards per attempt. However, injuries and inconsistent offensive line play have limited his opportunities. The former NFL Offensive Player of the Year has averaged just 9.3 carries per game, a significant departure from his workload during Philadelphia's championship season in 2024.

Milton's ability to threaten defenses with his legs could create additional opportunities for Barkley.

McKee operated primarily from the pocket, allowing opposing defenses to concentrate on Barkley without consistently accounting for the quarterback as a rushing threat.

The 6-foot-5 quarterback has rushed for 66 yards and a touchdown on 15 carries during his limited NFL career. He recorded 50 yards on five attempts with Dallas last season, demonstrating his ability to generate explosive gains when escaping pressure. Offensive coordinator Sean Mannion can incorporate read-option concepts, designed quarterback movement, and bootlegs that force backside defenders to account for Milton.

That added responsibility could keep defenders from aggressively pursuing Barkley and create better blocking angles for Philadelphia's offensive line. The Eagles don't need Milton to become a primary rushing option. Simply forcing defenses to respect his mobility could change how opponents defend the running game.

New York enters Sunday allowing 128.1 rushing yards per game and 4.8 yards per carry, ranking among the league's least effective run defenses.

Those numbers give Philadelphia an opportunity to play a physical game and avoid putting the entire offensive burden on Milton in his first NFL start. Barkley also has an impressive history against his former team. In three meetings against New York since joining Philadelphia, he has rushed for 384 yards and two touchdowns. His status remains important after dealing with hamstring and back injuries. Barkley was limited during Friday's practice but carries no injury designation into Sunday's matchup.

Philadelphia must also account for the loss of Lane Johnson and changes along its offensive line. Milton's mobility can create opportunities, but it won't eliminate the need for consistent blocking and better execution.

The Eagles have struggled to establish an offensive identity during their six-game losing streak. A productive rushing attack could help stabilize the offense, shorten third-down situations, and create more favorable passing opportunities.

Milton provides another dimension, but Barkley remains Philadelphia's most accomplished offensive weapon. Finding ways to get him consistent touches will be essential if the Eagles hope to end their losing streak before entering their Week 10 bye.

#2845. Giants news: Cam Skattebo doubtful for Sunday, Eagles matchups, more

Good morning, New York Giants fans! Here are the stories you need to as the first place Giants get ready to face off tomorrow against the Philadelphia Eagles.

Based on the analytics and percentages, a Giants win would put them in prime position for a playoff spot. A loss would drop them back deep into the pack. A Giants win would give them a 68 percent chance to win the NFC East and a 71 percent shot at making the playoffs.

A loss would lessen their division odds to 41 percent and their postseason odds to 43 percent. So, this is kind of a "must-win" for Big Blue (4-2-1) as their schedule gets dicey in the second half of the season.

Q: Getting into November football, is there a new skill that your team has to unlock?

Harbaugh: Everything is new. Every step of the way is going to be new this year. I think it's kind of what first years are a lot of times. Like we told them, this is November football. You've earned the right to play meaningful games in November. What does that mean? What are you playing for? I asked them, "What do they say about December football? Have you heard the old saying?" And someone got it. [Fullback Patrick] Ricard got it. "They remember what you do in December." That's right. I said that's what they remember. I said they won't really remember November too much. But without November you can't get to the point where you're playing games that are going to be memorable. That's what we're playing for right now. We're playing for the opportunity to play in memorable games, and that's why November is so important and we've earned the right to be to be playing in these games. So, it's just another notch up of value and then I just talked to them a little bit about as the season goes along, it's still one game but each game becomes worth more. It's one game, that's what this is about, but each game becomes even more valuable because there's more payoff. Later, that's why they remember what you do in December. There's more payoff. So, that's kind of cool.

"Before Lane Johnson had a name, before Jason Kelce had a name, before Cam Jurgens had a name, they had to go earn it," defensive tackle Shelby Harris said. "You can't put down anybody just because they haven't established themselves in the league. The idea is you don't want them to establish themselves against you. They're still physical, they're humungous size-wise across the board, so it's just go out there and don't let them be the determining factor of the game."

"I'm expecting [Barkley] to be healthier than he has been so far," Harris said. "They've done a good job trying to manage him on his way back from injury. If he's going to play, I expect to get the best out of him — full-on star Saquon Barkley. And we'll have to stop him in order to be successful."

What we're hearing on the Giants: The Giants believe the bye week should've helped QB J.J. McCarthy. He's now fully caught up after being traded to New York in late September. McCarthy, who is making his second start for the Giants, expects Eagles DC Vic Fangio to make him "earn it." It will be the latest test of whether the young QB can succeed while facing simulated pressures and getting through his progressions.

"I mean, we've lost four times [in a row]," Henry said. "It's frustrating. I don't have the magical answer. We've got to go back to work." When asked about a potential trade, Henry said, "I have not mentioned me going anywhere else. I'm saying let's go back to work."

Henry turns 33 in January. He is under contract through next season. Harbaugh doesn't want a one-year rental, and Henry technically would not be that. His salary next year is $11 million — all of which is non-guaranteed. He has a $1 million roster bonus due in March. So the Ravens (or Giants) have a team option on him for 2027.

New York Giants WR Malik Nabers vs. Philadelphia Eagles CB Quinyon Mitchell. The Giants seek to widen their NFC East lead when they face the Eagles, losers of six in a row. The Giants have had heartbreakers at Lincoln Financial Field, but a loss Sunday would be the worst of the streak because they're the better team with a far more promising outlook. The Giants are 3.5-point favorites Sunday and winning in Philadelphia would be another step in Harbaugh's process of rebuilding the franchise.

Harbaugh's track record suggests the Giants are in a strong position to end the streak. His Ravens teams went 15-3 after the bye during his 18 seasons in Baltimore. "We've got everything to gain and we know how big this game is," Harbaugh said. "We know who we're going against. We'll be ready."

#2844. Hansi Flick talks Raphinha, Eric, Rodri, Bernal, De Jong, Julian Alvarez ahead of Atletico Madrid vs Barcelona

Barcelona will be eager to strengthen their grip at the top of the La Liga standings when they take on Atletico Madrid tomorrow at the Riyadh Air Metropolitano.

The Blaugrana are currently only two points clear of the Rojiblancos and a defeat could see them lose the top position heading into the international break – something that Hansi Flick & co. will be eager to avoid.

Ahead of the trip to the Spanish capital, Flick spoke to the media in his pre-match press conference, providing an update on the injured players among other things.

Confirming that Barcelona defensive duo Eric Garcia and Andreas Christensen are available for the game, the manager said:

"Eric could start and both will be in the squad for tomorrow. I think Eric can start, but we'll see."

Flick then spoke about the injury suffered by Rodri in the win over Aston Villa and whether that could have been avoided by more careful management of minutes, saying:

"You can always do things better, but in this case I spoke with the player and we managed his minutes. He's an experienced player and we talked.

"We agreed on these things. I can't do more. When you see the results, you think you could have done things differently, but that's in the past now."

As for Frenkie de Jong starting as the replacement for the Spanish international in the lineup, Flick said:

"I haven't decided if he'll start. It's good to think about it tonight. We know his quality, but we also know Bernal's. He did very well in the last match and I'm really enjoying him."

De Jong is pushing to start in Rodri's absence. (Photo by David Ramos/Getty Images)

Discussing the qualities that De Jong and Marc Bernal bring to the table, the manager elaborated:

"Frenkie can play in several positions, not just as a '6'. They're similar. Frenkie has more experience, but both are confident and decisive on the ball.

"Bernal has done very well and knows where he should be. I'm very happy with him, but also that Frenkie is back. We have to find the right starting eleven; we have time to make the decision."

Providing an update on Jules Kounde's recovery, he added: "We were hoping he'd recover faster, but we have to take it easy. We need to keep an eye on his progress. Hopefully, he'll be available after the international break."

Barcelona faced Atletico Madrid six times last season across La Liga, Copa del Rey, and the UEFA Champions League.

Although the Blaugrana came out with a better head-to-head record, winning four of those games, they were eliminated by the Spanish capital club in the Copa del Rey semi-final and the Champions League quarter-final.

Flick, though, insisted that the past results were inconsequential heading into tomorrow's clash, saying:

"That's the past. It's not relevant to me. What's important is what happens tomorrow, when we play against a fantastic team. We want to win to be top of the table during the international break. We've worked hard and we want to show our strengths."

On the challenge of facing Atletico Madrid at the Metropolitano, the Barcelona head coach added:

"Every match is important. In LaLiga, we have three or four excellent teams that are close together in the standings. It's important to win at the Metropolitano; that's our objective.

"We'll do everything we can to win. There's still a long way to go in the season. We'll go there to win, but they're a fantastic team with a superb coach. It will be a demanding match."

Flick then discussed the importance of club captain Raphinha who signed a new contract with Barcelona until 2030 earlier this week.

"There are no words for Raphinha. It's the best situation for the club and for Raphinha. I'm grateful for his renewal. He's a fantastic player, our captain, and very important for our club," he said.

Raphinha signed a new contract earlier this week. (Picture credit: X/@FCBarcelona)

The manager also had words of praise for summer signing Anthony Gordon, as he said:

"He gives us a lot on the ball. He maintains possession and doesn't make mistakes. I think he could be a little more selfish in the final third. He's done very well in the last few matches and has raised his level. I'm happy with what he's doing."

Flick also highlighted the contribution of youngster Brian Farinas, who has slotted in seamlessly at right-back in the last two games, saying:

"He's never played there (right-back) but physically and technically he's a great player. Thanks to La Masia, for how they educate the players and the attitude they have.

"He has Lamine on his flank and he does very well. He has the level to play this match. Against Aston Villa he played at a top level. He could play this Saturday."

When asked for his thoughts on Atletico Madrid forward Julian Alvarez, whom Barcelona chased all summer long but were unable to sign, Flick remarked:

"Julian is a fantastic player, but he plays for Atletico and I have to focus on my team."

On being probed further about the pursuit of the Argentine international, the Barcelona coach said:

"I don't talk about players who are on other teams. I focus on my players and I'm very happy with the team's situation. We have many options in attack. There's nothing more to say."

#2843. Brandon Aiyuk: I’m back to 100 percent and looking to get reinstated

Brandon Aiyuk will not play for the 49ers again, but his future is in question.

The wide receiver arrived at the team’s business offices in a van with his agent Friday afternoon to meet with the 49ers and presumably negotiate a resolution.

He’s healthy. He’s ready to get back to work. Don’t really have any comment about where he’s going to play. We’ll keep that between us and the Niners. But obviously we’re here with all the right intentions.”

Aiyuk last played in a game on Oct. 20, 2024, when he tore the ACL and MCL in his right knee. The relationship between the team and the player fell apart after that, and the 49ers voided the guaranteed money due to his unwillingness to rehab at their facility.

The 49ers placed him on the reserve/left squad list on Dec. 13, 2025. Rosenhaus filed a reinstatement request on Oct. 27, but the NFL still hasn’t reinstated him.

“Nice to see you all,” Aiyuk said. “I’m feeling great, feeling healthy. It was a long recovery process, a long rehab process. But I’m back to 100 percent and looking to get reinstated into the National Football League.”

49ers coach Kyle Shanahan said earlier in the day he "[couldn’t] care less” how the meeting went since “it’s been over for our football team for a long time.”

#2841. 3 overlooked Joe Burrow throws that helped Bengals defeat Titans

Seven players caught at least one pass from Joe Burrow in Sunday's 43-36 overtime win for the Bengals over the Tennessee Titans. Samaje Perine and Jack Endries were both only targeted one time.

Perine hauled in a nine-yard reception on the second play of overtime, and Endries hauled in a one-yard touchdown that gave the Bengals a 14-13 lead just before halftime.

Coming into this season, there was a lot of conversation about the Bengals' wide receiver depth behind Ja'Marr Chase and Tee Higgins. Sunday, though, showed that depth is good heading into mid-November. Mike Gesicki has a touchdown in five straight games and six out of seven overall this season, and Dohnte Meyers has gone from a great training camp story to an integral part of the Bengals' receiver room and special teams.

Burrow has trust in a lot of receivers right now. Several players caught passes from Burrow in the fourth quarter and overtime Sunday in got-to-have-it situations.

On a day where Burrow was historically good, three throws are getting overlooked, but they stand out for the growing trust he has in the Bengals deep group of pass catchers and also for how ridiculously accurate and prolific Joe Burrow is at throwing the football.

Tensions were high in Paycor Stadium going into this play. The Bengals had just turned the ball over on their previous offensive possession, their fourth of the day, and Tennessee cashed in with a touchdown to take the lead. After a Chase Brown run on 2nd&4 gained just one yard, there were boo-birds coming down from the crowd. Then on 3rd&3, Burrow coolly found Mike Gesicki on the right side of the field for 18 yards and a badly needed first down.

It was just Gesicki's second catch of the game, but it would be the first of three receptions for him in the fourth quarter that accumulated 48 yards. Four plays after his third-down reception, Gesicki hauled in a 25-yard touchdown for his sixth score of the season and to give the Bengals a 29-28 lead. Gesicki is now tied for the most touchdowns he's ever scored in a season, and that was back in 2020, when he caught six touchdowns in his third season with the Dolphins.

This season, Gesicki has 17 receptions for 241 yards and six touchdowns, averaging 14.2 yards per reception.

Fourteen of Gesicki's 17 receptions are for first downs or touchdowns. He set the tone for this season in Week 1, catching five passes for 78 yards and a touchdown, including three catches for 52 yards on the Bengals' final first-half drive that led to a field goal. Now, Gesicki has a touchdown in five straight games, the longest streak for a tight end in Bengals' franchise history.

Don't let Gesicki's emergence and Tee's career-year overshadow the incredible connection Burrow and Chase have. They've been on the same page going all the way back to their first season at LSU in 2018. This play on Sunday shows the timing Burrow and Chase have on a difficult throw as Burrow is getting hit from a defensive stunt.

First of all, this play was set up by a 10-yard reception by Chase Brown on 2nd&20, which the Bengals were in after Burrow was sacked on first down. So getting back into third-and-manageable is impressive in and of itself.

Then on 3rd&10, the Titans ran a stunt with defensive end Jacob Martin looping around the left side of the Bengals' offensive line. It gave him a free rush at Burrow, but he didn't sack him. While he hit him hard, Burrow got rid of the ball just in time and layered a throw around multiple Titans' defenders to Chase on the far side of the field.

It gained 23 yards, and it set up Gesicki's 25-yard touchdown on the next play.

Chase had 11 receptions for 141 yards and a touchdown on Sunday, his first game with 100+ receiving yards and his first game with 10+ receptions. Even though Chase "only" has 492 receiving yards through seven games, he still has six touchdowns on the season, touchdowns in three straight games and five of his last six.

And if Chase has 10 receptions on Sunday, he'll tie Michael Thomas for the most games with 10+ receptions by a player in his first six seasons all-time with 18.

For what it's worth, Chase is still on pace for 148 targets, 104 receptions, 1,195 receiving yards, and 15 touchdowns this season. Even if that's a "down" year, it'd still be a great year. But don't worry; Chase had eight receptions for 130 yards and two touchdowns the last time the Bengals played the Falcons… and he didn't even play in the second half of that game.

This is my favorite play of the game. Tennessee just scored what could have been a back-breaking touchdown to take a 36-29 lead. I don't know about you, but I didn't know what the Bengals were going to do on their ensuing drive. It felt to me that it could go either way. But after this play, I was confident the Bengals were going to score and tie the game.

Burrow was in trouble on this play, but he bounced out of the pocket and started rolling to his left. It wasn't Chase or Higgins who caught the pass, though. It was Dohnte Meyers. The standout in training camp and the preseason, and who recently starred in the CFL, came across the field and made a diving 18-yard reception to move the chains and get the offense rolling.

In that big of a spot, with Burrow escaping the pocket, it was Meyers making an incredible catch to gain the Bengals' first down on the drive that ultimately tied the game late in the fourth quarter.

Five plays later, Tee Higgins caught his second touchdown to tie the game at 36-36.

Through seven games, Meyers has 16 receptions for 175 yards. Ten of those catches are for first downs, and he also has one rush for 10 yards that gained a first down against Tampa Bay. On special teams, Meyers has 11 punt returns for 103 yards and a 9.4 average, and he has 25 kick returns for 660 yards and a 26.4 average. Meyers had three receptions for 34 yards on Sunday, including two receptions in the fourth quarter for 27 yards.

Joe Burrow is averaging 302.1 passing yards per game. Ja'Marr Chase and Tee Higgins both lead the Bengals with 43 receptions. But other players are also stepping up, as evidenced by their performances on Sunday and through seven games this season. The Bengals not only have the best wide receiver duo in the NFL, but they also have one of the NFL's overlooked but deepest groups of pass catchers.

That may be what lifts Joe Burrow to the pole position of the MVP race and ultimately wins him his first MVP award.
